# Gun VS Gun

A fast-paced 1v1 / 2v2 arena prototype with two main modes — **Duel** (single stock) and **Trio** (three-unit rosters). Auto-aim — no manual targeting. The fight is about resource management: when to sprint, when to dodge, when to break line of sight, when to fire.

## Modes

Two main modes, each playable **1v1 or 2v2**, offline and online:

- **Duel** — classic single stock: one unit per fighter; a team loses when all its fighters are down.
- **Trio** — three-unit stock: every slot (human or bot) fields an **ordered roster of three units**, repeats allowed. When a unit dies, the slot's next unit respawns at its original spawn point with the standard 3 s spawn immunity; the killer keeps position / HP / boost — no kill reward. A team loses when every roster on its side is spent. Each fighter's remaining units show as a row of small weapon renders under their side's HP bars (one line per team member; Duel shows its single unit the same way), and a small golden glow-bar marks each Trio line's currently fielded weapon.
- **Spectating (2v2, both modes)**: if you're out for good while your ally fights on, the camera follows the ally with your own-unit visual kit (rear art + through-wall X-ray); the lock reticle stays up and mirrors the ally's actual target (the TARGET button goes inert), and the boost bar and ammo readout follow the ally too (2026-09-29 — they used to stay frozen on your dead unit's numbers).

Orthogonal to all of the above, every player also picks a **view mode** — **Shooter** (the chase camera you pilot directly; named "Classic" until 2026-10-10) or **Command** (a commander's diorama view where your unit fights on its own bot brain and you give it orders). See the **Command mode** section below.

### Offline
- **1v1**: you vs one enemy bot.
- **2v2**: you + an ally bot vs two enemy bots. Friendly fire is off between teammates.
- **Trio picks**: you select your three units in order, then each bot's three — same selection grid, titles count up (1/3 → 3/3).
- Optional "Dummy" mode on the map-select screen — zeroes out damage from every bot (enemies and your ally), so you can practice movement and observe bot behaviour without dying.
- Optional "Spectator" mode beside it — a bot takes over your unit and you watch the match: **TARGET** cycles the camera across every unit on the field (both teams), the HUD (HP / boost / ammo) follows whoever you're watching, the edge arrows stay viewer-relative, and the end banner reads **TEAM 1 WINS / TEAM 2 WINS**. Stacks with Dummy: the bot driving your slot still deals damage exactly as you would, and every other bot's shots are blanks (2026-10-03 — before that the combination zeroed everyone for a no-deaths exhibition).
- The main menu also carries a **Guide** button (a popup summarising controls, mechanics and the command-mode gestures) and a **Server Boot-up** button that opens the online debug panel.

### Online
- **Mode selection**: the host picks **Duel or Trio**, then **1v1 or 2v2**; joiners inherit the lobby's modes.
- **1v1**: the host presses **Start Match** when ready. The opponent slot holds a bot (default **Saori / Unit 1**) until a second human queues in and takes it — start early to play the bot, or wait for a player.
- **2v2**: the host presses **Start Match** when ready; empty player slots fill with bots (2v2 rooms open as Saori / Hoshino / Aru / Atsuko by slot; Trio bots field three copies of that unit unless the host picks otherwise). Up to four humans can play (any split between teams); bots fill any remaining slots.
- **Bot unit selection**: in the lobby, the host can tap any bot slot to pick which unit that bot plays — in Trio, its three units in order (default: three copies of the slot's usual unit). A human joining the slot always overrides the bot.
- **Trio queue room**: every slot's roster is shown in three lines and updates live as picks land.
- **View mode**: each player picks Shooter or Command in the queue room; the pick is final once the match starts and is never shown to the other team (teammates see it as a gold **[CMD]** tag on the roster line). Details in the Command mode section.
- **Slot-colored corner HUD** (online, 2026-08-22): the corner HP bars follow the **server slot** — left column team A (p1 top / p3 bottom), right column team B (p2 top / p4 bottom), each bar tinted its slot color (p1 blue / p2 pink / p3 green / p4 orange), the same picture for every viewer and spectator (1v1: p1 left, p2 right); your own bar wears a thin **white rim**. The command diorama's markers, cards, rings and lock triangles use the same slot colors online (offline keeps the role palette).
- **Host migration**: if the host leaves in the lobby or at the end menu, the longest-waiting player is promoted to host (their unit picks carry over; the lobby keeps its Duel / Trio and 1v1 / 2v2 settings and the new host re-chooses the map).
- Multi-lobby — when an existing lobby is full or running, new joiners spawn their own lobby and become host.
- Team swap in 2v2: any non-host player can `Join` an empty slot to switch teams (e.g. two humans want to co-op on one side against two bots).

### Unit profile card

The first tap on a unit card opens its profile beside the grid (tap the same card again to confirm, anywhere else to cancel): the character art on the left and the **weapon panel** on the right — the gun's real-world name, its render, and since 2026-09-22 a **3×2 stat grid** under the render (owner pick "C" from three in-game samples: a third plate beside the panel and a label/value row list were the other two; the demo line carries the same stats as a row list). The six cells read live from the unit data:

- **Mag**, **RPM** and **Reload** (seconds) as plain numbers.
- **Dmg** shows every tier where a gun has them: Aru's PSG1 `50/35/20` (far / mid / near), Kei's Railgun `30/20` (quick / charged), the shotguns per pellet `3 ×8`.
- **Movement** is the walk-speed tier word (walk speeds retuned 2026-09-24): **Fast** (walk 16 — Atsuko, Marina, Yuuka, Hoshino, Haruka, Rabu), **Medium** (12 — Saori, Asuna, Subaru, Fubuki, Aris, Aru, Kei), **Slow** (Koyuki and Kurumi at 10, Hina at 8).
- The **Spread picture** is a seeded mini-simulation of a **walking spray at a common 60-unit reference** (the shotguns at their own 40): the ring is a standing target's half-width at that distance, the orange dots are up to 30 rounds — magazine-limited — each leaving on the cone the sim gives that round (base + bloom, per-round bloom up to the cap, and the between-round recovery of the no-delay marksman rifles), fading with round order so the first rounds read brightest. The spray stops at the first round whose cone no longer fits the frame (2026-09-22, owner pick from samples — pinning spilled rounds to the frame's edge had piled Fubuki's 27 out-of-frame rounds into a rectangle), so nothing is drawn outside the frame: Saori's whole 30-round spray stays inside the ring, Koyuki's and Hina's pictures end a few rounds short of the magazine with their late rounds at the frame's edge, and Fubuki's and Aris's show their first three quick rounds — one on the ring's centre, two at its edge — because the fourth already flies wider than the frame; the shotguns show their 8-pellet pattern (the Beretta 1301 with its 1.4× horizontal stretch — Hoshino's since the two shotguns swapped patterns on 2026-10-10).
- A unit may carry a **note** (`cardNote`, user 2026-10-24): one line at the card's bottom-left, under the art. Kurumi's reads "This unit has 20% more health." A map may carry one too (`cardNote` in the client's `MAP_DATA`; District's and Compound's read "Unit has more stamina in this map.", user 2026-10-29 and 2026-10-30), shown on its info card the same way, under the picture. (The notes were off for part of 2026-11-01, while every map had their boost, and came back with it; Neon carried the note from 2026-11-01 until its 5 went on 2026-11-03.)

The grid keeps the same sizes on phones (checked at 390 px: nothing wraps or spills).

### Random & All Random cards

Both offline and online pickers carry them:

- Every unit grid has a gray **Random** card (question-mark thumbnail): it rolls a unit the roster being built doesn't already contain (different players can still land on the same unit). The map picker is two sections, each with a Random card of its own: **Scene Maps** — the maps cut from modelled scenes (Town, Spacecraft) — over **Concept Maps**, the hand-made arenas; a section's Random rolls among that section alone and skips **Shooting Range** and **Plain Field** (`noRandom` in the map table; user 2026-10-19) and, since b1027p, **District** too (user 2026-10-30, "too much of a special map" — a manual pick only, offline and online alike: the roll is the picker's, the host's included). A map's row may say which part of its card picture the wide thumb shows (`thumb: 'top' | 'bottom'`; the thumb box cuts a square picture to a band, the centre by default) — Backrooms and Warehouse show their upper part, Spacecraft its lower; the profile shows the whole picture (user 2026-10-24).
- Every unit grid also carries a golden **All Random** card: one confirm fills every remaining unit slot of the current flow at once and advances it (offline: the rest of your roster, then every bot picker that follows — Duel included; online: the rest of your roster). The map picker deliberately has no All Random.

**The Splat skin** (PT, b1105l, 2026-11-05) — the first pass of the UI rework, the user's pick of seven directions drawn on the "GunVS UI Rework Samples" design canvas (A Tactical, B Arcade, C Signal, D Slash after Persona 5 and Valorant, E Holo after Apex and Overwatch, F Splat after Splatoon and Jet Set Radio, G Ops after Call of Duty: Mobile): direction F. Every menu is an opaque cream sheet with three ink blobs (an inline SVG in `style.css`, pinned while the content scrolls; user: the cream covers the 3D behind), the titles white Titan One stickers with an ink outline and a magenta drop, the buttons and option chips white pills with a 3 px ink border and a hard shadow (the chosen chip magenta; Sudden Death ink with yellow type), each a degree or two off square. The lobby keeps its flow — the first unit pick is made on it — with its four option rows labelled Play as / Teams / Roster / Rules (`showSelectMenu`). The unit grid is 84 px sticker tiles (`unitGridHTML`), seven to a row on a landscape phone so the 17 units and the two Random cards sit in three rows without scrolling; a tile shows the character's name and a weapon-class tag on its corner — AR, RF (rifle), SMG, SG, MG, SN (sniper) — and is coloured by that class (cyan, cyan, lime, yellow, magenta, ink). The map picker's two sections are tabs now, Scene Maps | Concept Maps with their counts (`mapGridHTML`, wired in `wireMapGrid`), one grid shown at a time; a tab's Random card rolls its own tab alone, as the stacked sections did. The profile card of both pickers (`.unit-profile-popup`) is a white sticker with a lime "tap to confirm" tag hanging off its bottom; its contents — the art, the weapon panel, the six stats, a unit's or a map's note — are as before. The match HUD keeps every box the JS mirrors (the corner bars' 180 px, the thumb buttons' `--btn-size`, the joystick's 140, the dio cards' 180x68 / 132x52) and wears the skin inside them: the bars a white rim with an ink outline and the fills lime (you) / magenta (enemy) / cyan (ally) / yellow (enemy 2), the online slot colours still set by the JS; the thumb buttons white, DODGE cyan, SPRINT magenta, TARGET yellow, FIRE's count in Titan One and its reload ring lime; PAUSE a sticker; the commander's cards ink with a white rim. The crosshair is untouched (the CS classic, user). Titan One and Rubik are bundled from `client/src/fonts` (latin and latin-ext, SIL Open Font License — the OFL texts beside them; 72 KB in all) so the PWA has them offline; the stylesheet's `url()`s go through Vite, so the hashed files follow the `./` base. Not in this pass: real ink-splat art in place of the SVG blobs, Chinese type. Online room, pause / guide / settings and the commander's order tray took the same components with no design of their own, as asked.

**The slot lobby and the pickers' page** (PT, b1105m, 2026-11-05; user: "i want that layout previously shown in the artifact" and, of the HUD sample, "i especially love those buttons and the simple arrows inside") — the lobby is the design canvas's: your unit's card on the left (the profile art, the name, the class and the weapon; a Trio roster as three thumbs), the option rows in the middle — Play as / Teams / Roster / Rules / Where (Online hands over to the online lobby, as the old button did) — with the Dummy and Spectator toggles under them (they sat on the map picker's page), the map's card on the right (its picture, its name, its section and its size in metres from the boundary table, its stamina note), and along the foot the summary chip, a Random die (rolls your unit, or your whole Trio roster), All random (rolls you and arms the chain) and GO!!. A card opens its picker and comes back; GO!! fills what is still missing — the pickers for an incomplete roster, then the bot pickers as ever — and starts on the card's map, so the map picker is no longer the chain's last page (`showSelectMenu`, `renderLobbySlots`, `startFromLobby`, `startFromMap`; `state.mapKey` starts at Plain Field). The pickers' page (`showUnitPicker`, `showMapPicker`): a back arrow (to the lobby) and the title on one row, the unit picker's class filter chips beside them (All / AR / SMG / SG / MG / Sniper; the rifles count as AR), the preview card on the left — the art, the name and class pill, five range pips from the lock range, the weapon's name and its six stats (the gun render stays on the floating card), or a map's section, size and tags (manual pick only, offline only, the stamina note) — with a CONFIRM! button; the tapped tile wears a PICK! tag, and tapping it again confirms as before. `spawnProfilePopup` fills the page's side card when the menu has one and floats the old card otherwise (the online pickers); on a portrait phone the side card floats over the grid as the old popup did. The thumb buttons carry arrows over their words — JUMP up, DODGE a sidestep, SPRINT a dash, TARGET a sight (`HUD_BTN_ICON`). The first lobby and the first frame moved to the module's end: the lobby's map card reads `GLB_MAPS`, declared far below the old spot, and the first frame's `updateMapRoof` read it before it stood — the render error logged on every page load since the roof hiding is gone with it.

**The lobby's second pass** (PT, b1105n, 2026-11-05; the user's five notes on b1105m) — the cards' art is the square thumbnail (the face-centred crop the tiles use) shown a little above its middle, where the full profile art cut to a band lost some units' eyes; the picker's side card the same. The two offline toggles are the **Practice** row (Dummy bots, Spectator — each on or off alone, lit yellow) under a new **Bots** row (I choose | Random: who picks the bots' units at GO!!, `state.botPick`). The foot: **Guide** and **Server** on the left (they were a "?" and "SRV" at the top-right, and the lone "?" read as the guide), and on the right **Random unit** and **All random** as dice with their words, then GO!!. **All random** no longer starts the match by itself: it rolls your unit (your Trio roster) and sets the Bots row to Random, so GO!! asks nothing more; the pickers' All Random card does the same and returns to the lobby. A site open lands on Saori (as before) and **Town** (Plain Field until now).

**The lobby's third pass** (PT, b1105o, 2026-11-05; the user's next five notes) — the option rows are taller and further apart (38 px, 10 px between; five of them: Play as / Teams / Roster / Rules / Practice). The Where row is gone: **ONLINE** is a sticker of GO!!'s height beside it ("vs player" under the word; it opens the online lobby as the old button did). The Bots row is gone with it: **All random · GO** rolls your unit (your Trio roster) and every bot and starts the match at once — the pickers' All Random card does the same — while GO!! alone runs the bot pickers as ever. The guide's round **"?"** is back at the top-right; Server stays a small pill at the foot's left. The **Trio** card draws the roster as the user's sketch: three slanted panels side by side, each on its unit's class colour with the face-centred thumbnail, the slashes between them the ink showing through, a slot still to pick a yellow "?" panel; the three names under them, and "n more to pick" while the roster is short (GO!! then opens the pickers for the missing slots alone).

**The lobby's fourth pass** (PT, b1105p, 2026-11-05; the user's next five notes) — the guide's **"?"** sits in the sheet's own top-right corner (a direct child of the sheet, not of the centred head). The **Server** pill is gone from the lobby: **Online** leads to an online connect page — the status chip, a word on the sleeping server, **Server Boot-up** (the debug panel, as before), **Leave** back to the lobby — in place of the bare "Connecting…" overlay, at the first connect and at every reconnect (renderOnlineUi's 'connecting'). **All random** rolls every slot of both cards (you — your Trio roster —, the ally in 2v2, the enemy, the second enemy) and shows them on the cards; it no longer starts the match (b1105o's All random · GO; user: "All random+Go is not ideal"). **GO!!** starts with nothing left to ask when every card is full, else the pickers for the missing picks alone, slot by slot (you, ally, enemy, enemy 2). An **enemy card** stands on the right where the map's card was — the same card as yours: one unit in a Duel 1v1, otherwise a row of slanted panels per slot (two rows in 2v2) and a line of names per slot (YOU / ALLY, ENEMY / ENEMY 2); its Pick / Change runs the enemy's pickers ("Select Enemy Unit", "Select Enemy 2 Unit"). A card's **Pick** fills the missing picks alone while something is missing (Saori, the default, stays as slot 1, so a fresh Trio's pickers start at 2/3); **Change** on a full card asks the roster(s) afresh; the pickers' All Random card fills the rest of that card only and returns to the lobby (in b1105o it started the match from there). The **map** moved to a chip at the foot's left (thumbnail, name, MAP · section · size, Change → the map page); the chip yields width first, so the four buttons never wrap on a landscape phone, and under 760 px the foot wraps (the chip full width, the buttons centred in a row of their own). A single roster's three names may take two lines (never a cut name); a 2v2 card lists its two rosters in Rubik 11.5 px with thin spaces. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots5.mjs`, 844×390 and 412×915): the corner "?" 9 px from the edges; All random refreshes both cards with no picker and no match; GO!! after it opens the HUD with no picker; the 2v2 Trio cards show two rows of three panels and fill from All random; the Trio card's Pick runs (2/3)(3/3) and returns to the lobby, Change asks (1/3), the enemy card asks "Select Enemy Unit (2/3)", GO!! with the enemy trio missing asks the same; Online shows the connect page (Server Boot-up / Leave) and Leave returns to the lobby with no overlay left.

**The lobby's fifth pass** (PT, b1105q, 2026-11-05; the user's next five notes) — the **enemy and the map swap places**: the map's card is back in the right column and shows its **whole square picture** (`object-fit: contain`, letterboxed on ink, 134 px tall; user: "the full uncropped image, not the further cropped version" — MAP_DATA.thumb stays the picker's wide tile's business), and the enemy side is a **chip at the foot's left**: a strip of its units' faces (small slanted panels, one row per slot; in 2v2 Trio the two rows stacked so the names keep their room), the names with ENEMY / ENEMY 2 pips before them, Pick / Change; the whole chip runs the enemy's pickers. A **2v2 Duel** card is one row split left / right (you | ally) instead of two full-width rows. Every roster panel is now a flex item of its row, overlapping its neighbour by 15 px and clipped to a parallelogram (slanted edges 18 px), so the **face sits in the middle of its own panel** (user: "the face should be in the middle"; the old thirds-of-the-row geometry showed a slice offset from the face), and the single card's wide band is taken lower on the square thumbnail (`center 70%`: the eyes sit at about 62% of its height, so the old 40% band cut them in a short row). The **Random unit** button is gone (All random stays). Small things: the size line uses no-break spaces, the sub line keeps 52 px clear of the Change word, a 2v2 Trio card's sub reads "three each, in turn". Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots6.mjs`): the foot holds in 390 px with no Random unit button; the map card's image is the whole 1024-square, contained; the 2v2 Duel card is one row of two panels; the 2v2 Trio card two rows of three and the chip six faces in two stacked rows; the card's Pick runs (2/3)(3/3); the chip's Pick asks "Select Enemy Unit (2/3)"; the map card opens "Select Map"; GO!! after All random opens the HUD with no picker; portrait wraps the chip full width and the buttons under it.

**Faces, the rifles' colour, the memo tape** (PT, b1105r, 2026-11-05; the user's next notes) — **Face placement**: every picture box of a unit (the lobby card's wide band, every slanted panel, the chip's small faces, the picker's side card) is a `face-box` — a CSS size container — and `UNIT_FACE` (main.js) gives a unit's face centre x and eye line y as fractions of its square thumbnail, read off a contact sheet with a 10% grid (`ui/face_sheet.mjs`; 0.5 / 0.62 unless set: Fubuki 0.43 / 0.63, Haruka 0.47, Atsuko y 0.68, Aris / Asuna / Koyuki / Kurumi x 0.52, Hina 0.48, Rabu 0.53 / 0.63). `object-position` is computed from the box's own width and height in container units so that point sits at the box's horizontal centre and at 44% of its height — a tall box crops the sides, a wide box the top and bottom, and a min()/max() pair makes the other axis 0 so the picture always covers the box (user: the eyes "should be higher" on a single character — the side card's band was cut at 42% from the top, where the eyes of these thumbnails are at about 62% and Atsuko's at 68%, so Fubuki's eyes sat on its bottom edge and Atsuko's below it; and Fubuki "cropped a bit more to the right": her face is at 43% of her thumbnail, so a centred crop showed it left of the panel). The side card's band is 100 px now (88). Browsers without container units leave the picture centred. **Rifle** (RF: Fubuki, Aris) has its own class colour, lavender `#C9B2FF`, no longer AR's cyan (user: "rifle and AR are not the same category"), and the picker's filter row has a **Rifle** chip; AR no longer folds the rifles in. **The memo tape**: a translucent yellow strip, torn at both ends, stuck over the top edge of the pickers' side cards (unit and map), left of centre, a few degrees off (user: "inside the selection menus, i want that yellow memo tape style" — the design canvas's F map card); in portrait, where the card is a scrolling overlay, it sits just inside the edge. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots7.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots8.mjs`): the computed object-position of the lobby card (−64 px), the side card on Fubuki (−90 px) and Atsuko (−100 px), Fubuki's middle panel (−11 px sideways), the chip's and the 2v2 rows' panels; the Rifle filter shows unit10 and unit7, AR unit1, unit9, unit14; the RF tag is lavender, AR cyan; the tape 96 × 22 px at the top edge of both side cards and inside the portrait overlay.

**HUD colours, the spectator ring, the tape and the map card** (PT, b1105s, 2026-11-05; the user's next notes) — **JUMP** is lime (the stick's old colour) and the **stick's knob red** (`--red #FF4F4F`; user: "give jump button the green tone the joycon has. change joycon green to red"). **Spectator**: the watched unit's corner bar wears a thick yellow ring inside an ink line and a wide halo that pulses yellow / white (1.1 s; still under reduced motion), and the Trio icon row's watched line glows yellow too (user: the white glow "is not obvious enough"; the ring stops 6 px out so the player's bar's ring just meets the ally's bar). **The memo tape in portrait** hangs over the card's top edge as in landscape: the overlay card no longer clips its outside — its inside scrolls instead when it must (user: "the tape shouldn't be cropped on the top"). **The map picker's info card** shows the whole square picture (contain on ink, 164 px tall in landscape, a square box in portrait; user: "the info card is not using the uncropped image"). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots9.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots8.mjs`): a 2v2 spectator match marks `.health` as watched with the ring; the stick's computed colour is the red and JUMP's the lime; the tape sits at −11 px above both side cards in landscape and above the portrait overlay; the portrait map card's picture box is 327 × 327, contained.

**The folder and the memo** (PT, b1105t–u, 2026-11-05; user: "make the character box on the left file folder style and the map on the right a memo-pinned-on-the-wall style … implement that to all character cards and map cards") — every unit card is a sheet in a **manila file folder** and every map card a **memo on the wall**. The lobby's left column is the **LINE-UP folder** (`.lobby-folder`, manila `#F6D27A`, a 24 px tab reading LINE-UP, a thumb notch on its right edge) holding two sheets: your unit's card (its YOUR UNIT sticker moved to the sheet's right, under the tab) and the enemy's slip (the face strip, the ENEMY / ENEMY 2 pip on its own line over the names, Change / Pick as a magenta sticker on the slip's top-right corner; in 2v2 Trio the strip's two rows stack). The map's card on the right is the **memo**: a white note with cyan ruled lines under the picture, a strip of the yellow tape over its top edge, centred, the whole square picture on ink, MAP · section · size, Change on the name's line. The grid is two rows now: the folder spans both, the option rows and the memo fill the first, the foot's buttons (All random / Online / GO!!) the second. The unit picker's side card is a folder too (tab UNIT FILE, a paper clip on the sheet's corner), the map picker's a memo (the tape over the top edge, no pin — b1105u, user); the floating cards of the online pickers follow (`.pp-folder` / `.pp-memo`). **SR** is the sniper rifle's tag (was SN; user), in the tiles, the filter chip and the class colours. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots10.mjs`): the 1v1 and the 2v2 Trio lobbies fit 390 px (sheet scroll height 390), the slip opens "Select Enemy Unit", the Sniper filter shows unit3 and unit6 with SR tags and no SN left, the folder card carries the tab and the clip, the memo card the 84 px tape and no pin; portrait keeps the folder and the memo side by side under the rows. (b1105v, user: "make the file slightly longer so the ally box and enemy box do not overlap") the sheet and the slip are 12 px apart on every screen — the slip's Change sticker, 9 px over its top edge, no longer touches the sheet — and on a screen at least 440 px tall (desktop, portrait) the folder is longer: the sheet 208 px, its art 118 px, the two 16 px apart. The slip's text lost its ENEMY pip and its corner sticker names the side instead of saying Change — ENEMY / ENEMY TRIO / ENEMY TEAM (user: "remove the enemy text box; change the change text to enemy text"); a short roster still says "n more to pick" in its small line. The design canvas (artifact 1489ieHXfUJZ3okkbwshBu, round 5) holds the folder-and-memo lobby as built and a **proposal for the match HUD**, not built: the corner HP bars grow into the F sample's chips (an ink pill with the portrait in a ring, the name with YOU / ENEMY, the bar, the number; the Trio row as faces under it; the ally's and the second enemy's chips smaller under the first two; the spectator's watched chip with the b1105s yellow ring), and the bottom boost bar becomes a yellow BOOST sticker in the sample's ammo-chip style (the word, an ink gauge with a white knob, the number; magenta when low).

**The HP chips and the BOOST sticker** (PT, b1105w, 2026-11-05; the design canvas's round 5, built on the user's "implement the HUD on PT") — the match HUD's corner bars and the Trio weapon-icon rows are gone; each slot has an **HP chip** (`.hp-chip`, main.js `hpChipHTML` / `renderHpChip`): an ink pill with a white rim and an ink outline, the portrait of the unit on the field in a ring of the side's colour (the thumbnail at 150% with its UNIT_FACE point at the frame's centre), the name in Titan One, the side word (YOU / ALLY / ENEMY / ENEMY 2) in the colour, and the bar — as long as the unit's HP (`--hp-scale`), a round knob at the fill's head (`--w`) — no number. In **Trio** the roster's other two units sit at the chip's far end as small faces, a fallen one greyed (the reserve, from the slot's roster and cursor offline, the snapshot's roster fields online). Yours top-left, the enemy's mirrored top-right; in 2v2 the ally's and the second enemy's smaller under them (cyan, yellow). Online the chips anchor by server slot as the bars did, in the slot's colour (`--c`); the side word says which is you (the thin white "self" rim is gone). **Spectator**: no side words while the viewer is a spectator — the Practice toggle or an online spectator (user: "if it's spectator mode, remove you/ally/enemy/enemy 2 text; if the spectator mode is triggered by player dying, then these text remains") — and the WATCHED chip (the camera's unit, by the toggle or because the player is out) wears the b1105s yellow ring with a WATCHING tag; a slot out for good dims with a DOWN tag. **Sudden Death** hides the chips' bars alone. The fullscreen button docks right of the left chip by `--left-chip-right`, a ResizeObserver on the chip (the chip is as wide as its name, its bar and its faces). The **BOOST sticker**: yellow, level, at the bottom centre — the word and an ink gauge with a white knob (`--bw`), no number; the fill turns magenta under 30% (`.low`). The DOM of a chip is rebuilt only when its unit, its word or its reserve changes; the bar and the classes move every frame. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots11.mjs`): a 1v1 Trio match shows both chips with two reserve faces each and the fullscreen button 10 px right of the left chip; a 2v2 Spectator match shows four chips with no side words and the ring on the player's chip, moving to the ally's on TARGET; a 2v2 Sudden Death match shows the chips with the bars hidden (`display: none`) and the words in place; a 1v1 on a 1280 px window. Not exercised headless: the DOWN tag and the after-death WATCHING ring (they need a death). (b1105x, the user's three notes on V472) the chips' far side has 18 px of padding (16 on the small chips) and the reserve faces start 12 px after the track, so the knob — 8 px past the track at full HP — stays 10 px inside the rim ("the HP bar should stay well inside the box"); the BOOST fill is the SPRINT button's magenta at every level, no ink and no low-state colour ("shouldn't be black"); and the round frames show the thumbnail at 125% with the face's middle at the centre, clamped to keep the frame covered, so the whole head fits ("face should be at the center" — 150% with the eyes at 46% cut the hair and the chin). (b1105y–z) the chips were levelled for a build on a misread of "still tilted" and **tilt again** (±1.5°, as the boards had them; the BOOST sticker alone is level) — the user meant the faces, which sat left and high in their browser: the round frames now place the face by an **inline transform computed in JS** (`faceTransform`: the thumbnail at 130%, moved so its UNIT_FACE point meets the frame's centre, clamped to ±15% so the frame stays covered) instead of the CSS calc / clamp / container-unit maths of b1105x, which rendered differently from browser to browser. The design canvas's round 6 (`End_F`, `End_F_2v2`) proposes the end-of-match page — the map memo in the middle, a roster panel each side in the HUD's round frames with the fallen greyed, Select Unit and REMATCH below — not built; after the user's notes on it the panels are wider (280 px, the last frame 24 px in from the edge), the memo carries the picture and the map's name alone (no MAP · section · size line, no rules caption) and its faces use the same transform.

**The memo's die** (PT, b1105aa, 2026-11-05; user: "add a random map dice at the bottom right of the map memo paper that picks randomly from both scene map and concept map pool") — the lobby's map memo has a **die sticker** at its bottom-right on the ruled lines (`.memo-dice`: the All random button's die, magenta, 36 px, tilted −6°): a tap rolls **one draw over both tabs' maps together** (`rollLobbyMap`: every map of MAP_DATA but the noRandom ones — Plain Field, District, the Backrooms, the Shooting Range, the same manual-only set the picker's Random cards skip — and never the map already on the memo), the memo shows the new map at once with a half-second spin of the die, and the picker stays shut; the rest of the memo still opens the picker. The paper does not press down with the die (`:has(.memo-dice:active)`). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots12.mjs`): on the 844 × 390 lobby the die (36 px, 9 px in from the memo's right, 5 px up from its bottom) sits 8 px under the Change word and the size line's text ends 6 px short of it; six taps gave six maps, each different from the last, from both sections (Spacecraft, Neon, Backrooms, Airport, Spacecraft, Town), with the lobby still up and no picker; a tap on the name opens Select Map; the portrait and the 1045 px lobbies keep the die on the memo's corner.

**No watched chip in Command mode; four chips of one size on two clear rows** (PT, b1105ab, 2026-11-05; user: "no need WATCHING and glowing HP bar effect in command mode", "no overlapping for HP boxes; make all HP boxes same size — use the current bigger size one as standard") — in Command mode the camera is the tabletop, so no HP chip is the watched one: the yellow ring, its pulse and the WATCHING tag stay off there (`updateHud`: `watching` is false while `dioramaActive()`), whatever drives the player's slot — the Spectator toggle, or the player being out. Shooter mode is as before: the Spectator toggle, an online spectator and the after-death camera still ring the camera's chip. In **2v2** the ally's and the second enemy's chips are no longer the small kind: all four are the first row's chip (54 px, the 40 px face, the 15 px name, the 150 px bar, the 24 px reserve faces), the second row 86 px down and 10 px further in — 22 px under the first row's box, so a WATCHING / DOWN tag (now hanging 9 px under its chip, not 13) and a second-row chip's 7 px ring clear each other with the tilts (the b1105w second row sat 70 px down, 40 px tall: in Trio the first row's wide chips, their ring and their tag overlapped it). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots13.mjs`): a 2v2 Trio with the Spectator toggle in Command and a 2v2 Duel in Command alone show four chips of one height and no `.spec-watched`; a 2v2 Trio Spectator match in Shooter rings exactly one chip with the WATCHING tag and TARGET moves it to the second row; no two chip boxes overlap in any of them.

**The end-of-match page, the command cards' colours, the reserve's order** (PT, b1105ac, 2026-11-05; user: "implement the ending screen in PT", "use the HP bar color as base, make the color of command card aligned", "the next-up unit should be placed at the uncovered position") — the match's end is the design canvas's round-6 page (main.js `showEndPage`, shared by the offline `showEndMenu` and the online `showOnlineEndMenu`): the verdict as the sticker title (YOU WIN / YOU LOSE; TEAM 1 WINS / TEAM 2 WINS for a spectator; MATCH ENDED on an online tie), a **roster panel** each side — the HUD chip's ink and white rim as a card, TEAM 1 (the viewer's side, left: the player's team, or the camera slot's team for an online spectator) and TEAM 2, the yellow WINNER sticker on the side that won — with a row per slot: the slot's word (YOU / ALLY, ENEMY / ENEMY 2; SLOT 1 / SLOT 2 for a spectator) and the slot's roster in the HUD's round frames with the names: a fallen unit greyed with a slash, the unit on the field alive with the dot, a reserve unit plain (Trio from the roster and its cursor, offline `trioRosters` / `rosterIdx`, online the snapshot's roster fields; Duel the one unit); the **map's memo** in the middle (the lobby's — the tape, the ruled lines, the whole picture on ink and the name alone); **Select Unit** (Leave online) and **REMATCH** below, the lobby's GO!! style. The rows' colours are the chips' (lime / cyan, magenta / yellow). The sheet centres the three; portrait wraps them and scrolls. **Command mode's cards** wear the HP chips' colours now (`DIO_SLOT_META`: lime YOU, cyan ALLY, magenta ENEMY 1, yellow ENEMY 2 — the card's border, its HP fill, its marker square and its line; online the slot palette as before, which the online chips share), and the cards' stamina gauge is white (two yellow bars on ENEMY 2's card read as one). **The chips' reserve faces** are ordered so the NEXT-UP unit is the outermost, last-drawn face — whole, not under its neighbour: the fallen first (inner), then the upcoming from the farthest to the next (`renderHpChip`). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots14.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots15.mjs`): a 1v1 Trio ended by zeroing HP from the dev state shows YOU WIN with WINNER on TEAM 1, your row one out / one standing / one in reserve against three out, the memo's picture loaded and 172 × 122 inside it, and REMATCH starts a match; a 2v2 Duel under the Spectator toggle shows TEAM 2 WINS with SLOT 1 / SLOT 2 words and Select Unit returns to the lobby; a 1v1 on a 1280 px window and a 2v2 Trio in portrait (the three stacked, the sheet scrolling) render; a 2v2 Trio in Command shows each card's border and fill equal to its chip's ring colour and the chips' last reserve face equal to the slot's next prebuilt unit.

**ArchiveVS; the online room's names; the last kill on the end page; bigger reserve faces; the cards' names** (b1105ad, 2026-11-05; the user's notes on the 0.9.5 line's first online matches) — the game is **ArchiveVS** in the lobby's title, the guide's first line, the page title and the web manifest (was GunVS / Gun VS Gun). **The online queue room** names a player's units by character (Saori, not "Unit 1 / Assault Rifle"), as the bots' rows did, and the host's "tap to change" hint sits LEFT of a bot row's names (`.roster-hint`), so every row's names end on the same right edge. **The end page online** read the mechs, which are mirrored only in the playing frames — the frame that turns the page over mirrors nothing, so the last unit killed still showed alive (user: "the last killed unit is shown as alive"); `endRowUnits` now reads the LAST SNAPSHOT (the server sends the final tick's before `match:end`) and, on a knockout, marks the losing team's every unit out whatever the data says. **The chips' reserve faces** are 34 px, side by side 4 px apart (24 px overlapping by 7 until now; user: "bigger without overlapping with others or changing other existing layouts") — the chip alone grows, by its own width (a Trio chip 320 px, was 289), the rows and the fullscreen button's dock follow as before. **Command mode's cards** name the unit, in capitals, as its chip does (user: "change the YOU / ENEMY 1 / ALLY / ENEMY 2 to unit name as in HP box"); the side is the card's colour. Verified headless: an online Trio 1v1 against the branch's own server (`ui/ui_online1.mjs`, the server on a local port) — the room reads "You — Saori Marina Hoshino" and "tap to change (BOT) Saori Saori Saori" with the hint's right edge left of the names', and after the idle host's three units fall (51 s) the end page reads YOU LOSE with the three out and the bot's unit standing, the last snapshot at hp 0; the Command and HUD checks (`ui_shots15.mjs`, `ui_shots13.mjs`) show the cards named by unit, the chips 320 px wide, two rows 15 px apart and no two boxes overlapping; the offline end page (`ui_shots14.mjs`) as before. One brain, online and offline (the user asked): `shared/src/sim/nbrain.js` drives every bot — the server's `tickBot` and the offline client's `updateEnemy` each host it — and the Sudden Death brain is shared the same way.

**The next-up marker** (b1105ae, 2026-11-05; user: "now user cannot tell who's up next — add a little glowing bar or triangle at the marked areas so user will know the one next to it is the next unit") — in a Trio chip a small **glowing white triangle** (`.chip-next`, 8 × 12 px, a soft yellow halo, breathing) sits between the track and the reserve and points at the **next-up** face, which is now the FIRST of the reserve, right beside it; the upcoming units follow in roster order and the fallen come last, greyed (b1105ac had put the next-up outermost because the faces overlapped then; since b1105ad they stand apart, so the natural order returns). No marker when nothing is left to come. The chip grows by the marker alone (a Trio chip 333 px). PT and the 0.9.5 line alike. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots15.mjs`, `ui/ui_shots13.mjs`): in a 2v2 Trio every chip's first reserve face is the slot's next prebuilt unit and the marker lies between the track's end and that face on both sides; the rows keep 15 px apart with no two boxes overlapping.

**The memos' red crayon** (b1105af, 2026-11-05; the design canvas's round 7, the user's pick: style B) — District's and Compound's little "· more stamina" leaves the lobby memo's size line and the map card's note line, and Backrooms (Anomaly) reads **Backrooms** on the memos (`MAP_DATA.shortName`); in their place a line of **red crayon** on the memo's ruled lines — "More Stamina!" (any map with a `cardNote`, or its own `crayon`), "Anomaly!" — in **Permanent Marker** (bundled, Apache 2.0, `client/src/fonts`), 17 px, four degrees off, multiplied into the paper so the rules show through the stroke, its edge roughened by an SVG noise filter (`#crayon`, put on the page by main.js before the first lobby). The lobby memo and the end page's memo place it by hand, left of the die (`.memo-crayon`); the picker's side memo and the floating card let it flow under the size line and the tags (`.memo-crayon-flow`). The picker's grid tiles keep the full names. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots16.mjs`): District and Compound picked from the lobby show the mark 19 px before the die and 19 px above the memo's edge with no "more stamina" in the size line, the side memo shows it under the tags with no note line, Backrooms (Anomaly) reads Backrooms with "Anomaly!" on the lobby memo, the side memo and the end page's memo, Town wears nothing, and the Permanent Marker face reports loaded.

**The narrow landscape phone** (b1105ag, 2026-11-05; user, with pictures of a phone about 640 × 342 CSS px in landscape: "the mobile end layout is off, too much overlapping in game, and i want the main menu layout same as desktop end") — **The lobby** keeps the desktop's three columns on every landscape screen: the wrap (`@media (max-width: 760px)`) is for portrait alone, and a landscape screen narrower than the columns (722 px + the sheet's 32) **zooms the grid** to fit (`showSelectMenu` fitLobby: CSS `zoom`, re-measured on resize — 0.84 at 640 px, 0.93 at 700, 1 from 754 up). **The match HUD**: an HP chip never passes its half of the screen less PAUSE, the fullscreen button and the margins (`max-width: calc(50vw - 112px)`): the bar gives first (down to 56 px), then the name is cut with an ellipsis; the face and the reserve keep their size. The fullscreen button docks right of the left chip but never past PAUSE's left edge (`min(--left-chip-right, 50% - 96px)`). The BOOST sticker is shorter under 720 px (32 px tall, the track `min(150px, 22vw)`). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots17.mjs`) at 640 × 342, 700 × 360 and 844 × 390: the lobby shows three columns with no sideways scroll (zoom 0.842 / 0.925 / 1) and the folder, the options and the memo side by side; in a 1v1 Spectator match and a 2v2 Trio match no chip overlaps PAUSE, the fullscreen button or the other chip, and the BOOST sticker clears the stick and the ammo dial (at 640 the Trio chips' bars are at their 56 px floor); a 412 × 915 portrait lobby still wraps.

**The narrow phone, second pass** (b1105ah–ai, 2026-11-05; the user's notes on b1105ag's pictures: "no '…', make sure text fit", "the full screen button is overlapping", "HP bar / button / thumbnails / triangle overlapping, cut off text, HP bar too short; if one of YOU / ALLY / ENEMY and the unit name must be omitted for the space, omit the side word") — **The lobby's** middle column asks 320 px at the least (was 250), so every option pill holds its words at the zoomed sizes; the zoom's reference is 792 px. **The chips' name line** wraps instead of cutting: the name never shrinks or takes an ellipsis; when the side word does not fit beside it, the word drops to a second line that the line's height clips — the word goes whole, by the space alone. **Under 720 px** the chips come in a smaller cut (46 px, a 34 px face, a 120 px bar, 26 px reserve faces, a smaller marker), PAUSE is a size down with the **fullscreen button under it** in the middle (between the second row's chips in 2v2), so each first-row chip may run to 58 px short of the middle and the second row to 44 px; the second row sits 70 px down; the thumb buttons and the stick are a size down so the second row clears DODGE. **At 844 × 390** the second row steps in by 108 px and is a little narrower: DODGE's top reaches into the row's band there, so the second enemy's chip must end before the thumb cluster, and the ally's steps in the same for one stagger. The fullscreen button docks 16 px off the left chip (clear of its WATCHING ring) and never past PAUSE. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots17.mjs`) at 640 × 342, 700 × 360 and 844 × 390: no option pill is cut; in a 1v1 Spectator match and a 2v2 Trio match no chip overlaps PAUSE, the fullscreen button, DODGE, the stick or the other chips, the fullscreen button sits 13 px or more from every chip, every name shows whole, the side words show where they fit and go whole where they do not, and the bars read 120 / 120 (1v1) and 114 / 111 / 120 / 120 (2v2 Trio) at 640, 150 and 128 / 128 / 112 / 112 at 844; the portrait lobby still wraps.

**No HP chips on the Shooting Range** (b1105aj, 2026-11-05; user: "no need hp bars in shooting range") — on the practice map the HUD shows no HP chips (`updateHud` sets `hud-range` on the HUD and on `<html>`; `.touch-hud.hud-range .hp-chip { display: none }`), the fullscreen button sits where the left chip would start, and the BOOST sticker stays. Verified headless (`__startMatch` on the range at 844 × 390 and 640 × 342): both chips hidden, the button at 12 / 10, BOOST shown; Town keeps its two chips.

**Pickers keep the desktop layout on narrow landscape screens** (b1105ak, 2026-11-05; user: "the layout in selection menus are also deformed") — the unit and map pickers' floating card over the grid is for portrait phones alone now (`@media (max-width: 760px) and (orientation: portrait)`); a landscape screen narrower than the pickers' 812 px page keeps the side card beside the six-column grid and zooms the header row and the body to fit (`fitPickerMenu`, as the lobby's `fitLobby`). Verified headless: at 640 × 342 zoom 0.749, the card at 12–197 beside the grid (202–627), CONFIRM at 321 of 342, no sideways scroll, the map picker alike; 844 × 390 unchanged (zoom 1); portrait 412 × 915 still floats the card.

**Smaller, equal HP chips and bigger thumb buttons on a narrow landscape phone; the pickers' card never cut** (b1105al, 2026-11-05; user: "hp boxes too big. buttons way too small. HP boxes sizes not aligned. i can afford smaller hp boxes" and "in selection menu, make the file and memo paper uncropped" — the phone is about 709 × 327 CSS px) — **HUD, every width:** each chip is ONE width now (`.hp-chip { width }`: room for the face, the match's longest bar — `--hp-max`, set by `updateHud` from the slots' units and their reserves — and in Trio the reserve block, `.hud-trio`), so the four boxes are alike whatever their names; both rows share one cap. **The narrow tier (≤ 720 px):** chips 34 px tall (26 px face, 90 px bar, 20 px reserve faces, 11 px name), 141 px wide in a duel and 207 px in Trio, both rows flush at 12 px (top 8 and 48, done by 82); the thumb buttons `clamp(56px, min(10vw, 20vh), 84px)` — 64–69 px on the phone sizes instead of 56 — with the cluster 12 px from the corner, its top always under the second row. **The pickers:** `fitPickerMenu` zooms for the height as well: the header row plus the side card (its layout height, the top margin that makes room for the folder tab or the memo tape, and the tilt; 378 / 341 assumed until a card is shown) must stand inside the screen, so the unit file and the map memo are whole; the grid may scroll under a sticky card. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots18.mjs`) at 709 × 327, 640 × 342, 667 × 308, 720 × 332, 844 × 390 and 1280 × 720: chip widths equal in every match, left and right edges flush, no overlaps, no cut names, DODGE 10–19 px under the second row at the narrow sizes; the pickers' card WHOLE at every size with the zoom steady across the first tap (709 × 327: 0.706 both before and after); portrait 412 × 915 unchanged.

**The pickers' card a touch smaller, by plain numbers; the fullscreen button in the menus' top-left corner** (b1105am, 2026-11-05; user: "just a little bit smaller so the whole file can be displayed. the full screen button should be in the up left corner") — on the user's phone the b1105al fit still cut the unit file's bottom by about 9 px: that fit read the zoomed header row's `offsetHeight`, and what such a reading means under `zoom` differs between browsers. `fitPickerMenu` measures nothing zoomed now: the header is 40 px (its back button), the card 378 px (unit file) / 341 px (map memo) — the card at zoom 1 with the top margin for the folder tab or the memo tape and the 2° tilt — and the budget is the visible height (the menu's or `innerHeight`, whichever is less) minus the menu's top padding, two flex gaps and 12 px to spare. The fullscreen button in menus sits at 10 / 10 (inside the safe area) instead of 36 px down; the pickers' header keeps its 40 px left padding, so the back arrow clears it. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots19.mjs`) at 707 × 327, 640 × 342, 667 × 308, 844 × 390, 1280 × 720 and portrait 412 × 915: the card whole with 14–16 px to spare on the phone sizes (zoom 0.687 at 707 × 327, the same before and after the tap), the button at 10 / 10 clear of the back arrow and the lobby title everywhere.

**The lobby memo one height in every mode, the die under its lines; the folder as long; the lobby fits the height too; PRACTICE whole; the thumb buttons by 12vw** (b1105an, 2026-11-05; user: "both the file and the memo can afford to be slightly higher (longer), especially the memo shouldn't shrink itself when the mode is 1v1 and block the dice") — the memo's own height was 235 px (its lines end at 199, the die's 41 px zone began at 194) and the grid row only grew in 2v2 (245), so in 1v1 the die sat on the size line. `.map-slot.memo { min-height: 266px }`: the paper is 266 in 1v1 and 2v2 alike, the die 31–33 px under the lines, and the folder beside it (the same rows) grows as much — 304 px instead of 264 / 284. `fitLobby` now takes the height into account as well (the floor would have overrun a short, wide screen): measured UNZOOMED — the grid's tracks are fixed widths, so its height does not depend on the menu's width and nothing zoomed is read — the zoom is the least of 1, the width fit and (the visible height less what the sheet scrolls beyond the grid at zoom 1) / the grid's height. The option label pill is 72 px with a 0.04em tracking (was 68 / 0.06em, no slack: the user's phone draws text a little wider than this Chromium and spilt PRACTICE's E). The narrow tier's thumb buttons take `min(12vw, 20vh)` (10vw gave 60 px on the user's 600 px page; 65 now). Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots20.mjs`) at 600 × 327 (the user's page), 640 × 342, 707 × 327, 667 × 308, 1280 × 720 and portrait 412 × 915, in 1v1 Duel, 1v1 Trio and 2v2 Trio: the memo 266 everywhere, the die 31–33 px clear, no sheet scrolls (707 × 327 and 667 × 308 zoom to 0.744 / 0.685 by the height), no pill or label cut; the HUD at 600 × 327 and 568 × 320: 65 / 64 px buttons, no overlaps.

**Every lobby pill whole with room; the option label sizes itself** (b1105ao, 2026-11-05) — the 72 px label of b1105an took 4 px that Sudden Death needed (2 px to spare at the 320 px middle column; the 2v2 sheet at 600 × 327 showed "Sudden Dea…"). The middle column's least is 332 px (`minmax(332px, 1fr)`, the zoom reference 804), and the label is a 72 px FLOOR (`min-width`) in a 10 px face with a 0.03em tracking: PRACTICE has 8 px to spare, and a browser that draws it wider widens the pill instead of spilling. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots20.mjs`, a text-range measure with the tilt taken off): at 600 × 327 Sudden Death has 3.7 px to spare and PRACTICE 8; nothing cut at 640 × 342, 707 × 327, 667 × 308, 1280 × 720 or portrait.

**The memo clears the GO button** (b1105ap, 2026-11-05; user: "the memo even overlaps with the GO button") — the memo's 2.5° tilt drops its lower-right corner 9 px under its box and its shadow 7 px more, so with the grid's 8 px row gap the paper stood 1 px off GO and the shadow 6 px into it, in every mode and at every size. The row gap is 22 px: the paper 15 px off GO, the shadow 8. **The thumb buttons sized by the room under the chip rows** (b1105aq, 2026-11-05; user: "the in game buttons can be bigger. they can use the marked space" — the space under the chips, down to the bottom, on a 2v2 picture) — on the narrow tier the five buttons stand in three rows anchored at the bottom-right, so their size is what the height between the chips' last row and the bottom allows: `--btn-size: clamp(56px, min(13vw, (100vh − --hud-rows − 12px − 2 gaps) / 3), 84px)` with `--hud-rows` 50 px in 1v1 (one chip row, done by 44) and 90 px in 2v2 (`.hud-rows-2`, set by `setupHUD`; two rows, done by 84), 13vw so the ammo dial clears the BOOST sticker. On the user's 600 × 310 page that is 77 px in 1v1 and 64 px in 2v2 (56 until b1105am; 65 / 60 in b1105an–ap). In 2v2 the marked area's height is the limit — wider buttons would need a different arrangement. Verified headless (`ui/ui_shots21.mjs`) at 600 × 310, 600 × 327, 568 × 320, 640 × 342, 707 × 327, 720 × 332, 667 × 308 (1v1 74–84 px, 2v2 63–75 px, DODGE 5 px under the chips, the dial 14 px or more off BOOST, no overlaps) and 844 × 390 (the big tier, unchanged at 72).

**The in-match fullscreen button under the left chips on narrow phones** (b1105ar, 2026-11-05; user: "move the full screen button to around here" — an arrow from the button under PAUSE to the space under the ally chip) — on the narrow tier the button sits at 16 px from the left, 54 px down in 1v1 (under the one chip row) and 94 px in 2v2 (under both: `html.hud-rows-2`, set by `setupHUD` beside `in-match` and taken off with it — the button is not inside `.touch-hud`, so the HUD's own class could not reach it). **The box fade back — the walls go see-through again** (b1105be, 2026-11-06; user: "the transparent effect in concept maps seems to be gone, try to bring them back") — since 9f9dabe (Oct 23, Prison) a registered box is `transparent` only while it fades: the flag flips on as the opacity leaves 1 and off as it comes back, so that a solid box is drawn in the opaque pass. three.js (r152 and later; 0.179 here) bakes that flag into the shader — an opaque material's program forces alpha 1 (its `OPAQUE` define) — and re-picks a material's program only on `needsUpdate`; flipped bare, a fading box kept its opaque program and drew SOLID at opacity 0.25 (the number read 0.25; the pixels did not). Every box fade had gone: the concept maps' boxes and the modelled maps' merged fade copies alike. The flip now sets `needsUpdate`, so the right program is picked — flips happen only at a fade's two ends and each program compiles once — and a fresh box stays transparent through its first two passes (`fadeWarm`), so its blended program compiles with the match's first frame (the intro) rather than as a hitch at its first fade. Verified headless on arena2: the mechanism reproduced on the built page (a big building at opacity 0.25 with the flag alone → solid; with `needsUpdate` → the building behind shows through), and with the fix the building between the camera and the watched unit fades to 0.25 and the unit shows through it (the before/after pictures in the session's report).

The collision (`gates_coll.mjs`) is edited on the installed data, so b1105bk's and b1105bl's patches stay: the copy's back strip is copied to its front (the two lines are the same sections moved by Δz, the foot band with them); the original's front strip comes from the data installed before the scaffold's edits (backup_v428), read round that very wall; at both back openings the room between the posts' faces is cleared row by row (the faces the model's own; the posts' boxes and their patches untouched; the slit fills at the original's old corner gone with the slit). Each cage now opens on both sides. The collision (`gates_undo_coll.mjs` in the session's scratch, on the installed data): both lines were open before b1105fj moved a wall onto each, so b1105fj's own changes at those two lines are undone (its run reproduced byte for byte on the data it was run on, `gates_trace.mjs`): its additions there removed, 59 boxes and 12 floors at the copy's line and the 28 boxes and 13 floors from v428 at the original's, and the 46 boxes and 20 floors it had cut put back, every one matched exactly; the back openings b1105fj made stay open. That state still had boxes standing up to 25 cm into each opening beside the jamb posts, 2.25 to 5.25 m up (the reading's cells round a post's hazard pad), so from the bases' top (0.858 m) to the top ring's boxes (5.2525 m) each opening is cleared between the side structures' own collision faces (the W fence's −11.69 and the post's −4.183; the post's 11.303 and the E fence's 18.81), and each jamb post's pad, the one piece of the model there, gets one box of its own (its outer face 0.266 m off the post, 2.34 to 3.43 m up). **Neon's transporters** (PT b1105fk, b1105fl and b1105fo, 2026-11-10; user: "make a golden glowing circle area at this wall ... Within the wall there would be golden text showing \"Hold Jump to Transport\". When unit near to the wall holds jump button, a golden half-transparent circle timer will start running with same style text \"TRANSPORTING\" showing up. The timer takes 1 sec to complete the circle. When the timer is done, the unit will be transported to this position ... The position also has a golden circle area on the ground, but no text. The transportation can be done from this spot as well. Bot should know this as part of the path", and "Transporting unit's own crosshair indicator is enough") — two pads, a new kind of arena record (`shared/src/sim/pads.js`, rows in arena.js `ARENA_PADS`, as the ladders are): A at the tunnel's T junction, a golden ring on its floor that is the zone itself (0.54 m, r 1.1 m round model x −3.30, z 8.00, the middle of the junction's 2.7 m square, 0.25 m clear of the north wall), and the words alone on the north wall over the tube (b1105fj's seal, 3.36 m up; a `sign`, no zone) — b1105fl, user: "Move the transporting circle and its detection on the floor instead of on the wall. The text stays on the wall though, without detection"; b1105fk had drawn the circle round the words on the wall and set the zone 0.9 m in front of it; B on the south tower's walled walkway (21.24 m, r 1.0 m round x −3.31, z 19.76), a ring on its floor. Standing in a zone, a fresh press of jump starts the charge instead of a jump; while the button is held the unit stands (no move, shot, dodge or jump), and 1 s after the press it stands on the other pad's arrival point (a spot beside it if another body is there). Letting go or leaving the zone drops the charge; a hit does not; a unit that jumped in holding the button is not caught; after the move (or a charge dropped with the button held) the held button is spent until let go, so an arrival never sends a unit straight back; no cooldown. Online the hold is the frame's new `jumpDown` (the button's own state; `jump` stays a tap for a unit that does not fly), so the server runs the second and the client's prediction agrees; offline the client runs the same rule. The charging player alone sees a golden ring round its crosshair (the screen's centre in the angled view) filling in the second, TRANSPORTING under it. Bots: each pad is a link on the route grid (`attachLadderLinks`' third argument; cost the second at the climb's speed, never under the cells it spans), taken where the ladders are — travel, Pursue / Maze and command orders, not Defense, the Hide search or Sudden Death; the follower walks to the zone with the goal set (`botPadGoal`), the pad charges and moves it, the brain sleeps for the second. B was reachable by no one but Aris before; every spawn now routes to it through A, and a drop link still takes a bot off B. Verified: `pads.test.js` (8: the records on their floors, the hold, the let-go, the jump-in, the latch, no shot or dodge meanwhile, the second arrival beside the first, the route from every spawn, a bot's trip to a target on B, the snapshot), the shared suite 194 of 194; headless offline both ways, the ring, a jump off the pads still a jump, an offline bot's trip; on the real server with a client's frames, A to B, the held button spent, back to A, a 300 ms press dropped. b1105fl: each pad's ring lies whole on its floor and clear of every wall (a test), the shots from the stem and the real server's run again. **The silver pair** (b1105fo; user: "Add another pair of transport points as well. use silver instead of golden this time"): C on the ground floor south of the bridge, in front of the framed door with the diamond sign (0.54 m, r 1.0 m round model x 4.24, z 67.45, where the user stood; the same floor and nothing in the way 2.65 m round), and D in the covered bridge near its south end (20.24 m, r 1.0 m round x 4.07, z 56.40, where the user stood; the walls 3.3 and 4.0 m off), each sending to the other, no sign. A record's `color` (padRow, 'gold' unless set) is its look alone: the client draws a silver ring and halo (main.js `PAD_LOOKS`) and the charging player's ring and TRANSPORTING in silver (style.css `#pad-ring.pr-silver`); the rule, the hold, the latch and the bots' links are the same for every pad. Routes now take whichever pair is shorter: from the spawns nearest C to the bridge through C, from the others through A, B and the strip. Verified: `pads.test.js` 10 (the records with both pairs and their colours, the user's spots in the zones, C to D and back, a bot's goal on D, the route grid's four links, routes to B and to D from every spawn up through A or C and no other way), the shared suite 196 of 196; headless offline C to D, the held button spent, D to C, the silver ring at the crosshair, the gold A unchanged; on the real server C to D, held on, back to C, a 300 ms press dropped; bots from the five spawns to a player at the bridge's south end, two by the silver pair (there in 3.9 and 8.3 s), three by the gold; the offline client's bot from the ground floor walks to C, charges and arrives on D.

**Neon's bridge: the cube out, the windows open to shots, the sign without boxes, a wall at a ledge's end, the bots' way on** (PT b1105fm and b1105fn, 2026-11-10; user: "remove this weird cube at the end of the catwalk visually and physically", "make sure the projectiles can go through the windows of the catwalk, currently it feels like only unit can pass through, but not bullets", "The neon sign attached to the catwalk needs no cboxes at all", and at x −18.19, z 5.20, 6.48 m, "Add a cbox wall ahead since unit can fall into below area from here") — the covered bridge at 20.25 m between the south tower's top and the south building (model x −0.11..8.89, z 23.38..59.38; four 9 m pieces, Object_241/243/245/247, eight windows a side). The north row, z −40.10..−40.06, −0.05..1.50 m: x 0.31..2.44, 3.56..5.69, 8.31..10.44, 19.56..21.69, 24.31..26.44, 27.56..29.69, 32.31..34.44. The two stretches a body did not cross (#5151, #5152, the centre building's two doors at the top of their stairs, x 6.5..8.8, z −1.4 and 2.1: it is held 0.3 to 0.5 m in) are as they were. Wall pieces under 1.5 m tall are outside the scan; a wider run of it (0.5 m or more) lists 34 more short pieces that a body set beside them passes under — over the centre building's barred windows and beside its south stair, a stop sign's plate, the fire truck's ladder over its roof — and by the walks made none is a way in: at the barred windows, from either street, 0 of 246 starts end inside; from the strip beside the stair 0 of 96 end under its landing. They are as they were. The route grid rebuilt: 15,822 → 15,521 nodes, the 301 gone all behind the north row's and the south house's fronts, where a bot could route (the user's house had none); 675 → 672 jump links, no node new. Verified on the old data and the new. The sim's own walks at the three fronts, every 0.125 m along each from the street (walking, jumping, sprinting): 101, 110 and 101 of 509 starts ended behind a front before, none now; standing on the two awnings (311 starts, 0.30 to 0.70 m off the wall): 36 ended behind the wall before, none now; from the recess beside each front's end, 32 walks each, none ends inside the house. The scan again on the new data: the 2 held stretches, none crossed. In the PT site's built page (headless, offline, free pov, the forward key held from the street): at the user's window the unit went on to x −43.2, 6.3 m inside, and now stops at x −36.62, its radius off the front; the house's second window, a north-row window and a south-house window the same (x −42.7 → −36.62; z −46.1 → −39.77; z 33.31 → 31.51). Shared suite 200 of 200; no page errors. Here the data file is the PT site's byte for byte, and the route grid this tree's builder makes of it (`town_nav.nav`) equals the site's. The server reads the same two files at its warm-up (`server/src/warmup.js`), so an online match is closed with them. On this tree's build the unit stops at the three windows walked: the user's at x −36.62, a north-row window at z −39.77, a south-house window at z 31.51. No page errors; 200 of 200.

Now the low end steps over: x −8.94..−8.44 stays 6.135 m, x −8.44..−7.94 is 6.24 m, x −7.94..−7.44 6.35 m (0.36 / 0.46 / 0.57 m over the deck), each trimmed box solid from the deck up, floors and boxes split at x −7.94 / −7.44; from x −7.44 on the frame keeps its steps — the ramp itself stands more than a step over the deck there (`rampside/apply.mjs`). From the deck on either side a unit walks over the frame onto the ramp anywhere along x −8.8..−7.5; up the ramp from its foot as before (7.72 m mid-ramp, the 8.64 m walkway along the north frame). No standing place lost, 70 beside the frame freed, joint sweeps within 32 ms, the three suites and both trees' 185 tests pass, bot sight unchanged.

**The unit picker's folder: PROFILE, the weapon print over the tab** (b1105bn, 2026-11-06; user: "change the "UNIT FILE" text in unit selection menu to "PROFILE"" and "the weapon box is slightly covered a little on the left side by the file. Move the weapon box on top of the file so it won't be covered. the paper clip part should be untouched visually") — the folder's tab and the profile popup's tab read PROFILE. The weapon print's left edge sat under the tab: the tab is the folder's `::before` at z-index 2, and the card that holds the print turns (rotate 0.8°), so it is a layer of its own at the folder's base level and the print's own z-index could not lift it past the tab; the card now stands at z-index 3 (`.menu .picker-folder .picker-card`), the print and the clip it holds with it. Headless at 1280×720 and 600×310: the pixels that changed are the tab's word and the strip where tab and print meet; the clip's are identical.

**Hina's MG42: a 100-round drum, a 5 s reload** (b1105bo, 2026-11-06; user: "change Hina magazine size from 250 to 100, reload time from 7sec to 5sec. PT and 9.5.") — unit5's `magCapacity` 250 → 100 and `reloadMs` 7000 → 5000, in both copies of her numbers: the shared sim's (`shared/src/sim/constants.js`) and the client's own (`client/src/main.js` UNIT_DATA, which the picker card reads). Her `botFireCap` goes to 100 with the magazine: 250 meant "the whole drum per trigger pull", and an empty magazine already ends a bot's burst (the empty-mag branch of the bot's firing clears the count), so one pull still empties the drum — now ~4.8 s of fire at her 48 ms cadence where it was ~12 s. She still spends the reload in the Hide stance (5 s is over `BOT_RELOAD_HIDE_MIN_MS`, 3 s), and the reload animation, fitted to `reloadMs`, now plays over 5 s. Fire rate, damage, spread, bloom, HP and movement are as they were. The unit table, the fire-cap table and the reload-hide line carry the new numbers; Hina's hit-rate row is re-measured with `shared/tools/hitcurve.mjs` at 100 rounds (200 runs a series, as before; the other fifteen rows stay the 2026-10-24 measurement) and her panel in the chart is redrawn from it. The bot-vs-bot duels of the same day (Hina against each of the other sixteen on the seven maps, 10 a pairing a map: 1,120 duels a setting, the same seeds) put her 1v1 win rate at 89.4% with the 250-round drum and the 7 s reload, 70.4% with 100 rounds on the 7 s reload, and 77.4% with 100 rounds on the 5 s reload (95% interval 74.9–79.8%).

**The lobby's option rows in three groups, a red crayon line between them, the pills straight** (b1105bp, 2026-11-07; user: "make the menu order as "TEAMS>ROSTER>PLAY AS>RULES>PRACTICE"", "Slightly add a gap between "teams/roster" and "play as/rules" and "practice", three groups", "Add a red crayon dotted line between each group"; then, on the design canvas's round 10b, "Style G. implement in PT, make the options buttons untilted also" and "implement in 9.5 also") — the rows run Teams, Roster | Play as, Rules | Practice: 10 px apart inside a group, 22 px between groups, and in the middle of each group gap a red crayon line, style G: one unbroken stroke, a little wavy, thinner where it starts and ends, the memos' red (#E0382C) with the paper's grain streaked along it. `lobbyCrayonLine` (main.js) draws it as a filled outline on a 388 × 12 box that stretches to the column (332 px at the least, the whole width in portrait); the page's hidden SVG carries its wax, the `#crayon-line` filter, beside the memos' `#crayon`. The column grows from 230 to 254 px, still under the map memo's 275, so the folder, the memo and the buttons stay where they were; a short landscape phone zooms the grid as before (640 × 300: zoom 0.633 either way, nothing overflows), and a 390 × 844 portrait phone, where the lobby stacks, now scrolls 3 px. The option pills stand straight in the lobby (`.menu.lobby .mode-chip button { transform: none }`); the row labels keep their −2° and the menus elsewhere keep their tilt. Headless at 1280 × 720: the order, the 10 / 22 px gaps, no tilt on any pill, all ten option buttons still toggle, no page errors.

**The unit file's photo opens the full picture: a stack of instant photos with a binder clip** (b1105bq, 2026-11-07; user: "when user taps on the character profile image shown in the file, make it show a pop-up of the character's full image. in a stacked photo style"; then, on the design canvas's rounds 11 and 11b, "style A. But change the tape to smth stationery stuff; like a paper clip or smth" and "Type A2. implement in PT and 9.5") — in the unit picker a tap on the side card's photo band opens `showFullPicture`: over the dimmed picker, three instant photos stacked, two blank ones behind tilted −9° and 6°, and on the top one the unit's whole profile art (`units/<spriteKey>_profile.png`, the full body; the band shows the thumbnail's face crop) over its class colour, the name in marker on its foot with the weapon and class beside it, a black binder clip on the top edge. A tap anywhere closes it, the round × included. The band's tap stops there, so it neither confirms the pick nor reaches the menu's cancel, and a tap on the rest of the card still confirms. The stack is laid out at its design size, a 228 × 282 print in a 300 × 330 box, and scaled to the screen from 0.5 to 1.35 (844 × 390: 0.97, 1280 × 720: 1.35, 740 × 340: 0.82). Headless: the band opens it with the full picture loaded, the backdrop and the × close it with the preview kept, the card's name then confirms (2/3 to 3/3); the ground follows the class (SG yellow, MG magenta, SR ink); no page errors.

**Bots keep a shotgun at arm's length** (b1105br, 2026-11-07, PT first and then 9.5; user: "if there's a SG unit in play (on the field) on enemy site, all friendly unit locking on SG unit get +40 engage range", "Effect does not stack up or inherit", "Effect does not apply on SG units themselves", then "change the engage range to 110, if the engage range is already longer than 110, then ignore this rule" and "implement in 9.5") — the plain brain (nbrain.js `BOT_SHOTGUN_ENGAGE_RANGE`): a bot whose current target is an enemy shotgun fights on a band centred on 110, 103 to 117, outside the shotguns' 100 reach (`pelletRange`); a longer band of its own stays (the snipers' 120 in 1v1), a shotgun bot keeps its own, and in 2v2, where every band but the shotguns' is 70 or less, all go to 110. It is read off the target every tick, so it never adds up across two shotguns and never outlives the lock; it stands down in Sudden Death. Setting the constant to 0 turns it off exactly: checked on PT, the brain then plays tick for tick as the b1105b references, both equivalence tests. Assessed before the build on the bot-vs-bot round robin (16 units, seven maps, 3,360 duels, the same seeds): the shotguns' mean win rate 59.7% to 53.1% (Hoshino 65.4 to 56.0, Haruka 53.8 to 52.3, Rabu 59.9 to 51.1), against the rifles and assault rifles 69.8% to 54.6%, the machine guns 36.0% to 23.5%, the SMGs and the snipers as they were; duels against a shotgun 3 to 7 s longer, no timeouts; the brain's time a tick no longer (Neon about 0.103 to 0.098 ms a bot). An SMG walks as fast as a shotgun, so it seldom opens the gap: Atsuko on Hoshino spends 0 to 35% of the time beyond 100 depending on the map. Against the b1105b references one equivalence match with a shotgun parted, the other and the five without one played equal; the Sudden Death seven played equal; re-frozen at b1105br.

**The in-game Guide rewritten in plain words** (b1105bs, 2026-11-07, PT first and then 9.5; user: "Time to upgrade the in game Guide. Some yellow text needs their text color changed. Try to cover the info needed to play the game in easy plain words." and "implement in 9.5") — `showGuidePopup` now runs: how to win (HP, Sudden Death's 1 HP, Duel and Trio with the 3 s spawn protection), the main menu row by row, the controls (the phone's stick and buttons; on a PC W A S D or the arrows, J fire, K or Shift sprint, L or Q dodge, Space jump, U or E target, V Shooter or Command, I free camera), fighting (the auto lock and the red lock's homing, the spread that grows with held fire, the reload when empty, the hit slow, stamina, the sprint lock, the dodge's protection, cover), the six weapon types, and Command mode in shorter words. The yellow text, the double-tap sprint line written in #ffe14d on the white card, and the dodge line read in ink over a yellow highlighter stroke (style.css `.guide-hl`).

**The edge arrows' glint drawn on top of the triangle** (b1105dc, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Currently for the triangle edge indicator, the sniper glint(normal one and kei's) is shown beneath the triangle. Make them shown above the triangle.") — the glint layer inside both screen-edge arrows, the teammate's and each enemy's, gets `z-index:1`, so the plain flash and Kei's shard paint over the chevron instead of under it; one markup for offline and online. While a glint shows, its bright middle now covers most of the chevron.

**No shot sooner than the cooldown offline** (b1105df, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "by spamming sprinting or firing the unit can skip/shorten the cooldown. Found this on 9.5 Aru and Kei as well") — the offline fire gate (`fireGatePass`) stamped a shot at its scheduled 16 ms slot whenever it came less than a whole cooldown late, so the lateness went to the next shot: a sniper's next charge could start a moment after a charged or sprint-cancelled shot, a late tap let the next round follow at once. The slot is now kept only for a shot the gate refused within the last 50 ms (`FIRE_HELD_GAP_MS`: the trigger held through the cooldown); any other shot is stamped at its own tick. The server always stamped the real tick, so online play never had it.

**Iori, and Fubuki at 10 damage, in 9.5** (b1105dg, 2026-11-08, from PT b1105dd and b1105de; user: "Update iori unit and fubuki's 10 damage to 9.5.") — unit20 in both unit tables as PT has her: Fubuki's rifle with a 750 ms forced cooldown per shot (`firePerMinute` 80), a magazine of 5, 13 damage, a 2 s reload, walk 12, Fubuki's spread, bloom, trail and spindle; `forcedCooldown`: the snipers' between-shot ring on the fire button; UNIT_MODELS iori (models/iori.glb, her own motions, Saori's jump, Subaru's Move_Jump as her dodge, Normal_Attack_Delay across each cooldown, restarted by every shot: `rig.coolShot`); the user's three pictures, UNIT_FACE and WEAPON_BOX rows. The server takes her as any unit in the shared table. Fubuki's `damage` 13 to 10 in both tables.

**District's command mode: a closer opening, no fade** (b1105dy, 2026-11-09, PT and 9.5; user, shown that the opening command view was almost empty and that locking every object translucent would cost more, not less: "Make the initial camera closer. Turn off the transparent effect in District command mode.") — the command camera opens at the whole-map overview, 2.7 times the board's half-length off; District's board is so large (547 units half-length) that the overview stood the camera 1940 units from its centre and its far corner 2487 off, past command mode's far plane of 1600, so the match opened on an almost empty plate. `DIORAMA_MAP_VIEW.district.startDist` 700: the camera opens there, the whole city in view, its farthest corner 1493 units off; the zoom's range and the default yaw and tilt are unchanged. And GLB_MAPS `cmdNoFade` on District: in command mode nothing of it fades (`updateWallFade` treats no thing as blocking there, runs no occlusion test, and a faded thing eases back to solid); the shooter view fades as before, and every other map's command mode is unchanged. Measured before the change (headless, the city in view): every thing locked translucent drew 24–26% more and took 12–15% longer a frame than the fade.

The yellow token and its other uses (the shotgun class, the watched-chip ring, the guide) are unchanged.
