Implement in PT and 9.5 (online and offline)."; asked about online, where the colours are the server seats', the pick was "A. 線上不動，只改離線", online unchanged; asked whether the teammate's edge arrow should follow: "Yes, make edge triangle indicator's color matches the unit's slot color. This one should work for online") — offline, YOU is cyan #3EE0FF and ALLY lime #C6F24A, where YOU was lime and ALLY cyan: the HP chips (style.css `.hp-chip.health` / `.ally-health`), the command cards, markers and lines (main.js `DIO_SLOT_META`), the bars over the heads (they follow the palette since b1105dz) and the end page (the TEAM 1 flag and first row cyan, the second row lime). Online keeps its seat colours (p1 blue, p3 mint, p2 pink, p4 orange) on the chips, cards and bars, and its end page keeps YOU lime and ALLY cyan (`showEndPage` marks it `end-online`). And the screen-edge triangle that points to an off-screen teammate wears that unit's own colour, its chip's and its bar's — `dioSlotColor(roleOfMech(mate))`: offline ALLY's lime (spectating, the watched unit's partner's colour), online the teammate's seat colour; it was a fixed light cyan #7fe9ff. `roleOfMech` is the role test the bars used inline since b1105dz.

**The enemies' edge triangles in their own colours** (b1105eb, 2026-11-09, PT and 9.5, online and offline; user: "the edge triangle indicator should use according color for enemy units too.") — the screen-edge triangle that points to an off-screen enemy (the one not locked; every live enemy while the viewer climbs a ladder) wears that unit's own colour, its chip's and its bar's, as the teammate's has since b1105ea: `dioSlotColor(roleOfMech(f))` per arrow, re-read each frame because the lock decides which enemy an arrow rides. Offline ENEMY magenta #FF3EA5 and ENEMY 2 orange #FF8A2A (spectating an enemy: YOU's cyan and ALLY's lime); online the enemy's seat colour (for p1 and p3, p2 pink and p4 orange). It was a fixed red-orange, #ff6a2c.

**Saori's line-up picture, a new crop** (b1105ec, 2026-11-09, PT and 9.5; user, from the Line-up Card Crop tool: "Saori card crop, saori_profile.png 531x531: x 171, y 118, w 251, h 156. Change Saori 1V1 photo preview with this. All other layouts and photos stay the same.") — units/saori_card.png, the 1v1 lobby line-up card's picture of her (main.js UNIT_CARD_ART), is now that 251 x 156 cut of her profile art as it is, in place of b1105dw's 339 x 210 one: the same shape as the card's 190 x 118 picture box, her picture 1.35 times the size b1105dw's cut gave it. Nothing else changes: the box, the card, her other pictures, the other units' cards.

**Faster rounds, doubled lock ranges, no 2v2 lock ranges, the shotguns' rounds at 500; Minori's damage 5** (b1105eg, 2026-11-09, 9.6, online and offline; from PT's b1105ed to b1105ef; user: "Change all AR/SMG/MG/Rifle bullet speed to 1100", "remove all 2V2 lock range settings. multiply all existing LR settings by 2 times. Change Shotgun bullet speed to 500", "change SG pellet effective range from 150 back to 100", then "change minori damage from 4.5 to 5. Implement these changes to 9.6, offline and online" — these changes being PT's latest with the damage, and PT too) — in the shared table (the server's sim, online) and the client's (offline) alike: `projectileSpeed` 1100 for the fifteen units whose rounds flew at 600 (the four ARs, the five SMGs, the three MGs, the three rifles); every unit's `lockRange` twice what it was (the ARs and rifles 112, the SMGs 100 and Kurumi 80, the MGs 160, the shotguns 80, the snipers 240) and `lockRange2v2` gone from all twenty units that had it, so 2v2 bots centre their range band on `lockRange` as 1v1 bots do; the shotguns' `projectileSpeed` 500 (350), their `pelletRange` 100 as it was; Minori's `damage` 5 (4.5 since b1105dt). The picker's range pips doubled their steps, so each unit shows the pips it showed. Why the rounds: homing being off, a round is aimed where its target is when fired, and at 600 a sprinting target had moved past the body's half-width by about 33 units, so a tight cone always missed it beyond that and a wide one hit more; at 1100 the order follows the cones out to about 80 (the user's sims of 2026-11-09: a sprinting target at 50, Saori 3% at 600 and 77% at 1100, Minori 31% and 66%). The bot sims of the same day on PT's build (1v1 round robins on Plain Field, Factory and Compound): fights about twice as far apart, the low-bloom guns up and the high-bloom ones down, the snipers and rifles up, the shotguns down to a quarter of their fights.

**The spindle 1.5 times as thick and 1.8 times as long** (b1105ek, 2026-11-09, 9.6, offline and online; from PT's b1105eh to b1105ek; user: "粗 2 倍、長 1.8 倍. Apply to PT first.", then "2 倍粗 is a bit too much. change it to 1.5 times instead", "change 1.8 倍長 to 1.5 times as well", then "revert the length from 1.5 times to 1.8 times. Implement in 9.6 as well, offline and online") — the one spindle every single-round gun flies (the autos, the rifles and the snipers; the shotgun pellets and Aris's bolt keep their own): `SNIPER_TRACER_MID_RADIUS` 0.225 (0.15), `SNIPER_TRACER_LENGTH` 36.72 (20.4). Visual only, and the same offline and online: both draw it with buildProjectileMesh, and the hit test, the client's offline one and the server's alike, sweeps the round's point (the spindle's front tip) along its path, never the mesh. It still grows out of the muzzle, its length the distance flown up to the full 36.72, so at 1100 u/s its tail leaves the muzzle after 33 ms, as a 600 u/s round's did with 20.4. Why: with the rounds at 1100 and the fights twice as far (b1105eg), an AR round seen from the chase camera at 1920 x 1080 stood out for one 60 fps frame instead of three and was about 5 pixels just before the hit; at this size it shows about 253 pixels instead of 104 in the frame after it clears the shooter's own body, and about 15 just before the hit.

**Hina's spread cap 0.20** (b1105er, 2026-11-09, 9.6, offline and online; from PT's b1105el to b1105er, which tried 0.30 with other bloom settings and settled on this cap with her own; user: "Implement Hina's SA cap 0.2 in 9.6 as well") — unit5's `bloomCap` 0.20 (0.14) in the shared table (the server's sim, online) and the client's (offline); her base cone 0.04, her 0.003 a round and her recovery of 0.03 a second 200 ms behind her last round as they were. Firing on the move her MG42's cone now opens on to 0.20, reached after 54 rounds (2.6 s) where 0.14 came after 34; the sure-hit distance at the cap 16 instead of 23. A Hina standing still fires without bloom by the game's rule, so a still MG42 is as it was; her spray picture on the picker is unchanged (its 26 rounds stop inside the frame before either cap).

**The machine guns' lock range 126, the marksman rifles' 160** (b1105es, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: "1. change Maki/Hina/Koyuki Lockrange to 126 2. change Iori/Aris/Fubuki Lockrange to 160 3. Implement in PT and 9.6 (online and offline).") — `lockRange` 126 for Koyuki, Hina and Maki (unit12, unit5, unit21; 160 since b1105eg) and 160 for Fubuki, Aris and Iori (unit10, unit7, unit20; 112 since b1105eg), in the shared table (the server's sim, online) and the client's (offline). The lock range steers the bots: their band, lock range ± 7, is 119–133 for the machine guns (153–167 until now) and 153–167 for the marksman rifles (105–119), and the firing-position search's reach, 2.5 times the band's upper edge, moves with it (333 and 418 u); a bot against a target that stands still on an open field holds at 119–126 with a machine gun and at 160 with a rifle. The RANGE pips on the picker's side card follow the lock range: four for the rifles (three until now), four still for the machine guns. Unchanged: the band against a shotgun (at least 110, which both classes pass), Sudden Death (its reach is the cone's), the red-lock flag (it only feeds the homing, which is off), the cones and the bot fire gate: the rifles' gate line, their base cone's sure-hit line, is 160, so a rifle bot still fires every round from a fully recovered cone, past the line as its one-round suppress burst. The six were re-measured for the Units chapter's hit-rate table (still / walking / sprinting target, mean over a magazine): Koyuki 31 / 29 / 25 at 126 (24 / 22 / 18 at 160), Hina 22 / 21 / 19 (16 / 16 / 14), Maki 55 / 51 / 35 (44 / 41 / 29); Fubuki 100 / 46 / 1 at 160 (100 / 75 / 15 at 112), Aris 100 / 63 / 5 (100 / 93 / 37), Iori 100 / 44 / 1 (100 / 74 / 14) — at 160 a round flies 0.15 s, in which a walking target moves about the capsule's half-width and a sprinter twice that. And the harness's shot count is fixed (shared/tools/hitcurve.mjs): it counted a round when the magazine went down, and Aris's 1.2 s refill lands on every other shot of her ~0.6 s gate rhythm, so the count missed those shots and gave their bolts to the round before (her b1105er row read 95 / 39 on the walking and sprinting targets for 93 / 37) and, past her gate line, never spent her one-round suppress burst, so her next round left bloomed; a gun that refills a round at a time now also counts a round when a projectile leaves without the count going down. The other nineteen were never affected: none missed a shot in a check of 20 runs a series.

**The lock-range table; the shotguns' reach 150; the band against a shotgun at 160** (b1105et, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: a table of lock ranges (assault rifles 110, SMGs 100, Kurumi 80, shotguns 80, machine guns 120, rifles 135, Aru and Hiyori 200, Kei 250), then "Also, change shotgun pellet effective range from 100 to 150. Change the range gating for SG encounter rule from 110 to 160. Implement in PT and 9.6.") — `lockRange` in the shared table (the server's sim, online) and the client's (offline): the assault rifles 110 (112), the machine guns 120 (126), the marksman rifles 135 (160), Aru and Hiyori 200 (240), Kei 250 (240); the SMGs 100, Kurumi and the shotguns 80 as they were. The bots' bands, lock range ± 7, follow: 103–117, 113–127, 128–142, 193–207 and 243–257. The shotguns' `pelletRange` 150 (100): a volley flies on to 150 before it dies, and a shotgun bot, which holds fire on a target beyond its reach, now opens fire from 150 (shared/test/pellet_range.test.js reads the new reach and holds its target at 220). `BOT_SHOTGUN_ENGAGE_RANGE` 160 (110): a bot whose target is an enemy shotgun centres its band on at least 160, so every class but the snipers fights a shotgun at 153–167; a shotgun bot keeps its own band and Sudden Death is untouched. The picker's RANGE pips: the machine guns three (four), the rest as they were. Kei's beam reaches 400, past her 257. The Units chapter's hit-rate table and chart were measured before this build and keep the ranges they were measured at.

**Dust: the wall by the ramp blocks rounds** (b1105eu, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user, with a picture of the stone wall beside the ramp: "This wall by the ramp is not blocking bullets correctly. Fix in PT and 9.6.") — client/public/maps/dust_data.json: the five boxes along the ramp's west edge (x 61.4, z 204.4 to 278.4: #2998 the wall, #3171 its run on, #3163, #3303 and #3321 the pillar and the buildings beside it) began at y 0, while the ramp beside them falls from -2.34 to -20.1: a round fired from the ramp at a target behind the wall flew under the box, through the drawn wall. Each now reaches down to the ramp's lowest point along it, less 0.1 (-12.74, -13.36, -18.66, -19.6, -20.22); their tops are as they were, and no floor lies under any of them. The nav grid rebuilt. Checked with straight lines from the ramp to targets behind the wall: clear before, stopped by the wall now.

**Dust: the rubble by the yard's broken rim laid flat** (b1105ev, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user, of three circled patches on a box view of the raised yard: "Ignore very little objects without giving them cboxes ... i want those little cboxes in the image removed anyway") — client/public/maps/dust_data.json: twelve small floors the generator laid on the loose bricks by the yard's broken south rim (model x about 59.6 to 62.4, z -28.7 to -25.4), none over 3 units across, stood 0.4 to 0.8 units over the ground around them: four inside the yard (#1600, #1604, #1606, #2919, over its 12.12) and eight outside on the street (#2928 to #2955, over its 1.4). The nav grid rebuilt: 4041 walkable nodes, 659 jump links and 387 drop links, as before.

**Dust: the ramp and the wall's top as simple slopes; the cage crate as one box** (b1105ew, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user, with pictures of the ramp east of the raised yard: "The ramp floor and the tilt on the wall should be simple slopes. Unit should be able to walk on wall edge without getting blocked." and "the crate cboxes are too complicated; a simple square there would do.") — client/public/maps/dust_data.json: the ramp (arena x 60.4 to 87.4, z -38.6 to -5.6, falling from 13.9 to 3.8) was some 55 one-unit strips of slopes over a staircase of row and column boxes that left a hollow under most of it; it is now two planes of one slope (gx 0.0046, gz -0.3065, within 0.12 of the old corners) over eleven solid steps that stay under the slope. The wall's top beside it (x 59.4 to 60.4, z -38.6 to -24.6) was fourteen steep one-unit planes, a bevel from the yard's lip down to the ramp, over stepped boxes; it is now one plane from 14.32 to 13.52 on two boxes, so the wall meets the ramp with a plain face and a body walks the ramp along it, or the wall's top, without catching. The cage crate against the wall (x 61.4 to 70.21, z 2.55 to 12.4) was thirteen boxes and thirteen lids; it is one box to 10.93 with one lid. 81 boxes and 81 floors became 15 and 4. The nav grid rebuilt: 4041 walkable nodes and 387 drop links as before, 675 jump links (659).

**Dust, simple collision map-wide: slopes, things, rubble, hanging slabs; the yard rim's corner as one slope** (b1105ex, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: "照今天斜坡和箱子的做法，把整張 Dust 一區一區對照外觀改成簡單的碰撞方塊", then, of two pass-through slabs at the yard rim's corner, "I don't understand why these two have to be there" and "here where i stand should be a slope on the wall edge") — shared/tools/maps/dust_clean.py, run once over client/public/maps/dust_data.json, finds three patterns the generator left and rebuilds them as b1105ew rebuilt the ramp and the cage crate: (A) a slope cut into strips, 26 of them, 411 strips, is one plane per rectangle of its own cells, fitted within 0.2, 308 planes, and its staircase of boxes gives way to solid steps under the slope where they are fewer (13 staircases kept); a slope with a floor under it keeps its boxes; (B) a thing made of many boxes standing on the ground, 9 of them, is its outline as one box or a body and a top as Town's cars, 29 boxes to 10, only where the outline fills 75% of itself and leaves no body-sized gap; (C) rubble, 99 small floors standing 0.2 to 1 unit over the ground around them with no box under them, is laid flat at that ground. And the 33 thin pass-through (noProjectile) slabs that hang 6 units and more over the floor below them, a sign's or a frame's reading that stopped a body at a wall's edge or in a jump, are gone, the two at the yard rim's corner among them. The corner itself (arena x 58.4 to 61.4, z -24.6 to -16.6), eight boxes of four heights, four of them pass-through, is now the wall's top as one slope from 13.52 up to 16.0 on five steps, where the model's top rises from the low rim to the tall one: a body walks the wall's edge up it. Boxes 4551 to 4498, floors 3069 to 2969. The nav grid rebuilt: 4059 walkable nodes (4041), 667 jump links, 415 drop links (387).

**Dust: the yard's east wall as one parapet one walks along** (b1105ey, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user, stuck on the wall at arena x 59.5 z -10.8: "stuck. unit should be able to walk on wall edge. This shouldn't be that hard. Look at the visual from multiple angle, do not just use numbers.") — seen from the ramp, from along its top and from the yard, the model's wall is one rounded parapet about a metre thick, its top level at about 16.8, rising from the low rim at its south corner; the reading had left a pit in its top (z -14.6 to -9.6, down to the yard's 12.12, walled on four sides by the wall's own pieces), slivers on its inner face standing on the yard floor, and steps. client/public/maps/dust_data.json: nineteen boxes and ten floors between x 57.4 and 61.4, z -24.6 to 18.4, are now the corner as one slope from 13.52 up to 16.8 on five steps (it rose to 16.0 since b1105ex) and the straight wall as one box, x 57.6 to 61.4, to 16.8, with one level lid to the south corner. The nav grid rebuilt: 4063 walkable nodes, 671 jump links, 417 drop links.

**Dust: the yard's south rim, its break as slopes** (b1105ez, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6; user: "moving on. rework the whole Dust map. Some other parts of the same wall have issue too, don't miss them.", then "update in PT only.") — seen from the street, from above, along it and from the yard, the south rim is one rounded wall about 0.65 m thick, its top about 16.8 at both ends and broken down in its west half in a U to just over the yard (12.92), bricks on it. client/public/maps/dust_data.json: the rim's 26 boxes and 17 floors between arena x 29.4 and 57.6, z 15.4 to 17.96 (pieces of a dozen heights, pass-through slivers on its street face, small slopes in the break) are now the west end and the east end as one box each to 16.8 with a level lid, and the break as a slope down (x 33.4 to 39.4), a floor (to 43.4) and a slope up (to 48.4) on steps two units long; the east end meets b1105ey's east wall at its corner. The rubble boxes left on the yard beside the break by b1105ev (it laid only their floors flat; the boxes still stood 0.4 over the yard) are gone. The north side, a retaining wall whose top is the higher ground, has no gap; the west side is a building's wall. The nav grid rebuilt: 4063 walkable nodes, 671 jump links, 417 drop links.

**Dust: wall tops one walks along, the stairs as slopes** (b1105fa, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6; user, of the yard's west wall: "these two make the unit falls. Moving on, expand the fix scale.", then "make stairs slopes") — the east yard's west wall (arena x 27.4 to 30.4, z 3.4 to 15.4; #1524) had no floor on its top: a body walking onto it off the south rim fell through it and was pushed off (b1105ez's entry called that side a building's wall; it is this wall, its crown 16.56 in the model). It is one box with a level lid, the south-west corner one box to the rim's 16.8 (the 0.4 m sliver #1615 gone). A scan for the same fault map-wide (a standable box with no floor on its top, beside a floor that play reaches by walking, jumping or dropping, with a clear walk onto it) found 36 more; the batch covers them all. The model has two staircases of four steps and more besides the south-east one already a slope: the west stair (x -35.2 to -7.6, 17 steps from the west yard's raised top step 13.03 to the street -4.94) and the north-west stair (x -63.6 to -49, 12 steps from 12.12 to -0.59), each now one plane over steps two units long (the west stair's block with its walls had been 104 boxes and 89 floors, the north-west stair's 48 and 57). The walls beside them are rebuilt from the model's top surface (sampled every 0.1 m): the west stair's north wall, which runs on east to the east yard (16.4, a break down to 12.5 over the stair, then its top falling to 15.73 and rising to 16.56; slabs of 15.9 to 16.8 had stood in three strips), and its south parapet falling with the steps (13.09 to -3.12); the west yard's east wall, the mirror of the east yard's south rim (15.8, a break to 12.7 between z 24.5 and 33, 16.15 to its north end; the arched door in its street face keeps its recess, the walls the reading stood over it to 20 and 40 gone); the north-west stair's east parapet (12.35 to 1.98), the invisible slivers along its outer face gone. Also: the wooden crate on the east yard one box with a lid (its middle had none), the walkway edge over the yard's north side given lids and closed to the yard (a 0.23-unit crack between them), lids on the low crate by the east yard's west wall and on the south-east walkway's edges, and the pocket between the crate stack and that wall (the reading had filled it with two boxes to 16.4) filled to the crates' top, 10. A body walked along every rebuilt top and both stairs with the sim's ground and wall rules: before, it fell off the west wall at its corner and stuck in it, dropped a step at each stair's step and was stopped along the north wall and the west yard's wall; after, no fall and no stop. Boxes 4468 to 4335, floors 2949 to 2796; validate: spawns unchanged, 1.2% of 3000 standing points inside a box (old step strips and wall-top bevels under the new boxes), sight lines 1 newly open and 1 newly blocked of 600. The nav grid rebuilt: 4075 walkable nodes, 622 jump links (the stairs walked now), 417 drop links.

**Dust: the boxes outside play gone, four spots that blocked the way opened** (b1105fb, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6; user, of two walls by the west stair: "blocking the way."; at the south-east walkway: "The cboxes are way too much for a thin pole."; of two pillars: "blocking the way when there's nothing there."; then "there seem to be a huge amount of unnecessary cboxes outside the map.") — outside play, 2426 boxes and 1202 floors are gone: four runs (the bots' reach from the spawns, and a reach widened by every jump a wall does not stop; two seeds each, 2,000,000 shot lines a run) found no body that could touch them (6 units across, -3 to +12 up) and no line between two reached places on which they are the first thing hit; a floor likewise (stood on). Where such a box held a body at the street, the sim's default street (0) would have lain bare and open, so 113 flat plugs from the street to 0.5, with no top, cover exactly those places (the old footprints' own edges; they may also cover the inside of a box still standing, where nobody stands) and nothing else: a place bare before stays bare, and a body deeper than a step under the street is held at the edge by the void rule as before. The plugs reach the old data's outer edge, so the nav grid's cells stay where they were. The two walls with no top over the west stair's north wall (#3787, #3788: to 20 and 16, 0.45 into the stair) are gone; b1105fa's stair rebuild kept every wall with no top and should have taken them. The south-east walkway's pole (arena x 94.83 to 96.91, z 199.07 to 201.25, the model's 0.52 by 0.55 m post) is one box from the street to its crossbars, where 13 stood (five round it, six columns from the walkway to 42 under its crossbars and wires); the walkway's start (x 98.4 to 103.4, z 198.4 to 203.4), which had no top of its own, is one box to 7.19 with a lid, and the street round the post, which had no floor (the boxes stood on it), a floor sloping -1.44 to -1.05 as its neighbours do. The five pillars by the south walkway (x 56.4 to 61.4, z 236.4 to 241.4, to 37.3 to 37.7 under wires 9.3 m up, nothing else of the model there) are gone, and the raised pavement they stood on (x 56.4 to 61.4, z 204.4 to 255.4), whose boxes had no floor (a body stood at the street's 0 inside it), has one at 1.59. At the walkway's far end the columns on its last two units are gone, and the building's face along the whole walkway (x 61.4 to 103.4) stands where the model has it, one straight line at z 258.15, where the reading had it 0.75 out at 257.4, the walkway's floor carried to it. Inside play, four boxes where the model has nothing (#1912, #2476, #2786, #3676) are gone; #1695 stays (the north-west yard's floor stops a unit short of its wall there, and without it a body would drop into the gap). Of the 2643 nav nodes the spawns reached before, 2628 are reached unchanged; the others moved by a cell beside a change, and new are the pavement at 1.59 (it was 0), the walkway's start, the stair's north edge, the street round the post and the alley west of the building at x -30 (always walkable; the grid had dropped it as sealed under the high boxes over it). Links between reached nodes 685 to 703, all at the changes. A body walked with the sim's ground and wall rules along the north wall's top, the stair's north edge, round the post, the walkway to the post, the pavement and along the building's face: before, stopped at each; after, through, stopping at the post and at the face. Bare tops reachable: none. Sight lines between reached places, 20000: 57 newly open (through the pillars and columns removed), 3 newly blocked (by the post's foot at the street, where the old boxes started at 0). Boxes 4335 to 2004 (113 of them plugs), floors 2796 to 1600; the nav grid rebuilt: 2887 walkable nodes (the outside rooftops' gone), 361 jump links, 183 drop links, 0.19 MB from 0.23.

**Dust: the stones on a building's corner gone, the model only** (b1105fc, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6; user, standing at model x 62.99 z 6.73: "Nothing wrong with the cboxes. But i don't like those brick visual at the corner. can you remove those bricks visual only?") — the eight quoin stones standing out of the south-east corner of the building just north of that spot (model x 62.96 to 63.88, z 4.49 to 5.15, 0.73 to 4.59 m over the street; each a box of 12 triangles in the merged mesh Object_15) are cut out of client/public/maps/dust.glb (dust.wasm on the PT site, the same file): shared/tools/maps/glb_delete_boxes.mjs with a box round each stone (each took its 12 triangles and nothing else), then glb_strip.mjs --keep-normals to drop the old index list (run on the old file it gives the same data back). 96 triangles and 192 vertices fewer, 4,333,448 to 4,326,728 bytes; every other triangle, texture and material is the same. The wall behind the stones is whole on both faces, seen from four sides and in the game. A search of the whole model for stacks of such stones found no other corner. The collision data is not touched.

**Aru and Hiyori at 4500, the rifles at 2000** (b1105fd, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: "Change Aru/Hiyori bullet speed from 4000 to 4500. Change Iori/Aris/Fubuki bullet speed from 1100 to 2000.") — `projectileSpeed` in the sim's unit table (shared/src/sim/constants.js, online) and the client's (client/src/main.js, offline): the two sniper rifles, unit3 (Aru) and unit19 (Hiyori), 4000 to 4500 (72 units a tick: their 200 lock range crossed in under three ticks, 0.044 s); the three rifles, unit7 (Aris), unit10 (Fubuki) and unit20 (Iori), 1100 to 2000 (32 units a tick: the 135 lock range in 0.07 s, where it took 0.12 s). Kei's sniper (unit6) stays at 2000, the AR, SMG and MG rounds at 1100 and the shotguns at 500. Rounds live 2.2 s as before, so a missed rifle round now flies 4400 units, a sniper's 9900, until something stops it.

**Hina gates on the autos' 33% line** (b1105fe, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: "Give Hina the same 33% hit rate gating other auto-weapons have. That would make Koyuki and Maki the only two auto-weapons that don't use the 33% hit rate gating, correct?") — Hina (unit5) loses her `botGateWidth: 0` in the sim's unit table (shared/src/sim/constants.js, online) and the client's (client/src/main.js, offline), so her bot rides the default `BOT_GATE_WIDTH_AUTO` (8.84 ÷ cone, the distance where her current cone still lands one round in three on a standing target), as the assault rifles and the submachine guns do: past that line it holds until the cone has recovered, then fires a suppressing burst. Among the auto weapons only Koyuki (unit12) and Maki (unit21) are ungated now (`botGateWidth: 0`); the marksman rifles keep the sure-hit line (3.2), and the shotguns and sniper rifles are never gated. shared/test/bloom.test.js: the ungated test is Koyuki and Maki's; a new one holds Hina to the default line.

**The hit X: a red X pops on what your rounds hit** (b1105ff, b1105fg, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: "Whenever a unit's own bullet hits the target, a small thin X crosshair shortly appears around the target, within the lock icon. The X crosshair can be overwritten for high RPM weapons", then "use white", "shown in spectating mode, no showing in command mode", "move the X crosshair a bit more outside, leaving a bit more space in the middle. change x crosshair color to red", sample G and free pov's option 2 picked, then "For high RPM weapons, i want an fast over-writing pop-of of the XC", "kinda like the already-had blue effect when player unit got hit", the ring's 0.18 s picked, for every weapon and for free pov's X too) — client/src/main.js: when the viewed unit's own round (the player's, or the unit the camera watches) hits an enemy, locked or not, a red X (#e61b1b, the user's mockup's red: four thin strokes with a dark rim, 16 to 40 of the bracket's 192 canvas) appears on that enemy where the bracket sits, at the bracket's base size (its bloom growth left out, so the X stays on the target while the bracket swells with a spray). Each hit restarts it at that size and full colour; over 0.18 s, the life of `spawnHitEffect`'s ring, it grows to 1.5 times (fast, then slowing) while it fades out evenly, and the next hit overwrites it, so a high-RPM weapon pops it at its rate of fire (1250 RPM: every 48 ms). One X per enemy. In free pov, which has no bracket, the X pops round the crosshair instead, 4 px out from the crosshair's lines. Command mode shows none. Offline the four hit sites (the round, the volley, the beam, the charged sweep) call `noteHitX`; online the snapshots' hit events, which the server already sent, are queued in client/src/online/connection.js (`drainHits`), so the events of a snapshot the render loop skips are no longer lost, and the X follows the server's confirmation. No server or sim change: nothing new on the wire. Tunables: `HIT_X_COLOR`, `HIT_X_LIFE_MS`, `HIT_X_POP_SCALE`, `hitXPop`.

**Iori, Aris and Fubuki at 1500** (b1105fh, 2026-11-10, PT and 9.6, online and offline; user: "reduce Iori/Aris/Fubuki bullet speed from 2000 to 1500.") — `projectileSpeed` in the sim's unit table (shared/src/sim/constants.js, online) and the client's (client/src/main.js, offline): the three rifles, unit7 (Aris), unit10 (Fubuki) and unit20 (Iori), 2000 to 1500 (24 units a tick: their 135 lock range crossed in 0.09 s, where it took 0.07 s since b1105fd; 1100 before that). Kei's sniper (unit6) stays at 2000, Aru and Hiyori at 4500, the AR, SMG and MG rounds at 1100 and the shotguns at 500. Rounds live 2.2 s as before, so a missed rifle round now flies 3300 units.

**Every base spread 0 but Aris's** (b1105fw, 2026-11-11, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user, after the hit-rate run with every spread at 0: "Change every units accuracy from 0.02 to 0. Except Aris, Aris accuracy stays the same", then "Even for those who are not 0.02, change theirs to 0 as well") — `spreadAngle`, the base cone (the *Base SA* column), is 0 for every unit in the sim's unit table (shared/src/sim/constants.js, online) and the client's (client/src/main.js, offline): the 0.02 guns, Atsuko's, Yuuka's and Kurumi's 0.03, Hina's and the hidden Mika's 0.04, Marina's 0.06 and the shotguns' 16°; Aris (unit7) keeps her 0.02. The bloom per shot, the caps and the recovery are as they were, so a fresh trigger pull, or a cone recovered, puts its round on the aim line, and a spray opens from 0 to the same cap: the whole cap is bloom now, and a sure-hit base reads ∞. What follows from it: the bot fire gate's line at a recovered cone has no end, so an auto pushed past its capped line still holds until its cone has recovered, but none fires a suppress burst any more (Aris, her base kept, still fires her one round past 160); the gated guns' rhythms move with the cone — at their lock ranges Minori fires six rounds between holds of about 0.75 s (five and 0.66 s before), Marina thirty and 2.0 s (ten and 0.82 s), Hina twenty-five and 2.7 s (twelve and 1.4 s) — and Fubuki with a target at 56 u fires in pairs: her second round's cone, 0.043, still lands every round there (its line at 74), where the 0.063 of the 0.02 base did not (51); a Sudden Death bot that carries no `sdReach` reads its reach as 200 where its base cone set it (Atsuko, Yuuka and Kurumi 107, Marina 53, Hina 80, Fubuki, Iori, Aru, Kei and Hiyori 160), while Aris stays at 160, the shotguns at their lock range and the six with an `sdReach` at 80; a shotgun volley's centre no longer wanders off the aim point (it jittered by up to 0.08 of the spread, 0.64° at 16°); and the free pov crosshair of a recovered cone draws at its 12 px floor. Measured again (the Units chapter's hit-rate table and chart): a standing or a walking target takes more rounds from every automatic (Saori 77 / 61 then, 90 / 73 now; Marina 54 / 49, 82 / 67; Hina 60 / 57, 80 / 75), Aru and Hiyori land every round, and Fubuki and Iori every round on a walker; a sprinter takes fewer from most automatics (Saori 31 then, 20 now) and almost none from Fubuki and Iori (37 then, 1 now): a round aimed where the target is misses a body that moves more than the capsule's half-width during its flight, and only a cone wide enough to throw some rounds ahead of the body landed those. Tests: the bloom and gate tests carry the new numbers (Aris carries the suppress-burst cases now), a new one pins every base cone, and the shotgun reach test stands its shooter at x −100: from the spawn the boundary stood 144 u off, short of the 150 reach, and the volley had passed it only on its centre's jitter.

**Bloom in every firing condition: standing still no longer keeps a gun pin-point** (b1105fx, 2026-11-11, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user: "Currently, when unit fires while standing still, the bloom wouldn't take effect. Make bloom take effect in any firing condition.") — every shot adds its unit's `bloomPerShot`, standing, walking or sprinting, and the recovery waits out `bloomRecoverDelayMs` after the last shot in every case. Until now a fighter that had stood on the ground at under 1 u/s for 200 ms (`BLOOM_STILL_SPEED`, `BLOOM_STILL_DWELL_MS`) added no bloom per shot and recovered with no delay, even mid-burst (owner 2026-09-21; a sprint, even in place, never counted as standing since 2026-11-03), so a planted shooter kept its base cone through a whole magazine. The rule's machinery had no other reader and went with it: the `stillSince` stamp and `tickStillness` / `isStill` (shared/src/sim/bloom.js, tick.js, state.js, index.js), the shot's bloom skip and the no-delay recovery in the sim (projectiles.js, actions.js: online, and the client's prediction) and in the offline client (client/src/main.js: both fire paths, `tickBloomOffline`, the per-frame stamp). What follows from it: a player or a bot standing still while it sprays opens its cone exactly as when moving — Saori's M4 reaches its 0.06 cap with the magazine's last round, Hina's MG42 her 0.20 at the 68th — so standing still buys the gun nothing any more, and the bots' fire gates (the autos' 33% line, the marksman rifles' 100%) close on a standing shooter as on a moving one. The hit-rate measurement is untouched: its shooter strafes, so every round bloomed already. Tests: bloom.test.js's standing-still test is turned round (a standing shooter blooms shot for shot as a walking one, nothing drains inside the 200 ms after the last shot, then the bloom drains as fast as after a walk), and the sprint test's last part has the standing shots keep adding one bloom each; both fail against the old rule.

**Hina's cone: cap 0.16, recovery 0.06/s** (b1105fz, 2026-11-11, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user: "change hina bloom to 0.16 CAP, recovery 0.06/s, 200 ms. implement in PT and 9.7. Update 9.7 readme (including hit rate charts).") — unit5's `bloomCap` from 0.20 to 0.16 and `bloomRecoverPerSec` from 0.03 to 0.06, in the sim's table (online) and the client's (offline); her 0.003 a round, her 0 base and the 200 ms delay as they were (PT tried 0.17 and 0.05 the same day, as b1105fy). Her spray reaches the cap at the 55th round, 2.6 s in (the 0.20 came at the 68th, 3.2 s), its sure-hit line 20 u (16 at 0.20), and the cap drains to 0 in 2.7 s after the delay (6.7 s at 0.03/s). Her bot at its 120 lock still fires in bursts of 25 rounds from cones of 0 to 0.072 — its cone never reaches the cap behind the 33% gate — so its hit rate stays 80 / 75 / 34 (re-measured, the Units chapter's table and chart), but each hold now lasts 1.5 s where it lasted 2.7 s, and its magazine empties in 9.0 s instead of 12.8 s. Measured on the hit-rate field over 120 s with the trigger held and the 5 s reloads (200 runs a series): a 100 HP target standing goes down in 2.9 s where it took 4.2, a sprinting one in 6.3 s where it took 8.9, and her long-run damage rises from 17.8 to 22.7 a second on a standing target, 16.8 to 21.2 on a walking one and 7.8 to 9.9 on a sprinter. With the gate off, as the gun would fire with no bot discipline, the magazine's tail now leaves at the 0.16 cap and the drum's cone is fully back before the 5 s reload ends (at 0.03/s it still carried about 0.06 into the next drum), so her ungated long-run damage rises from 8.0 to 14.7 a second standing — still under the gated 22.7.

**Hina: a 150-round drum, her own 20% bot gate** (b1105ga, 2026-11-11, PT and 9.7, online and offline; user: "1. change hina mag size from 100 to 150 2. change hina hit rate gating from 33% to 20% 3. Implement in PT and 9.7.") — unit5's `magCapacity` from 100 to 150 and her `botFireCap` with it (every auto's bot empties its whole magazine in one pull), and a gate line of her own, `botGateWidth: 11.42`: her bot fires while her current cone still lands one round in five on a standing target (the 3.2 × 6.4 capsule a fifth of the cone's disc at radius 5.71), where the autos' 8.84 line asks one in three; both unit tables (the sim's, online; the client's, offline). The drum fires for 7.2 s (4.8 s at 100) and its sustained DPS, the reload counted, rises from 41.0 to 49.4. At her 120 lock the 20% line passes her at a 0.095 cone, so her bot fires bursts of thirty-two rounds from cones of 0 to 0.093 (twenty-five to 0.072 on the 33% line) with holds of 1.8 s (1.5 s), the drum in 14.2 s; its hit rate falls to 73 / 69 / 35 (80 / 75 / 34), the wider line spending more rounds from wider cones. Measured over 120 s on the hit-rate field (200 runs a series, the trigger held, the 5 s reloads): its long-run damage stays where it was on a standing and a walking target (22.6 and 21.4 a second; 22.7 and 21.2 before) and rises on a sprinter (10.8; 9.9), each drum dealing more (436 on a standing target; 319); but a 100 HP target goes down later (standing 3.4 s where it took 2.9, sprinting 7.1 where it took 6.3) — the first burst's seven extra rounds leave it a few points short, and the longer hold delays the next — while a 120 HP one sprinting goes down sooner (7.7 s; 8.6). Tests: bloom.test.js's Hina gate test rides her own line (the width, its fifth-of-the-disc geometry, the 0.095 cone at 120, the hold far past it) and checks the drum and the fire cap.

**A bot under its enemy's floor: the route cut stands on the floor the route walks** (b1105ge, PT 2026-11-11; 9.7 2026-11-12 with build b1105gf, online and offline, every map; user, with their unit on the upper deck of Compound's stair building: "When player unit is at the position, BOT got stuck beneath it. Assess the cause and provide suggestion.", "Implement in PT.", then "update the BOT and the ending cameras to 9.7") — the plain brain cuts its walk route at the first sample that sees the enemy (Maze, under Bot logic), and read that sample's height as the topmost surface over it. The stair building stands a deck and a walkway (27.64) over its ground floor: a route in at the door runs under the walkway to the stairs, the test stood its samples ON the walkway, saw the player along the upper floor, and cut the route 43 units short of them, on the ground floor (waypoint 12 of 17; from there the true line stops at the first flight). Arrived, the bot saw nothing; every plan from then on was cut at its first sample, so it held no route, and the routeless steer took it to the spot under the player — 77% of a 40 s run there, blind. Of the building's 804 ground-floor points 799 read as a level above and 430 gave that false sight of the reported place. **Now** a sample stands where the route does (`nbrain.js` WHERE A SAMPLE STANDS): `walkFloorTo` along the leg, the waypoint's own floor at a waypoint — the function the nav grid's walk edges are proved with, so the cut and the grid agree on what a leg's floor is. **The report, on this tree's sim**: from its spawn the bot sees the player after 8.2 s and stands on the deck after 8.5; from the ground floor under the walkway 2.1 and 2.4 s; from right under the player 3.4 and 3.8 s; it stays up there, in sight three quarters of the time. In the built page, offline, the player's unit held on the deck: the bot climbs both flights and fights from the upper floor, at no time under the player. **Wider** (measured on the PT tree with the boost gauge in play; the same brain): a standing enemy on 160 places of Compound, Town, Neon and District (20 on an upper level and 20 under one a map, drawn from the nav grid; the bot from its spawn, 40 s each) — 122 runs ending alike to the last figure, the enemy seen within 40 s on 141 before and 141 after, time in sight 45.0% to 45.5%, time under or over the enemy without sight 1.6% to 0.7%; and 40 bot-against-bot duels on Compound, Town, Neon, Lobby and District, every one ending alike. **One place worse**: an enemy in the upper corridor west of that deck. The route from the deck is now cut on the deck itself, at the first spot that sees into the corridor through the gap beside its parapet's end; the follower stops within 3 units of that spot, on the gap's blind side, and plans from there are cut at their first sample, so the bot works the gap's edge (in sight 43 to 75% of the last 20 s over eight places along the corridor) where the misread roof over the deck used to send it on into the corridor (78 to 96% on seven of them, 43% on the eighth). It fights; it does not stack. The cut's first sighted sample being a spot the follower's stop-short loses is the cut's own rule, older than this change. **Cost**: the cut follows its leg a unit at a time, so one cut takes up to 1.0 ms on Town and Neon where it took 0.2 (the longest seen 3.3 ms, 1.0 before), about once in 5 s a bot; over the 40 duels the brains' whole time was 7.15 s before and 7.27 after, inside the spread between two runs of one build. **Tests**: `route_cut_floor.test.js` — the report from the spawn, from the ground floor and from under the player, each on the deck and in sight within a limit (never, on the build before); the bots' frozen references re-frozen at b1105ge — against the b1105bt ones the equivalence test's Streets match parted at 7.63 s, its sniper's route running under the bridge, where the old reading stood a sample on the deck and saw the enemy the street could not; the six others and the seven Sudden Death matches played equal; 200 of 200.

**The finale: the deciding knockout on camera before the end page** (b1105gb to b1105gf, PT 2026-11-11 and 12; 9.7 2026-11-12 with build b1105gf, online and offline; user, on a player's feedback that the ending and the end page come too suddenly: "how about a sudden camera hijack for every player, which taken from the third person view (three-quarter view, high angle) to watch the last killed unit's death motion in 0.5times speed? After that the camera hijack shows the victory motion of the unit who delivers the final blow (if all units are dead, skip this one) in normal speed?", "Use reload motion for victory motion for now."; of the cut between the two shots: "can you zoom out from the defeated one and zoom in to the winning one like it one take? the transition should be done in 0.3 to 1 sec depending on their relatively distance"; of a blocked take: "make the camera go through the wall/object."; of the hold after the death motion: "there was a 停留? no need that."; of its speed: "Im thinking playing the death motion in 0.5 times speed is too slow, make it 0.6 times."; then "update the BOT and the ending cameras to 9.7") — the knockout that decides the match (Duel: the first death; 2v2: the team wipe; Trio: the last roster unit) no longer turns straight to the end page. The match freezes where it stands, the HUD goes (`html.finale-on`), and so do the battle's leftovers, each of which fades or moves in an update the frozen match no longer runs (the lock, the edge triangles, the hit X, the laser sights, the glints, the dash puffs, the rounds in the air and their trails, the beams; the last hit's stagger tilt is cleared); the camera cuts, for every viewer and in every view (Shooter, Command, Free pov; online both players, and the spectators through the same end-of-match path, which no check has watched yet). **Shot 1**: the fallen unit from its front diagonal, high — 12 out along its facing turned 35°, 12.8 above level ground, looking at its chest — its death motion from the first frame at 0.6 of its speed (`FINALE.deathRate`; 0.5 as first asked), nothing held after it: Atsuko's shot 2.5 s, Saori's 3.5. **The take**: the camera flies to shot 2 in one move, 0.3 s with the two units 20 apart or less, 1.0 s at 120 or more, in proportion between (bot duels on 14 maps put them a median 87 apart at the knockout, so about 0.8 s as a rule), along a curve that swells to one side by 0.6 of their distance, kept within 12 and 25 — up to about 40 apart both units share the frame half-way, beyond that it is a low sweep over the floor; a close pair is circled, never crossed. Its height runs straight from shot 1's to shot 2's, and the view pans evenly from the one unit to the other. Each side of the curve is rated — clear; the way free but a unit hidden at some point; a step through a box or a deck — and the better side is flown, the smaller turn on a tie: where both are blocked the camera passes through what stands in its way (it flashes by: a thing's faces are not drawn from inside it), and it never cuts. **Shot 2**: the unit that dealt the last hit, from the front (30° round to its right since b1105gg, below), a little above its head, its victory motion at normal speed — for now its reload motion — capped at 4.5 s and held 0.6 s after; skipped, the take with it, when that unit is down too (a double knockout, or its round still in the air when it fell; since b1105gg, below, shown as it lies when every unit is down). Each shot tries its angles in turn (shot 1: ±35°, ±70°, 0°, ±110°, 180°; shot 2: 0°, ±20°, ±40°, ±65°, ±90°, 180°), each at 1, 0.8 and 0.6 of the distance, for the first whose line to the unit is clear; with every line blocked, the first angle at 0.6 and the wall fade. A tap or a key press skips to the end page, through the shots and the take alike: not in the first 0.5 s (the TAP TO SKIP chip shows from then on, bottom right), not a held key's repeat, and the half second of clicks after it is swallowed, so the tap does not land on the end page now under it. A forfeit or a disconnect goes straight to the end page as before. Online the client alone runs it: the server sends what it sent before (its last snapshot, whose hit events name the lethal hit's owner and target, then `match:end`), the connection keeps that last lethal hit (`getLastKill`), and the finale shows its two units itself, whatever the last snapshot drawn had hidden — nothing new rides the network and the server's tick is untouched. The camera's near plane is 1 through the finale (0.1 in play, put back after), against the floor's thin layers flickering under the low sweep. The numbers sit together in main.js's `FINALE` for tuning. It is the PT site's finale as that site has it at b1105gf, less the site's photo mode, which this tree does not carry. Checked headless on this tree's build: offline on Factory a Duel (Atsuko down: shot 1 2.5 s, the take 1.0 s at 134 apart, the end page 6.7 s after the finale began), a skip in shot 1, 2v2, Command, and Free pov (Saori down: 3.5 s); knockouts staged 94, 44 and 3 apart (the take from 2.5 s into the finale; 0.815, 0.471 and 0.3 s planned, 0.83, 0.48 and 0.32 flown at the test's frame rate, the camera within 0.03 of its curve, the fallen unit shown every frame), a wall put between the units (flown through), a tap at 0.41 of a take (the end page at once) and a knockout on Town; online on Plain Field against a bot, two players (both pages ran it, then YOU LOSE and YOU WIN), a forfeit (the end page at once, no finale) and `match:end` held back 0.3 s (the fallen unit shown throughout); no page errors.

**The winner's camera 30° round to the unit's right; with every unit down, the take to the winner all the same** (b1105gg, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7, online and offline; user: "1. Make the camera angle of the winner camera rotate 30 degree to the left, so the winner unit can be seen better from its right side. 2. Currently when all units die at the ending, the camera skips the winner side take. Make it the camera still do the take on winner, and use the last frame of the death motion for winner. The winner side camera take duration is same as the normal version, just that the winner is a fixed defeated visual as well. 3. Implement in PT.", then "Update to 9.7.") — **The angle**: shot 2 stands 30° round from the unit's facing toward its right — the camera's left as it faces the unit (`FINALE.shot2.yaws` from −30; blocked, the angles fan out from there, the right side first: −50, −10, −70, 10, −95, 35, −120, 60, 150; it was 0, ±20, ±40, ±65, ±90, 180). A unit stands bladed behind its rifle, its chest turned to its right of where it aims, so from straight along the aim the camera looked at its left shoulder; 30° round it is square on to the body. The take ends on the new place, its pan some 30° shorter. **Every unit down**: a double knockout, or the final blow's round still in the air when its shooter fell, ended after shot 1 with nobody to show. Now the take flies and shot 2 holds for its usual length — the unit's reload motion's, capped, and the 0.6 s after: Saori's 2.4 s as ever — on the final blow's unit as it lies: its death motion's last frame, set the frame the finale begins (no blend into it, no motion; `finale.killerDown`, the pose's `end`), so it lies there through shot 1 and the take as well where they have it in view. Shot 2 looks at the body then, 1.5 over the floor (`FINALE.shot2.lookUpDown`), where the aim at a standing unit's face left the body on the frame's bottom edge, the rifle cut off; the camera stands where it always does. The rule is every unit of the match down: the final blow's unit down with a teammate still standing (its side won) has no shot 2, as first settled. Checked headless on this tree's build: the normal ending on Factory (the camera −30° round the unit, 3.2 over its root, the reload motion playing); a double knockout written into an offline Duel with the two 94 apart (shot 2 on Saori lying, her death clip at its end and paused, 2.4 s, the end page after) and 3.4 apart (the −30° line blocked there, −50° taken); 2v2 with the final blow's unit down and its ally standing (no shot 2, the end page after shot 1) and with all four down (shot 2 on it lying); online against a bot, the snapshots rewritten on their way to the page as a double knockout has them (the hit's owner at 0 HP from the lethal hit's snapshot on): the bot's unit lying from the finale's first frame through shot 2, the end page; and the round of b1105gf's checks again — offline a Duel, a skip in shot 1, 2v2, Command, Free pov, the staged takes 94, 44 and 3 apart, the wall flown through, a tap at 0.40 of a take; online a bot, two players, a forfeit, a late `match:end` — no page errors; 200 of 200.

**The finale's call, K.O. and FINAL BLOW; the winner's camera on the next roster unit** (b1105gh, 2026-11-12, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105gj, online and offline; user: "I want some visual HUD during the ending camera. Ask claude design to provide few samples.", then, of the four on the design canvas's round 12 — the HP chips kept on A, one big word a shot B, cinema bars C, a taped photo D — "Option B. Also found an issue; when a team is eliminated, but the enemy unit who kills it also dies, and there's another roster spawning, there's no winner camera. suggestions without making online/offline games more lag? Implement in PT.", then "Update cameras and Town to 9.7.") — **The call**: each shot of the finale wears one big word in the end page's title lettering — white, an ink outline, a hard drop in the colour of the unit in view (its HP chip's: cyan YOU, lime ALLY, magenta ENEMY, orange ENEMY 2; online the slot's own) — and under it a small chip of the HP chips' make with that unit's face, name and side word (no side word for a spectator, as the chips). **K.O.** over the fallen unit in shot 1; **FINAL BLOW**, on two lines, over the final blow's unit in shot 2, lying too where every unit is down. It stands on the left, tilted 6° as the title is, its middle 42.5% down the screen, clear of the unit the shot centres; at the sample's 844 × 390 the word is 108 px (FINAL BLOW 74) and the name 17. It is sized by the screen and by the room beside the unit: a unit is as wide on the screen as the screen is tall, so on a squarer screen the word shrinks to the strip left of it (`clamp(56px, min(12.8vw, 27.7vh, (45.7vw − 30vh) / 2.3), 150px)`: 150 px at 1280 × 720, 103 at 1024 × 768, 82 at 640 × 342), and a screen less than 5:4 wide has no strip — there the call goes over the unit, near the top. It pops in with its shot (0.18 s, from 1.4 times its size) and fades in 0.12 s as the take starts; a still under reduced motion. `main.js finaleCall`, `FINALE.call` (the words), `style.css .finale-call`. The TAP TO SKIP chip is as it was. **The next roster unit**: in Trio the game refills a slot the frame its unit falls — offline before the win check, on the server before its end check. So where the unit that fired the final blow was itself down by the time it landed (the same frame, or its round still in the air), that unit was a mech no longer on the field, with its slot's next roster unit alive in its place: not every unit was down, and shot 2 was dropped (offline). Online the slot's mech stood for it — the fallen unit's own model, on its feet again, when the last snapshot's swap had not been drawn yet (Saori standing for Asuna in the check on the build before) — and where the tick's last lethal hit was the one on that unit the finale did not run at all; offline the same order opened shot 1 on an empty floor, the fallen unit already off the field, and went from there to the end page. Now **the unit standing in the final blow's slot takes shot 2** — the winning side's unit on the field, its victory motion as ever — and the call over it reads **WINNER** (one word, 60 px at the sample's size), the blow having been its predecessor's; a unit that came on and then dealt the blow itself reads FINAL BLOW. The finale opens on the last knockout whose unit still lies on the field. Offline the fallen mech points at the one that replaced it (`respawnSlotMech`, `heir`). Online the last snapshot's roster swaps are drawn before the shots are set (`startOnlineFinale`: the mech built at match load swapped in and stood where the snapshot has it; a roster that fields the same unit again keeps its mech and is stood there too), the connection keeps the last eight lethal hits with the moment each arrived (`getKills`), and a unit that came on within 2.5 s of the lethal hit counts as standing in (`FINALE.heirWindowMs`: a round lives 2.2 s). The spawn glow of a unit in its first three seconds is hidden with the battle's other leftovers. **No lag in it**: the next unit is the mech built at match load that any respawn swaps in, the call is two small pieces of page written twice a finale after the match has stopped, and nothing is sent to the server or asked of it — the server's code is untouched. 2v2 is as it was: the final blow's unit down with no roster unit to follow and a teammate standing has no shot 2. Checked headless on the PT site's build, offline on Town at 844 × 390, the knockouts written into the match as real hits leave them: a win (K.O. over Hoshino in magenta, FINAL BLOW over Saori in cyan, the call's box 20 px clear of the unit's in both shots, gone through the take, YOU WIN after), a loss (the roles' colours swapped, YOU LOSE), every unit down (FINAL BLOW over Saori lying); Trio with the enemy on its last unit — both down in one frame in either order, and the player's unit down 0.6 s before its round lands: shot 1 on the enemy's unit, shot 2 on Fubuki, the player's next, WINNER, YOU WIN — the next unit's own blow 4 s after it came on (FINAL BLOW over Fubuki), and the player on its last unit with the enemy's next standing (K.O. over Saori, WINNER over Aru in magenta, YOU LOSE); the win again at 1280 × 720, 1024 × 768, 640 × 342 and upright at 390 × 844. Online against a bot on Plain Field, the knockouts staged on a test copy of the server (HP 0 and the tick's hit event; its tick, respawn and end check the real ones): a Duel (K.O. in the host's slot colour, FINAL BLOW in the bot's, YOU LOSE); Trio — both down in one tick in either order (before this the finale did not run where the hit on the host came last), the host's unit down 0.6 s before the bot's last, both WINNER over Asuna, the host's next; 4 s before, FINAL BLOW over her; and the host on its last unit with the bot fielding the same unit again (WINNER over it at its spawn place, YOU LOSE). No page errors; the shared suite 200 of 200. On this tree's build (b1105gj): offline on Town a Duel win (K.O. over Hoshino in magenta, FINAL BLOW over Saori in cyan, the call gone through the take, YOU WIN) and Trio with both down in one frame (WINNER over Fubuki, the player's next); online against a bot on Plain Field a Duel (K.O. in the host's slot colour, FINAL BLOW in the bot's, YOU LOSE) and Trio with both down in one tick, staged on a test copy of this tree's server (WINNER over Asuna, the host's next, YOU WIN). No page errors; 200 of 200.

**The finale's victory motion once on a shell-by-shell gun** (b1105gi, 2026-11-12, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105gj, online and offline; user: "When the winner camera is on auto reload unit (haruka, hoshino, aris), they would do their reload motion repeatedly. Make them only do once. Implement in PT.", then "Update cameras and Town to 9.7.") — the victory motion is the unit's reload clip for now, and a shell-by-shell gun (`autoReload`: Hoshino, Haruka, Aris) loops that clip in play, one pass a round, where a magazine gun plays it once. Shot 2 lasts the clip and 0.6 s more, so their clip started over in that hold. Now the finale's victory pose asks for one pass (`finalePoseFor`'s `once`): the clip plays once from its first frame and holds its last, as a magazine gun's does, and the loop is put back the next time play starts the clip (`updateUnitModelState`, the rig entry's `loops`). Shot 2's length is as it was: Hoshino 2.13 s, Haruka 2.6 s, Aris 2.8 s. Checked headless on the PT site's build with shot 2 held for 3.4 clip lengths and the clip watched every frame. Offline on Plain Field: Hoshino (her gun full, and three rounds short so that she is in her reload loop until the final blow), Haruka and Aris each make the one pass and stand still on the last frame, where the build before made three more in the same hold; Saori, a magazine gun, as before; in play before the knockout the three guns' reload still loops (four passes counted each). Online against a bot, the knockout staged on a test copy of the server: Hoshino and Haruka the one pass (Hoshino three more on the build before). No page errors. On this tree's build: Hoshino, three rounds short and in her reload loop until the final blow (three passes counted in play), makes the one pass in shot 2 and stands still on its last frame; no page errors.

**The finale's victory motion at 0.6 of its speed** (b1105gk, 2026-11-12, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105gl, online and offline; user: "play the winner reload motion in 0.6 times speed. implement in PT.", then, with b1105gl, "Then update to 9.7.") — the final blow's unit's victory motion (its reload clip for now) runs at 0.6 of its speed in shot 2, as the fallen unit's death motion does in shot 1 (`FINALE.victoryRate`, beside `deathRate`; `finalePoseFor`). Shot 1, the take and the call are as they were. Checked headless on the PT site's build, shot 2 held and the clip watched every frame, the seconds its mixer is advanced counted until it ends (the page runs under 30 frames a second, where the game's frame step is capped at 1/30 s and a clip runs slower than the clock): Saori's 1.8 s clip ends after 2.97 s at a time scale of 0.6 (after 1.77 s on the build before), shot 2 3.6 s; Hoshino, in her reload loop until the final blow, one pass, 1.53 s in 2.55 s, shot 2 3.16 s; Kurumi 2.8 s in 4.64 s, shot 2 5.27 s; both down in one frame, shot 2 3.6 s on Saori as she lies; nothing held, shot 2 lasted 3.68 s by the clock and the end page followed. Then update to 9.7.") — a model may name its own rate for its victory motion in the finale's shot 2 (`UNIT_MODELS` `victoryRate`, read by `finaleVictoryRate`), and Iori's is 1: her reload clip (2.5 s) plays at its own speed, where every other unit's runs at `FINALE.victoryRate`, 0.6 (b1105gk), and her shot 2 lasts 3.1 s again, the clip and the 0.6 s hold (4.77 s on b1105gk). **The kill feed: who knocked out whom, under PAUSE** (b1105gm, 2026-11-12, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105go, online and offline; user: "I want a killing HUD. It would visually be smth like \"O>O\", where O is the thumbnail and > refers to elimination", with two places marked under PAUSE and the pace set out; of the design canvas's rounds 13 and 13b, "Try type D, but make the black background blank", then "D1" and, for a mid-size 2v2 screen, "M3") — when a unit is knocked out the HUD shows the final blow's unit's face, its weapon with the muzzle toward the fallen unit, and the fallen unit's face greyed, each face ringed in its side's colour as the HP chips are (the seat's own online). Nothing stands behind them: every face carries an ink line round its ring and the weapon a white edge with an ink line, so a dark gun reads on a dark wall and a white one on a bright sky. An entry is 96 × 26 px with 20 px faces whose middles stand 70 px apart whatever the weapon, which is fitted to the 40 × 17 px between them; it leans 1.5° as the chips do. **Where.** Two places under PAUSE, one under the other: on a screen up to 720 px wide at 55 and 90 px from the top (the places marked on the user's 606 × 327 screen); wider, where the chips are bigger, the entry is a third bigger (125 × 34) at 66 and 110. Where the second row of HP chips stands under PAUSE, 2v2 on a screen 721 to 1099 px wide, the two places lie side by side under that row at the plain size (M3): an entry alone in the middle, a second to its right while the first steps left. One under the other there, the pair closed on the lock bracket at the screen's middle; side by side both stay 3 px above it at 844 × 390. A 2v2 screen under 576 px wide, where the second row's chips leave less than an entry's width beside PAUSE, uses the same side-by-side places under that row (not asked for: without it the entry lay over the chips there). **The pace** (`KILL_FEED`, `updateKillFeed`). A knockout takes the upper place when nothing shows, the lower beside one, and waits behind two. The upper entry leaves a second after it took that place, sliding up and fading over 0.25 s; the lower then moves up and stays a second of its own. While a knockout waits, the feed steps 0.3 s after its last change instead (never later than the plain second), and each step lets the next one in; once none waits, the two that show keep their second each. The clock is real time while the HUD is updated, so a paused match holds it. **What is shown.** Every knockout but the one that ends the match, in every view: shooter, command mode, a spectator's. Offline the feed is fed where the finale's kill log is (`finaleNoteKill`: the round's own shooter, so a unit that fell before its round landed is still the one drawn), and the win check, later in the same frame, clears it before a frame is drawn. Online it reads the lethal hits off the snapshots the page gets anyway (`connection.js` `noteKills`, `drainKills`; nothing more is sent): the fallen unit is the one its seat held in the snapshot before, since the server refills a Trio seat in the tick its unit falls; the shooter's unit is its seat's unless the seat's roster moved after the round was fired, which is told from when each round first appeared; and the knockout is the match's last when the snapshot says so by the server's own rule. **Its cost.** The three pictures of an entry are drawn ahead, when a unit's HP chip is first drawn (`warmKillArt`): the face as the round frames show it, the same greyed, and the weapon as a sticker with its edge, each kept as a small picture. An entry clones three of them; nothing is fetched, decoded or filtered when a unit falls. The first build drew the weapon's edge and the grey with CSS filters, and in the software renderer the checks run on each new entry stalled a frame for 0.85 to 1.06 s; with the pictures drawn ahead the median frame is 54 ms with an entry showing against 55 ms without. Checked headless on the built PT. The pace, knockouts pushed into the feed of a frozen match at about 20 frames a second: one knockout shows 1.03 s; two 0.4 s apart, the first leaves 1.00 s after it came and the second 1.04 s after it moved up; four at once, steps after 0.33 s and 0.30 s, then 1.04 s and 1.01 s; a third arriving with two showing steps at once, then 1.03 s and 1.00 s. The places, measured: 606 × 327, 96 × 26 at 55 and 90, the faces' middles 70 px apart; 844 × 390 2v2, one entry centred at 146, two at x 321 to 417 and 427 to 523; 844 × 390 1v1 and 1280 × 720, 125 × 34 at 66 and 110; 560 × 320 2v2, side by side at 88; command mode and the Practice spectator, the same places. A real offline match, 2v2 Trio with every seat bot-driven and every unit cut to 12 HP: 11 knockouts, 10 entries, each with the kill log's two units and their seats' colours; the 11th ended the match and drew none, the feed empty at the finale's first frame. Online against bots, knockouts staged on a test copy of the server: Trio 1v1, an entry for the bot's first unit, a second under it for the host's, two at once when both fall in one tick, each with the unit that stood there before the refill, and none for the match's last; Trio 2v2, the four seats' colours, a seat's last unit drawn while its side still fields one, the match's last not drawn. The working-out in `connection.js` passes 12 cases on made-up snapshots. No page errors. The shared suite was not run again: `main.js`, `style.css` and `online/connection.js` alone changed. In 9.7 (build b1105go; the three files merged, the change they take is PT's own to the line): checked headless on the built client. Offline, the pace's four cases, and a real 2v2 Trio bot match of 11 knockouts — two before the recorder started, eight drawn, each with the kill log's two units and their seats' colours, the last, which ended the match, not drawn, the feed empty at the finale's first frame. Online against a bot on this tree's own server (a test copy, knockouts staged), Trio 1v1: an entry for the bot's first unit, a second under it for the host's, none for the knockout that ended the match. No page errors.

**An ordered unit does not vault at a transporter's leg, offline** (9.7 build b1105go, 2026-11-12; found comparing this tree with PT file by file, user: "what else am i missing?") — the offline game's copy of the move order (`applyMoveOrder` in `main.js`) vaults toward a waypoint on a ledge over the unit's floor; the shared sim's (`command.js`) and PT's copy leave that out on a leg that is a ladder or a transporter (`!viaLink`), and this tree's copy still left it out on a ladder alone (`!ladderLeg`). One word; the online game, which runs the shared copy, was right already.

**The ending cameras' stops: what covers the unit goes see-through** (b1105gn, 2026-11-12, PT; 9.7 the same day with build b1105go, online and offline; user: "In ending cameras, make the object go transparent if they block the units.", then, of a first build that also opened along the camera's flight: "I don't need and don't want a clean hole on the path where the camera switches position. What i want is simply when the camera stops and focuses on the units, the objects blocking units during that time should be transparent", and of the two looks offered, the whole thing translucent or "只有蓋住單位的那一塊變透明，物件其他部分不變": the latter) — at the finale's two stops, shot 1 on the fallen unit and shot 2 on the final blow's unit, whatever of the map covers the unit is not drawn where it covers it: a window as wide and as tall as the unit's body, clear in its middle and thinning back to the whole thing at its rim, open from the camera to the unit. The rest of the thing stands as it was. **When.** Only while the camera stands at a stop, and only when something is in the way: the sight rule the shots' places are picked by (`botHasLineOfSight`, the collision boxes) is asked from the camera to up to 12 of the unit's bones, and one line blocked opens the stop's window over 0.15 s (`seeHidden`, `seeUpdate`). A stop whose unit is in plain view is drawn exactly as before. Through the take between the two stops nothing changes: the window shuts over the last 0.15 s of shot 1 and opens again only once the camera stands at shot 2. **What is cut** (`SEE`, the shader patch `SEE_FRAG`). A pixel of a map mesh is left out when the camera sees it inside the unit's ellipse (the box of its bones on the screen and 1.2 more), it stands nearer the camera than the unit's middle on the level (an upright plane, so the wall at the unit's back stays whole), it is over the unit's floor, and it is above the camera's line to the unit's feet (the ground between them, a kerb, stay). **How, and its cost.** Play's own fade turns a whole registered thing translucent, and Town, where the user saw a unit blocked, has none (off since b1026x); the window needs nothing registered. It is a `discard` in the shader, and a shader that may discard is taken out of a phone GPU's hidden-surface pass, so play's materials are not touched: each map material gets a twin carrying the window (`seeTwinOf`), made and compiled ahead one at a time in the match's first seconds (`seeWarmTick`), and a mesh wears its twin only at a stop whose window has opened. The fade of the maps that have one still runs through the finale as before. `SEE.on = false` turns the window off, `SEE.gate = false` opens it at every stop. **Where.** Town alone for now (b1105go; user: "turn this effect on in town only, for now."): `SEE.maps` is `['town']`, and on every other map nothing of it runs — no twin is made, no mesh listed, no gate asked; `null` puts it on every map. Checked headless on the built PT (b1105gn, the window then on every map), Town, at 20 knockouts taken from recorded bot duels, 9 of them with the final blow's unit stood behind the nearest low cover: of 60 stops with the unit in the picture, 5 had bones hidden (10 of 101 bones) and the window left 1; the gate opened it at 4 of those 5 (the fifth: one bone of 19 hidden for a moment early in shot 1) and at 4 of the 55 with nothing hidden; of the 37 stops with nothing hidden whose picture was compared, window on against off, one differed, by 30 of 230,400 pixels; at the stops it uncovered a unit it changed 2,091 to 3,350. In the take, half-way, the window was shut in 20 of 20, and the picture compared at 9 differed from the window-off one by at most 12 pixels, under its own frame-to-frame noise there. Neon, which has the fade: 3 knockouts, one stop with 3 of 19 bones hidden, none with the window. Cost, the build before and this one side by side in one headless run (both pages slow there, about two frames a second, so this shows no difference and not a speed): play a median 552 ms a frame against 554, the finale's frames no longer; the renderer held 19 shader programs before the knockout and 19 after the finale (14 and 14 before: the twins' five more, none compiled at a stop); Town's 10 twins were made in the match's first seconds. Online against a bot (Trio 1v1 on Plain Field, the knockout staged on a test copy of the server): shot 1, the take, shot 2 and the end page as before, the gate asked at both stops and, nothing in the way on that field, nothing opened. Not tried on a phone. No page errors. The shared suite was not run again: `main.js` alone changed.

**The second row of HP boxes flush on a mid-size screen** (b1105go, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7; user, of a 955 × 488 screen: "for smaller screen, move the lower HP boxes to the side more. The current mobile phone version and the big screen layout are fine, leave those alone") — between the phone's tier (720 px wide and under) and the desktop's (1100 and over) the ally's and the second enemy's boxes stood 108 px in from the first row's, so that the second enemy's ends before the thumb buttons on a landscape phone (b1105ah). On such a screen 460 px tall or more the thumb cluster starts under the row, and the second row now stands flush under the first, 12 px from the edge, as on a desktop (a media rule in `style.css`, 721 to 1099 px wide and 460 px tall or more): at 955 × 488 the two boxes moved from 108 px in to 12, the second enemy's box ends 143 px down and DODGE starts at 219; 1099 × 460 and 721 × 500 the same 12. A shorter screen of that width keeps the stagger (844 × 390 and 900 × 459: 108 still), where DODGE reaches into the row's band. The phone's tier and the desktop's are as they were (606 × 327 and 709 × 327: 12, the row 48 px down; 1100 × 600 and 1280 × 720: 12). Measured headless on the built PT in a 2v2 Trio match. `style.css` alone.

**The kill feed's entries stay 1.5 s; the winner's camera 40° round** (b1105gp, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7, online and offline; user: "make the kill HUD duration from 1sec to 1.5sec", "rotate the winner camera 10 more degrees to the left (so the user see the winner from the right side more)") — `KILL_FEED.stayMs` 1000 to 1500: an entry stays a second and a half in the upper place before it slides up and fades, and the one under it the same once it has moved up; the 0.3 s step while knockouts wait and the 0.25 s leave are as they were. `FINALE.shot2.yaws`: the final blow's unit is shown from 40° round to its right (30° since b1105gg), and the angles tried when that line is blocked move with it, the same fan about -40: -40, -60, -20, -80, 0, -105, 25, -130, 50, 140. Checked headless on the built PT, knockouts pushed into the feed of a frozen match (the page ran at 5 to 11 frames a second, and a step waits for the next frame): one knockout shows 1.57 s (1.03 on b1105gm); two 0.4 s apart, the first leaves 1.58 s after it came and the second 1.54 s after it moved up; four at once, steps after 0.38 and 0.41 s, then 1.53 and 1.58 s; a third arriving with two showing steps at once, then 1.53 and 1.56 s.
