**The ally tactic: FORWARD and BACK OFF, called to the bot teammate from a button by the stick** (b1105gq and b1105gr, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7 with build b1105gr, online and offline; user: "i want to make a in-game command that makes ally BOT moving forward/backward. The idea is a small button at the up left corner of the joystick. By tapping it, 2 pop up buttons will appear, one would say smth like 'forward' and the other would say smth like 'back off'. The goal is to ask ally BOT to stay close or away from enemies.", then: 'change the effect to "hide 10 sec" and "lockrange -30 for 10sec"', "Remove these command buttons in command mode", "If the screen is big, place it nearer to the joystick", and "when there is either command in effect, make the command button itself glowing as well") — a 30 px button over the stick's left edge opens two pills, FORWARD and BACK OFF; a pick is a call to the bot on the player's side and stands 10 s (`ALLY_TACTIC_MS`). **FORWARD** moves the bot's range band — the distance it keeps to its target, centred on its lock range with edges ±7 — 30 nearer (`ALLY_TACTIC_PUSH`), taken off after the shotgun rule so the call always moves the unit in from where it stood. Only where the bot wants to stand moves: the unit's lock range itself, the red lock's and the homing's, is not touched, so a pushed bot stands inside its lock. **BACK OFF** is command mode's Hide stance for those 10 s: out of every enemy's sight, firing back only at what it can see from where it stands. While a call stands the button itself is lit — yellow, with a pulsing halo — and so is the call's pill the next time the two are opened; a tap on the lit pill takes the call back at once, a tap on the other replaces it at once; the pills close on a pick, on a second tap of the button, or 3 s after they opened. The button shows in the shooter view of a 2v2 whose teammate is a bot (offline always; online a seat that was a bot at the match's start) and stands — not in 1v1, not beside a human teammate, not in Sudden Death (that brain takes no tactic), not on the Shooting Range, and not in command mode, where it hides with the stick. In spectator mode (the Practice toggle, an online spectator) it shows and does not answer. With the player's own unit down and the teammate fighting on, the calls still work. How: `shared/src/sim/tactic.js` keeps the call on the unit's command entry (`tactic` { kind, until }, never on the fighter, which is serialized into every snapshot); the brain (`nbrain.js`) reads it each tick, moves the band for a push and treats a hide as the stance without writing the entry's own hide flag, so a commander's or a reload's hide and the call never undo each other. The band is the one Pursue, Engage and the Maze's firing-position search already read, and a route under way is planned again the tick a call lands, is taken back or runs out. A call dies with the unit it was made to. Online the page sends `ally:tactic` { slot, kind | null } — what the player meant, so a call that ran out on the way is not set anew by its own take-back; the server takes it from a seated player who is not in command mode, for a seat of the same side that was a bot at the match's start and still is, outside Sudden Death, two calls to one unit at least 500 ms apart (a take-back is never held up), and answers nothing: what stands reaches the side with its snapshots (`commands[slot].tactic`, the end in the server's clock), so the lit button and the lit pill are the server's word. Placement: everything is measured off the stick (left 28, bottom 28; 140 across, 120 on a screen up to 720 px wide), the button 18 px over the stick's box and the lower pill 4 px over its ring, the same on a 606 × 327 phone and a 1920 × 1080 screen; a screen too short for the two rows (under 322 px high, under 380 on a wide one) lays button, FORWARD and BACK OFF in one row over the stick. In 9.7 every file takes PT's own change to the line (a three-way merge with no conflict). Checked here. The shared tests: six new (`shared/test/tactic.test.js` — the call on the table; FORWARD on Plain Field held at 73 to 87 with the lock on and back at the band after; the take-back; BACK OFF on Streets hidden from both enemies to the call's end; a standing Hide order outliving a call; a Maze route on Compound planned again on a call and on its take-back) and the 200 there were, the brain's equivalence references among them, unchanged: 206 of 206. The built page, offline, headless at 13 frames a second, the two enemies' bodies held still by the check: the teammate's distance to the nearer one 101.7 to 109.0 before a FORWARD, 64.5 to 75.5 under it, 99.5 to 107.9 after it ran out; under a BACK OFF on Streets its hide scratch set on every second of the call and cleared after; the taps — open, a call, the lit pill on re-opening, the take-back, the other call over the standing one, the 3 s close; the button lit under FORWARD with the two closed and open, under BACK OFF from the next frame drawn, and not lit with no call, after the take-back and after a call ran out; shown and answering in 2v2 and Trio 2v2, shown and not answering in spectator mode, absent in 1v1, hidden in Sudden Death, in the command view (a standing call ending there) and with the teammate down; a Trio teammate that fell with a call standing came back with none. Online, a 2v2 Duel against a test copy of this tree's server with the two enemies pinned: the teammate (lock 200) 195.2 to 199.6 from its target before the call, 163.7 to 169.4 under it, 194.5 to 200.0 after; the button lit and the lit pill from the echo, 5,894 ms left; the take-back; BACK OFF with the hide scratch on all 34 samples; FORWARD over a standing BACK OFF; calls to an enemy seat, to the caller's own, of an unknown kind and with no seat left nothing standing, and of two calls 120 ms apart the second was not taken. The button's box against the fullscreen button, the chips, the BOOST sticker and the stick's ring at 702 × 373, 606 × 327, 780 × 360, 844 × 390, 955 × 488, 1280 × 720 and 1920 × 1080: nothing touches. No page errors. Not tried on a phone. `shared/src/sim/tactic.js` (new), `constants.js`, `command.js`, `nbrain.js`, `index.js`; `shared/test/tactic.test.js` (new); `server/src/index.js`; `client/src/main.js`, `style.css`, `online/connection.js`.

**The tactic button beside the fullscreen button, at its size** (b1105gs, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7; user, on a picture of a 624 × 330 phone with an arrow from the button to the right of the fullscreen button: "Layout change, move the command button to the right of full screen button, make Command Button's size same as FC button") — the button is 26 px now, the fullscreen button's size (30 until then), its icon 15 px. On a narrow screen (up to 720 px wide), where the fullscreen button sits under the left chips, the button sits right of it on its row, 8 px off (left 50, top 94), and the two calls open in a column right of the button from that row down: FORWARD on the row, BACK OFF under it, both over the stick. On a wider screen the fullscreen button sits by the top chip against PAUSE, with no room for a second button beside it up to about 920 px of width, and the tactic button stays by the stick at the new size, as "If the screen is big, place it nearer to the joystick" had it. The taps at the mirror place — open, a call, the lit pill, the take-back, the other call over the standing one, the 3 s close — as before. No page errors. **The winner's camera: 0.6 s on the unit standing, its reload motion at its own speed, 0.6 s on its last frame** (b1105gu, 2026-11-12, PT then 9.7, online and offline; user, told that shot 2 lasted the motion's length over its rate and a closing hold: "Make it as below universally, including iori. starting 0.6 秒 + normal speed reload motion + ending 0.6 秒") — the finale's shot 2 runs in three parts, every unit by the one rule. The camera arrives on the final blow's unit standing at ease, as it stood through the take, and that holds for 0.6 s (`FINALE.holdBeforeVictoryS`, new; the motion used to begin the frame the camera arrived). The victory motion (the reload clip for now) then plays once from its first frame at its own speed (`FINALE.victoryRate` 1; 0.6 since b1105gk). Its last frame is held for 0.6 s (`holdAfterVictoryS`, as before). Shot 2 lasts the three together: Marina 2.7 s (3.1 before), Hoshino 2.73 (3.16), Saori 3.0 (3.6), Haruka 3.2 (3.93), Aris 3.4 (4.27), Iori 3.7 (3.1), Hina 3.87 (5.04), Kurumi, the longest reload, 4.0 (5.27). Iori's own rate (b1105gl) is gone with her exception, the model setting `victoryRate` and its reader `finaleVictoryRate` with it: her clip ran at its own speed already, and her shot 2 is 0.6 s longer for the opening hold. With every unit down (b1105gg) shot 2 keeps the normal ending's length, the two holds and the motion's, the unit lying still through it. The call's word comes in with the shot as before, over the unit still standing. Shot 1, the take and the skip are as they were. In 9.7 `main.js` takes PT's own change (a three-way merge; one conflict, in the finale's header comment, where this tree keeps its own lines on when each part came over). Checked headless on this tree's built page as PT's was, an offline Duel, the page's clock stepped 1/60 s for every frame drawn so the lengths read are those of a device at 60 frames a second, shot 2 left to run and every frame of it noted (each part reads a frame long or short): Saori stands 0.62 s, her 1.8 s clip then plays once in 1.78 s at a time scale of 1, its last frame holds 0.62 s, 3.02 s in all against 3.0 set; Iori 0.62, 2.48 of 2.5, 0.62, 3.72 against 3.7; Kurumi 0.62, 2.78 of 2.8, 0.62, 4.02 against 4.0; both down in one frame with Iori's the final blow, shot 2 3.72 s on her as she lies, against 3.7. On the PT site, the same lines: Marina 2.72 against 2.7; Hoshino, a shell-by-shell gun, one pass, 2.75 against 2.73; and online against a bot, the host's Saori knocking the bot's out (staged on a test copy of the server), 0.6 s standing, 1.78 s of motion at a time scale of 1, 0.63 s on its last frame, 3.02 s in all against 3.0, YOU WIN and the end page. No page errors. The shared suite was not run again: main.js alone changed. `client/src/main.js`.

Her model, client/public/models/hiyori_v2.glb (the user's Hiyori without her backpack case, her Formation_Idle, the swimsuit Hiyori's seven battle motions bound by exact bone names, her hair following her Formation_Idle, Saori's jump, Fubuki's dodge); `UNIT_MODELS.hiyori`: Normal_Idle to stand, Kneel_Attack_Start to dodge, Move_Jump to jump. A new clip key, `cooldown` (fit 'cooldown'): updateUnitModelState plays Normal_Attack_Delay from the shot to the end of her 1 s fire cooldown, one cycle, over walking and standing; the reload, a dodge, a jump or a sprint take its place, and an emptied magazine goes straight to the reload. Her pictures: units/hiyori_profile.png, units/hiyori_profile_thumbnail.png (UNIT_FACE 0.56 / 0.47), weapons/hiyori.png (WEAPON_BOX 158, 20, 712 x 206). Online the server reads her from the shared table; nothing new rides a snapshot.
**Dodging while firing faces the dodge, offline and online** (b1105cr, b1105cx, 2026-11-08, 9.5; user: "When the character is doing dodge+firing+motion input. Make the character facing the dodging direction instead of facing target. Once the dodge animation is over and the character is still firing, face at the target again. Also, if \"dodge+firing+motion\" is airborne, the character still faces the target.", then "implement the dodge facing direction update in 9.5. Online and offline.") — a player's dodge on the ground whose line the stick chose turns the model to that line while the unit fires, over its target and the free-pov crosshair; when the dodge ends the firing rule turns it back, at the usual MODEL_TURN_RATE. Offline the line is stamped where the dodge starts (updatePlayer: `stepFaceAt`/`stepFaceX`/`stepFaceZ`). Online the server marks the dodge: tick.js applyInput sets the fighter's `stepStick` when the stick chose the line, tryStartStep and tickStep clear it to undefined, so the snapshot carries the key only while such a dodge runs; mirrorFighterToMech reads it with the dodge's line (stepTo minus stepFrom), so every client turns that player's model. Untouched: a dodge with no stick, a dodge in the air, a dodge without firing, the bots. Only the model turns: the rounds, the hit capsule and the bots' reading are as before. Tests: 185 of 185.
**Hina dodges with Fubuki's motion** (b1105cw, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Apply Fubuki's dodge motion to Hina. Implement in PT and 9.5.") — her `dodge` borrows fubuki_dodge_clip.json (Fubuki's Kneel_Attack_Start) with `base: 'Move_End_Normal'`, her own clip, which still poses her MG42, jacket and wings; 32 of her 44 body bones take Fubuki's rotations, the rest (helper bones, toes) keep her own. Her jump stays Saori's (b1105ck). Her clips crossfade (MODEL_FADE_S).
**The lobby's map die takes the taps around it** (b1105cv, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, of the phone lobby with the die circled: "make the random map dice's detection wider in main menu. user sometimes trigger the map selection menu instead of the dice itself. Keep current visual layout.") — an invisible pad on the die (style.css `.memo-dice::before`) reaches 16 px out on the left, the top and the bottom and 12 px on the right, inside the paper's edges: about 64 x 68 px where the die is 36 x 36. A tap on the pad is a tap on the die and presses it the same way; nothing drawn changes. Measured on a 1000 x 462 phone view: taps 10 px left, 12 px above, 12 px below and 9 px right of the die roll the map; 40 px left still opens the picker.
**Warehouse: the two small crates stepped over** (b1105cu, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, of the debug panel's warehouse #195 and #506: "Make these two object walk-over-able instead of blocking the way.") — each crate's box and the thin lip box beside it (data obstacles 195 and 214, 506 and 515, 1 m tall) now top out at 0.6 m (2.4 u), under the 0.65 m a body steps (SURFACE_STEP_HEIGHT 2.6 u), and their floors (surfaces 19 and 168, 62 and 325) lie at the same height: a unit walks onto the crate and over it instead of being stopped, as Neon's ramp frames were trimmed (b1105bm). The model is unchanged, so a unit on a crate stands 0.4 m inside its top. The nav grid is rebuilt (2291 walkable nodes); the nav data test passes in both trees.
**Asuna's and Aru's crouched hit capsule online** (b1105ct, 2026-11-08, 9.5; user: "update Asuna/Aru HB to online as well") — the shared unit table lists `crouchHit` for unit9 and unit3 as the client's does, and the server's hit tests (shared/src/sim/projectiles.js: the round, the pellets, the beam, the charged beam, free aim's re-pick) read `hitCapAt`: the server plays no clips, so `hitCapOf` (history.js) reads the client's clip-picker moments off the fighter: alive, off a ladder, not dodging, not airborne, not sprinting, and firing (within 200 ms of a shot, charging a sniper shot, sweeping a charged beam) or reloading. Lag compensation records each tick's capsule with the body (`hitCap` on the history ring), so a lagged shot meets the capsule the shooter saw. The homing still steers at the standing centre, as the client's does. The bots never read it. Nothing new rides a snapshot.
**Borrowed motions for twelve more units, and Asuna's and Aru's crouched hit capsule** (b1105cj to b1105cs, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Update unit motions and Asuna&Aru hitbox to 9.5.") — the unit model table is PT's: Subaru's dodge (subaru_dodge_clip.json) for Koyuki, Asuna, Atsuko, Saori, Marina, Haruka, Yuuka and Rabu; Saori's jump (saori_jump_clip.json) for Koyuki and Fubuki, and for Hina's jump and her dodge; Fubuki's dodge, her Kneel_Attack_Start written out by shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs --no-root-motion to client/public/models/fubuki_dodge_clip.json (0.6 s, 124 tracks, 166 KB), for Aris and Kei. Each borrowed entry's `base` is the unit's own clip it replaced, which still poses its gear, hair and clothes (borrowClip). Asuna and Aru list `crouchHit: ['Normal_Attack_Ing', 'Normal_Reload']`: while their model plays one of them, `hitCapsule` gives them a capsule two thirds as tall (HIT_CAP_CROUCH [-16/15, 8/15], the bottom kept), read by every hit test of the client (rounds, pellets, Aris's beams, Kei's charged beam, the free pov's round and crosshair, the online mirror's own-shot ring); the bots never read it. Online, see b1105ct.
**Hoshino jumps with Saori's motion** (b1105ci, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "Apply Saori's jump motion to Hoshino. implement in PT.") — Saori's jump, Move_Jump in her file, written out by shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs (the b1105cf sampler) to client/public/models/saori_jump_clip.json (1 s, 119 tracks, every rotation of unit length, its root pinned in plan); Hoshino's `jump` entry borrows it as her dodge borrows Subaru's: her body takes Saori's rotations, her gear, gun, hair, skirt and face her own 01_Move_End_Normal (`base`), and the way back to her stance eases over BORROWED_EXIT_S. Its entry has `easeIn`: the way in eases the same way (measured at 60 fps under the fake clock, the cut into Saori's first frame turned 139 degrees in one frame); her dodge keeps its snap in.
**Hoshino eases out of the borrowed dodge** (b1105ch, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "there is a sudden flick between the last moment of dodge motion and her normal stance when she finishes the dodge move") — Hoshino's model cuts between its clips (`fade: 0`, the owner's 2026-10-10 pick: a crossfade slid her backpack across her back), and her own dodge ended near her stance, so the cut never showed; Subaru's ends in Subaru's pose. Leaving a borrowed clip on a cutting model, every part the clip moved but the gear (`borrowClip`'s `body`: the borrowed body, the Biped's helper bones, the hair and the skirt of her own clip) is caught as it stands and eased into the new clip's pose after the mixer each frame over BORROWED_EXIT_S = 0.15 s, the rotations by slerp and the positions by lerp; the gear (shield, plates, pistol, shells, holster, pockets, grenades) still cuts, so the backpack never slides. Measured under Playwright's fake clock at a steady 60 fps, the dodge's end: before, 83° of turn in one frame (three dodges alike); after, at most 9.4° a frame, the turn spread over the nine frames of the 0.15 s.
**Hoshino's gear stays on through the borrowed dodge** (b1105cg, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, with her bind pose: "it's better, but her accessories from \"bind pose\" are showing up during the dodge motion") — her plates, shield, pistol and shells are posed only by her own clips (her 01_Move_End_Normal scales the plates to 0.2 and the shield to 0.63 x 0.38 and moves them into her vest and onto her back), and a part no playing clip moves falls back to its bind pose, where her file lays them out beside her. `borrowClip` now takes from the borrowed file the Biped's body bones alone (`Bip001…`, the face, the eyes and `Bip001_Weapon` left out) and adds, for every other part, the tracks of the rig's own clip named by the entry's `base`, fitted to the borrowed clip's length: Hoshino's `dodge` has `base: '01_Move_End_Normal'` (0.9 s, fitted to 1 s), so her gear, gun, hair, skirt, pockets and mouth follow her own dodge while her body does Subaru's.
**Hoshino's borrowed dodge no longer flickers** (b1105cf, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user, with a screen recording: "it's all flickering and distorting") — Subaru's Move_Jump keeps most of its rotations as glTF CUBICSPLINE samplers, each key an in-tangent, its value and an out-tangent; shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs wrote those triplets as plain values marked smooth, which AnimationClip.parse read three keys to one, so a tangent was taken for a rotation: 88 of the 117 rotation tracks, quaternions up to 1.6 off unit length (Bip001_L_Hand the worst) in 3 of the 14 frames sampled through a dodge, the mesh sheared and stretched. The tool now samples a CUBICSPLINE sampler at 60 Hz by the glTF Hermite form, normalizes a rotation and writes plain linear keys; subaru_dodge_clip.json re-extracted (258 KB, every rotation of unit length). models/climb_clip.json carries the same fault but binds to no one: all sixteen unit files have their own Vital_Retreat.
**Hoshino dodges with Subaru's motion** (b1105ce, 2026-11-08, PT and 9.5; user: "let's try to apply subaru's dodge motion to Hoshino first. Implement in PT only.") — Subaru's dodge, Move_Jump in her file, written out by shared/tools/anim/clip_extract.mjs to client/public/models/subaru_dodge_clip.json (1 s, 117 rotation tracks, its root pinned in plan, 242 KB); Hoshino's `dodge` entry names it with `from`, and `borrowClip` fetches the file once and binds it by bone name as the climb clip is bound: the rotations alone, the bones her rig lacks left out. Her jump keeps her own 01_Move_End_Normal.
**The phone's buttons press down under a held stick; a cancelled touch lets go** (b1105cd, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "when the joycon is being held, other buttons on the right side (Target/Fire/Dodge/Sprint/Jump) do not show the pushed down effect", then "No" to a held look for a locked sprint and "If the fix won't bring any more lag to online/offline games, then yes" to the cancelled touch) — the pressed look was CSS `:active`, which a page gives one element at a time, so the finger on the stick held it and a second finger's button stayed up, its input going through all the same. setupHUD now marks each touch's own button `is-down` on its pointerdown and clears it on its pointerup or pointercancel, and style.css gives `.buttons button.is-down` the `:active` look; a mouse keeps `:active` alone. A touch the system cancels (a call, a swipe in from the screen's edge) sent no pointerup, so the stick stayed deflected and a held button stayed held, the unit walking and the gun firing on until the next touch: the stick and the buttons now take pointercancel as a release. Measured on a touch emulation with Hina, before: the fire button stayed up under the held stick, and after a cancel the unit walked 4.2 u and fired 13 rounds in 1.2 s. Event work only, on a press or a release: nothing per frame, the same inputs sent online.

**The lobby's red line is dashed** (b1105bx, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "change the red line style from current \"G\" to \"E\". Apply to PT and 9.5.") — `lobbyCrayonLine` draws the design canvas's round 10b style E in place of G: an even dashed line, 24 px dashes 9 px apart on the 388 px box along the same slow wobble, each dash a 3.9 px crayon stroke (within 5% thicker or thinner and 0.2 px up or down, from a seeded draw) whose ends thin over its first and last 28%, the canvas's generator (seps2.py `dashes_e`) with a seeded draw of JS's own; the same red, the same #crayon-line wax, the same two places, seeds and tilts. The SVG stretches to the column as before, so the dashes and the gaps stretch with it (22 and 8.3 px in the 358 px column of a 390 px portrait phone; the landscape phone's lobby is zoomed whole, the lines with it).
**Compound's roofs go in Command mode** (b1105bw, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "When in command mode, hide the building rooftops in map \"Compound\". Apply to PT and 9.5.") — GLB_MAPS compound `roof`: Object_4 (the sheds' sawtooth and corrugated roofs and the front shelters'), Object_27 (the long hall's and the middle block's), Object_15 (the hut's by the north lot) and Object_35 (the middle block's lid under its roof), 8 meshes with their fade copies, hidden in Command mode by the roof rule every map has (`updateMapRoof`; on PT photo mode follows its panel). Compound's objects are the meshes themselves and `fadeNodes` adds each one's fade copy beside it, not under it, so the name lists missed the copies: the copy now carries `userData.fadeSrc`, its source, and the `roof` and `cmdClear` lists match a copy by its source (Neon's copies sit under their nodes and are found as before). The middle block's upper floor (Object_16, 27.6 u) is walked and stays: the nav grid's reachable floors on Compound top out at 27.64 u, and of its 356 reachable nodes at 8 u or higher none stands on one of the four (a ray down from each).
**Town keeps its whole model in Command mode** (b1105bv, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "Town does not need this treatment") — GLB_MAPS town `cmdClear: false`: a map that says so is left out of `updateCmdClear`, rule and list alike, so Town's 2 meshes stay. The costs measured (headless Chromium, software GL, this build): the per-frame check under 0.1 µs; Neon's list looked up at load, 0.3 ms; the list built on a map's first entry into Command mode, once, 5.5 to 12.8 ms with every bounding box still to compute (Dust 5.5, Neon 6.4, Compound 9.1, Hospital 12.8 with its million vertices) and 0.9 to 5.7 ms with them computed; the hidden parts are not drawn, 112 meshes and 47,270 triangles fewer on Neon, 100 and 8,495 on Dust, 8 and 9,327 on Compound. A match started in Command mode never drew them, so the first switch to Shooter uploads their buffers then (Neon 97 geometries and 3 textures more in that frame than when they had been drawn once, Dust 75 and none), with no new shader program and no longer frame in the test; a Shooter start uploads them at load as before. Nothing is sent over the network.
**Command mode clears the view on the modelled maps** (b1105bu, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5; user: "Currently there are some maps where items outside the map blocking player's site in command mode (Blockade, Dust etc.). I want those objects be temporarily hidden in command mode", then "自動規則加手動清單" and "implement in PT first") — main.js `updateCmdClear` (called from `updateMapRoof`, every frame, acting only when the mode changes): in Command mode a modelled map hides, by rule, every mesh lying wholly outside the collision area and rising more than 2 m over the street, and by hand the meshes under the nodes its GLB_MAPS `cmdClear` lists; Shooter shows them again, each as visible as it was (on PT photo mode too). Neon lists 56 tower nodes, every part starting 12 m or more over the street (112 meshes; the highest floor a unit can reach there, from both spawns over the nav grid's walks and links, is 51.3 u, the lowest hidden part starts at 52.6 u, so no floor anyone stands on goes). The rule takes Dust's floating skyline (100 meshes), 2 meshes on Town and 8 on Compound; District, Spacecraft, Warehouse, Hospital and the Backrooms have none outside. Visibility only: the collision, the bots, the shots and the online sync are unchanged. Its own userData key, `cmdClear`: `userData.cmdHide` is the older flag on the built maps' airborne dressing, which `applyDioramaDressing` hides wherever it sits in arenaDecor.
**A Command unit under a move order defends for 0.3 s** (b1105bt, 2026-11-07, PT and 9.5 together; user: "In command mode, when a unit is given an assigned location, change these 4 durations from 1sec to 0.3sec for that assigned unit", then "Both" (the way there and the 40 s guard) and "No. Keep SD brain untouched") — nbrain.js `BOT_DEFENSE_CMD_MOVE_MS` = 300: while a unit holds a move order, its travel and its anchor alike, the four Defense commits (a hit, a hit under the locked sniper's charge, the anti-glint dodge's follow-through, the vestigial stuck one) run 300 ms instead of 1,000, so the escape across the line hands the frame back to the order sooner; a hit's Defense is then about 0.65 s with the 0.35 s evade window before it, where it was about 1.35 s. A lock-only order, a unit without orders and Sudden Death keep 1,000 ms. Assessed before the build (a Command unit ordered to the enemy's spawn under an unkillable machine gun, 8 runs a map): the time in Defense 20 to 40% shorter, the units that arrived there sooner (21 to 13 s, 25 to 17 s), more damage taken on the way. The equivalence matches carry no orders and played equal to the b1105br references after the change; re-frozen at b1105bt.

## Sudden Death

Picked as the **rules mode** beside the view mode (**Health Point** is the normal game; the two were named Normal / Classic until 2026-10-10). Everyone fights at **1 HP** — one landed round decides — and Trio respawns arrive at 1 HP too. The two snipers (Aru, Kei) are out of the pickers and the Random cards. The match opens on a full-width **SUDDEN DEATH** banner with the simulation frozen behind it; the 3 s spawn immunity and every bot slot's 650 ms first-shot delay count from the banner's end (the server holds the same way online).

**The Sudden Death bot brain** — one module for both hosts, `shared/src/sim/sdbrain.js`: the server's `tickBot` and the offline client's `updateEnemy` each build a small host and call `sdBrainTick` once a tick, for every bot slot offline and online, 1v1, 2v2, Trio and the spectated player slot; there is no second copy and no switch. Rewritten on 2026-10-16 (b1017a–h) as **one rule in four parts, each written once**, after the earlier brain had grown to 3,000 lines and 144 knobs of special cases: the unit stands only where it is hidden; it is exposed only to buy something — a firing line, a better cell, the way back; every exposure is priced the same way, and the cheapest one that buys the most is taken. 1,000 lines, 44 knobs (`BOT_SD`), nothing per weapon or per map; the enemy's magazine is never read, its shots are.

1. **Hidden** — one test of the body's silhouette (the front of the capsule, both shoulders, the four shoulder corners, the top) against every **eye**: each enemy where it stands, where its run takes it in the next 0.5 s, and where it fired **every round still in the air** (a shot log per enemy, each shot kept for a round's life — a sprinting sprayer has three or four rounds in the air at once, each from a different place). Hidden means no eye reaches any point of the silhouette.
2. **The price** — the chance of dying in an exposure of a given length at a given distance, moving across the line, under the enemy's reaction: none while its stream is on, 250 ms while its gun is aimed (it fired within 2 s), 600 ms when it is not, or the median of its last five measured reactions, whichever is faster; with its live bloom and cadence, the shotguns by their real cloud. Against it, the chance the unit's own rounds land in the same window. An attack is taken when the deaths it costs to land one hit stay under the one cap, **0.15**; a route, when its death chance does.
3. **The plan** — hidden with nothing under way, the unit prices **the Engage Run** (a straight run across the line through a firing point to a hidden end), **the Stand Peek** (a leg to a firing point, the window, the way back: inside **30 u** the leg is the dodge itself, **the Close Peek**, beyond it a sprint with the dodge kept for the way out) and a **relocation** (a hidden cell by a priced route: closer while beyond the unit's reach, a line-capable cell inside it, else any cell nearer), and takes the cheapest under the cap. Every option refused, it asks what it is waiting for, priced once more with what time brings back — the gun gone quiet, the dodge in hand, the tank full: something under the cap then makes the plan **the Wait** (pacing its cell inside a 6 u leash, priced again every 250–500 ms); nothing makes it **the Approach**, the pathfinder's route to the enemy, priced like any route. The cell it waits in is read at the horizon as well: seen by then, **the Escape** — the nearest cell hidden through the horizon, by a priced route, before the line opens. Caught exposed with no plan, **the Reaction**: the dodge across the line on the tick the line opens, then the sprint to the nearest hidden cell.
4. **The legs** — hidden, along the route; exposed, the crossing legs, always across the line, the side flipped only by a wall (physics' note of the face it pushed the body out of). A peek's window runs across the line away from its cover and ends the tick its way out would cost the cap. The brain and the gun keep running through the dodge; only the body's velocity stands down for the step.

**The unit's own reaction** (b1017f–g): the trigger opens **250 ms** after the unit *knew* — an attack's take (the Stand Peek and the Engage Run walk it into sight: a leg longer than the reaction fires the tick its line opens, a shorter one waits out the rest), else the first sighting of its silhouette. The hosts take this trigger as the Sudden Death bot's only trigger — the plain brain's bloom gate does not apply — and the own-hit price counts rounds from the same moment, so a window too short for its first round is refused rather than walked.

**The tank** (b1017d, b1017h): a reserve of **86** (a dodge, 48, and half a second of lateral sprint) times the map's boost scale — **430** on District and Compound, some nine dodges, the dodge and the sprint being costs, which the scale leaves alone — is kept through every planned exposure, which starts only with its own cost on top of it; the body exposed spends to the floor, 8 times the scale (40 on District and Compound). Hidden legs sprint through **the valve** — the tank is spent in one draught and refilled in one: the valve shuts when the gauge is down to the reserve plus the longest peek's sprint and opens again only at a full gauge. (A hidden leg used to sprint a tick every few hundred ms as the gauge refilled, each tick resetting the 500 ms refill pause, so the gauge never rose while the unit walked and attacks went out on a third of a tank.) Defensive spending — the dodge, the crossing legs, the retreat, the escape — does not pass the valve.

**Reach** — the one per-unit number the brain still reads: how far the unit's own gun lands every round on a standing target (the base cone's footprint fits the hit radius, and a 0 cone, every unit's but Aris's since b1105fw, reads 200; a shotgun's lock range stands in; `sdReach` in the unit data overrides the formula). Attacks are searched inside the reach plus 10 u of slack; beyond it the relocation closes in. The engage floor and the band of the earlier brain are gone: a route's or an attack's safety is its price, not a distance.

| Unit | Reach |
|---|---|
| Saori, Asuna, Subaru, Minori, Koyuki, Maki | 80 (set) |
| Hoshino, Haruka, Rabu | 80 (their lock range) |
| Aris | 160 (her 0.02 base cone) |
| Atsuko, Marina, Yuuka, Kurumi, Hina, Fubuki, Iori, Aru, Kei, Hiyori | 200 (a 0 base cone; from 53 to 160 by their cones until b1105fw) |

**Measured** (headless, the shared sim driven tick by tick): against the brain it replaced, 63% of decided duels at b1017e and about 50% once the unit's own reaction was in (the old brain reacts in 0 ms); the wall-hugger scan on flashpoint (the human 1.2 u off every obstacle face) 29–30 of 31 kills in 40 s; 10 ms of CPU per bot per second (was 14). The equivalence test (`shared/test/sdbrain_equiv.test.js`) plays the live brain against a frozen copy tick for tick on seeded matches, so a port between lines changes nothing. **F9** in the client prints the Sudden Death report to the console — the plans taken, the pricings refused, the rounds fired, and the last five seconds as a tape — and a bot's death prints the same with the killing round's geometry.

**Open observations** (2026-10-17, from playtest death reports; none changed yet — a single report never justifies a change, and every change must be a rule, not a patch):

- **The Reaction priced, not ordered.** Caught exposed, the unit always dodges and then runs to the nearest hidden cell; it never chooses to stand and fire. Three deaths in narrow corridors came at the turn-around right after the dodge, the body passing back through the point the gun was already on. The run and the stand should be priced by the same function (the route's exposure against the trade, deaths per hit) and the cheaper taken; a fixed order would fight where three steps reach cover.
- **The dodge's side and the run's side.** The dodge takes the side with the longer open run; the escape search then finds the nearest hidden cell, usually back where the unit came from, so the body reverses. Candidate: among the sides with room, the one toward the cell it last stood hidden in.
- **A gun is aimed when it has seen the unit, not only when it has fired.** A hunter who sprints without firing reads as a cold gun (600 ms), so escape routes with exposed stretches price low; he fires the moment the unit lands. The brain already knows when it was last seen.
- **A route under way keeps its first price.** Only the Approach is re-priced at the cadence; a relocation's exposed stretches are priced once, and a sprinting enemy changes them within a second. Candidate: every route re-priced from where the body is, at the cadence, like the Approach.
- **The Approach's route and the price of waiting.** When every option is refused, the Approach prices the shortest path, which crosses the open; the safest path (the exposure-weighted search the relocations already use) would pass more often. If stalemates remain, waiting needs a price of its own — the longer the duel stays undecided, the dearer the wait — compared against the safest route by the same function. Needs a stuck report (F9) first.
- **The crossing legs lean 45° toward their goal** (`reactDriftDeg`), against the rule that every sprint runs across the line. Held on the owner's word.
- **The jump walks the whole route ahead of it** regardless of the tank; a route with a jump link is walked while the gauge is full.
- **The dead-end pocket** on flashpoint, N(112, 81.35): the grid has no cell there and the Approach's straight last leg clips a corner. A map change, held.
- Two guards that cannot fire (`taken ?? now`, `Array.isArray` on the shot log) to be deleted with the next change; one report with a geometry the tape cannot explain (a perpendicular dodge whose ends both sit 1.1 u from the killing round's line).

**The one-brain experiment** (2026-10-17): the Sudden Death brain priced in life — a hit costs damage / HP of a life, so one brain and one price serve both modes — replaced the plain brain, was measured (against the plain brain: 69% wins, a 30.8 s median kill against 16.3 s, five times the CPU) and was shelved the same day: the plain brain is cheap, predictable and the easier game. It is archived on branch `Testing_0.9.0_New-BOT-Brain` (`docs/NEW_BOT_BRAIN.md` there: the principle, the structure, the reports, the pending designs). The plain brain here is that work's first two steps: its dead code deleted and one module for both hosts (`shared/src/sim/nbrain.js`), behaviour unchanged.


### Free pov (Shooter mode, b1027c, user 2026-10-30)

Shooter mode has two points of view, switched any time without the pause menu: `i` on the desktop, two quick taps on the empty screen on a phone (ignored while Command mode stands). **Angled pov** is the classic one: the chase camera frames the unit and its lock, every shot is aimed at the lock. **Free pov** keeps the angled pov's own camera rig (13 behind, 6.8 up, a shoulder to the right, looking at the mid-point to the lock, the field of view by the lock's distance — with the crosshair level the two cameras stand and look alike at every range, b1027i), turned to the aim and swung by its pitch about the unit itself (b1027j: swung about the far look point, a glance down lifted the camera into a bird's view), snapped with no lag, and a **crosshair** at the screen centre says where the rounds go: the shot is the line from the muzzle to the point under the crosshair (the first wall, or the enemy body the camera ray crosses before it), so it holds even up close. The player aims the crosshair. Desktop: the mouse (the pointer lock when the page's frame allows it, otherwise a drag with a button held), left click fires; `Shift` sprints (a double-tap locks it), `Q` dodges, `E` switches the lock, beside the older K / L / U (b1027f). Phone: a swipe on the empty screen, or a drag on the fire button, the sniper's charge included. Sensitivity (`window.__freePov.cfg`, live): 0.25°/px for a swipe or a fire-button drag (a landscape phone's width ≈ 200°), 0.10°/px for the mouse, pitch −50°…+60°. The lock is kept but not fired at: the unit turns to the crosshair while it fires or charges (the crosshair's own direction, not the shot's line, which leaves the muzzle a few degrees off it when the aimed point is near — b1027k) and otherwise faces the way it moves, holding its heading when still (b1027f), there is no red lock and no homing, the lock bracket hides, every live enemy off the frame has its screen-edge triangle — the one locked before the switch too, and a 1v1's one enemy (b1105fv, 2026-11-11; until then only the enemy not locked had one, as in angled pov, so the locked one was lost once it left the screen) — and the crosshair is Counter-Strike's classic — four lines, 8 px long and 2 px thick with a dark edge (user 2026-11-02, "the traditional counter-strike style crosshair instead of circle crosshair, and keep the bloom expand effect", the first of four samples; a ring until then) — their inner ends on the rounds' cone drawn on the screen (the base spread plus the bloom, as the rounds sample it, so a burst pushes the lines out; a shotgun's cone is fixed at the small base size every gun opens with — b1027h, user 2026-10-30: its rounds are one rigid pattern, not a cone; never under 12 px, the lines 6 px from the centre; b1027e, user 2026-10-30: the camera rig, the facing, the ring and the keys), and one green whatever it sits on (user 2026-11-02, "remove the 'cross hair becomes red when pointing at enemy' effect"; red over an enemy's body from b1027c, the first free pov build, until then). A round hits whichever enemy it meets (each tick its target is the living enemy nearest its flight: the sim's `retargetFreeAim`, the client's mirror); it still carries an *intent target*, the enemy nearest the aim line, for the bookkeeping, and a sniper charge is marked with it. Its glint warns **every** enemy: bots react as if aimed at, online every human enemy sees it. Back in angled pov the lock goes to the enemy nearest the crosshair — the smallest angle off the camera's centre ray; two within half a degree, the nearer one (b1027l, user 2026-10-30; before, the nearest enemy). Online the frame carries `freeAim`, `aimYaw`, `aimPitch` and `lockTarget` (the picked slot; the server takes it only for a living enemy), and the server fires along the aim it is sent. The TARGET button hides while the player's own free pov stands — no lock to switch — and a spectator keeps it to change the watched unit; a spectator's own `i` does nothing — the spectator-mode watcher, a dead player watching a teammate and an online spectator socket alike (b1027o) — and the lock bracket follows the watched unit's pov — shown while it is in angled pov, gone while it free-aims (b1027n, user 2026-10-30); watching a unit in free pov, the spectator sees that unit's crosshair drawn where its aim line lands on the spectator's own screen (the snapshot carries a free-aiming unit's `aimYaw`/`aimPitch`, three decimals, only while it free-aims; b1027m, user 2026-10-30). Universal: every map, every unit, offline and online alike.

## Command mode

The alternative to the classic chase camera (2026-08-21): a tilt-shift **diorama overview** of the whole map where the units fight on the standard bot brain and you play the commander — observe, select, order. The picker is a Shooter / Command chip in the menus; like Duel / 1v1 the choice is session-only — every site open starts Shooter. Offline, `V` or the pause menu also toggles it mid-match, and solo 2v2 play commands **both** of team one's units; online the pick happens in the queue room, is final once the match starts, and commands your own unit **plus a bot-filled teammate slot** (2026-08-22 — human teammates are never commandable; details in Command mode online below).

**Orders** — three kinds, layered on top of the bot rather than replacing it:

- **Move order** — tap your unit (selection glow), then tap anywhere on the map; or drag from the unit and release. Where floors stack (a bridge deck over ground), **hold** the tap to cycle the vertical layer — the stack lists only standable floors, and the preview auto-picks the lowest reachable one. The unit pathfinds to the spot, **fighting along the way but never abandoning the destination** — travel is **pathfinder-guided the whole way** (2026-08-22): the route re-plans from the unit's current position after every combat reflex and on a 1.5 s cadence, and the unit **vaults the route's jump-links** (raised platforms, fence gaps) instead of grinding at the ledge. On arrival it holds the area in an Engage-style orbit anchored on a **radius-12 ring** for **40 s**, then returns to full autonomy; the orbit **turns around at walls** (a ring straddling a fence — Airport's rim glass, say — patrols the reachable arc instead of grinding the pane), and a unit knocked out of the area by a Defense escape **returns as a fresh pathfinder-guided travel leg**, resuming whatever remains of the 40 s window. An unreachable spot draws a red **"Area is not available"** note at the tap and keeps your selection for an immediate retry. The standing ring is also a live handle (2026-08-27, nothing selected): **grab it anywhere on its disc and drag** to move the order (the whole circle plus a finger-sized margin counts at any zoom — a tap inside the area still orders a *selected* unit there, since ring grabs only engage with nothing selected) — the ring brightens in its unit's color the moment you pick it up (overlapping rings resolve to the nearer center; a dead tie hands it to your own unit), the same preview / validation / layer rules apply, and releasing re-issues the order with a **fresh 40 s window** (any drag counts, even released back where it started — there is no in-place cancel; an unreachable release just draws the red note and leaves the old order untouched) — or **double-tap the ring** to withdraw the move order alone (a standing force lock survives).
- **Force lock** — with your unit selected, tap an enemy: the unit locks that target until **either party dies**, then falls back to its own target-finding. The pinned enemy wears corner **triangles in the commanding unit's color** (the X layout; when two allied commanders pin the same enemy, the later lock wears the **+** edge-midpoint layout instead of splitting corners). Tapping the same enemy again cancels the lock; with **nothing selected**, tapping the pinned enemy's marker or card releases **every lock your units hold on it** at once (2026-08-27 — covers the bot teammate without selecting it; a human teammate's lock is never yours to drop).
- **Hide** (2026-09-26) — **select** your unit (tap its marker or its card — the selection glow) and a shield appears centred just above its **info card** (below it at the top edge) for as long as the selection stands; **tap the shield** to hide. It reads lit while the unit already hides. No hold anywhere. **Hide** wipes the unit's move and lock orders; **any new move or lock order releases it** (the order then stands as usual), as does a double-tap on the unit. A manual reload of 3 s or more enters Hide on its own for the reload (see *Reload hide* under the bot brain) — unless a move order stands, in which case it runs a Cover Hide and keeps the order. A hiding unit's first priority is line of sight: every tick it checks whether **both live enemies** can see it; if they can, it walks the shortest route (a walk-only Dijkstra over the nav grid, no distance cap, capped at 600 cells per search, at most one search per 0.5 s per unit and one per tick per match) to the nearest cell hidden from both. Once hidden it never stands still (owner: a statue looks awful): it **paces** its cover at walk speed on short legs that are verified hidden *before* they are taken (a 2.5 u look-ahead eye test against every enemy eye plus the walk rule), on a 6 u leash around the spot, biased away from the nearest enemy, re-picking its heading every 0.35 s. It also **watches the enemies move**: each is projected 0.5 s ahead on its live velocity, and an eye that would see the unit from there — or a nearest enemy inside 26 u closing at more than 2 u/s — triggers a **slip** to a cell hidden from the live *and* predicted eyes (when closing, one at least 6 u farther from that enemy), before the unit is ever seen. Searches run one attempt per tick (the fallback tiers follow on the next ticks) on the same 0.5 s cadence, so the per-tick cost stays at a handful of sight tests. It **does fire back** whenever a shot exists from where it stands, and never moves for a shot. Being hit does **not** trigger Defense while hiding (hit-stun still applies; the anti-glint dodge and the sniper charge rules are untouched) — **unless no spot hidden from both enemies exists within the search budget** (2026-09-27: the nearest-enemy-only fallback is gone — a walking target hidden from one enemy measured worse than Defense): then it fights on its pursue legs, a fresh hit runs the plain Defense escape, and it keeps searching every 0.5 s until such a spot appears. Sprints on the way are latched: a dash arms only above **50 boost** and may spend down to the **8-boost** hard floor (the escape sprint latch — since b1104b the one rule of every escape sprint: Defense's and the cover move's too) (times the map's boost scale — 250 and 40 on District and Compound). Released, the unit is plain autonomy again — no badge. Online the order travels as `order:stance` (same per-unit 0.5 s rate limit), lives in the team-scoped command side-table like the other two, and never reaches the enemy; a landed `order:move` / `order:lock` releases it server-side.
- One command per selection — the glow drops as soon as an order lands. **Double-tap** your unit to clear both orders at once; a slow re-tap just deselects.
- **Cover Hide** (2026-09-26, reload-only since 2026-09-27) — a unit under a move order spends a **long manual reload (3 s+)** in a hide **bound to the order**: it looks for cover from **both** enemies **inside the order's area first** (14 u around the ordered point; a unit still travelling has no area yet and skips that tier), then the nearest such cover anywhere, sprints there like Defense (down to the 8-boost floor — 40 at the scale of 5, District's and Compound's — then a walk until the gauge is back over 50, 250 at that scale: the escape sprint latch, b1104b; until then no arming), paces once hidden, and ends with the reload — after which the order resumes as after any reflex (travel replans, an anchored unit walks back to its ring; the 40 s hold clock keeps running). No cover anywhere: the reload is spent on the order's legs and Cover Hide is not retried for 0.5 s. No badge, the move order stands throughout, and a new move order landing mid-reload takes over at once. **A fresh hit under a move order runs plain Defense** — the hit-triggered Cover Hide measured about half of Defense's survival time in bot duels on Factory, Streets and Airport (the hidden unit paces at walk speed, stops firing back, and two routing enemies re-acquire it within seconds), so it was retired on 2026-09-27.
- **Reflexes always preempt orders**: Defense escapes, the anti-sniper dodge, the reload hide, hit-stun and the rest interrupt exactly as in autonomous play, and the route replans from wherever the unit ends up.

**Commanded travel dashes on the normal stamina economy** — no speed cheat. Dash segments are *latched*: a segment only starts once boost reaches **125** (or the unit's cap if lower), spends down to a **50-boost reserve floor**, then the unit walks until the gauge re-arms. Route jumps fund at the flat **mandated tier — 60 boost** (2026-08-22, shared with the Defense survival hop; the bot's discretionary jumps fund from its 200 travel floor since 2026-09-29 — 250 before — which the 50↔125 dash cycle could never reach), and while a jump lies ahead on the route the dash floor rises to **70** so the unit always arrives at the ledge able to afford the hop. Cap, drain and regen are the human values throughout, and every level here is times the map's boost scale — at District's and Compound's 5: the arm 625, the floor 250, the mandated tier 300, the floor with a jump ahead 350, the bot's travel floor 1,000; combat reflexes keep their own funding rules (Defense may still spend through the floor to survive).

**Status icons** — gold, on both the unit's marker and its info card: **`!`** while a move order stands (en route plus the 40 s anchor), an **eye** while force-locking (pairing with the colored triangles on the enemy); both side by side when both are active, a **shield** while hiding (which by construction has neither; on the marker it sits centred above the square, or above the diamond's top point), no icon when the unit is autonomous. On the info card the icons are 14×12 px (11×9.5 compact) since 2026-09-26 — the 20×17 originals hung below the card frame once the portrait grew the name row.

**Camera & HUD** — free camera: drag empty ground to pan, pinch / wheel to zoom, `Q`/`E` / right-mouse drag / two-finger twist to rotate about the screen centre; the tilt-shift blur keeps the miniature look with a clear pocket around every unit. Info cards dock in fixed corners — your team bottom-left, enemies bottom-right — each carrying HP and a **stamina bar** (the enemy's stamina bar is hidden online). The joystick, action buttons and the bottom-center stamina bar are gone: fire / dodge / jump / sprint are bot decisions, and each unit's stamina lives on its info card. On Factory, Lobby, Station, Airport and Flashpoint the tall airborne dressing (pipes, trusses, signage, ceiling grids) hides while command mode stands and returns in classic — visual only, nothing collidable. **Lighting is the map's own** (b1027b, user 2026-10-30, "the map looks yellowish in command mode"): the diorama used to re-light every map in a warm daylight grade (Lobby and Airport excepted since 2.1g), which turned grey streets and walls yellowish; command mode now keeps each map's normal lights and sets only its pale background, the fog push-out and the camera planes.

### Command mode online

- **Queue-room pick, hidden from opponents.** The Shooter / Command chip sits above the roster in the waiting room; the pick locks when the match starts (no mid-match toggle online) and is never sent to the other team — only teammates see it, as the gold **[CMD]** roster tag. Behavior will still hint it; accepted.
- **Your unit — and your bot teammate (2026-08-22).** A commander drives their own unit and, when the teammate slot was **bot-filled at match start**, that bot too (same tap/drag orders; the bot's lock triangles wear its own slot color; each unit has its own 0.5 s order rate limit). Human teammates are never commandable, and the commandable set freezes at match start — a mid-match disconnect turns that human's unit into a plain bot, but it is not adopted.
- **Server-authoritative orders.** The commander's client sends dedicated order messages; the server re-validates each one against the same pathfind checks (with a 500 ms rate limit per ordered unit — your own unit and the bot teammate throttle separately, and clears are never rate-limited) and answers with an ack the selection glow waits for. Commander clients skip movement prediction entirely — the unit is server-driven like any bot, and command timers run on the server clock.
- **Teammate share (2v2).** A command teammate sees your full annotations (destination ring, icons, lock triangles) in their own diorama. A **classic** teammate gets an in-world render: a ground ring in the commander's slot color standing at your unit's ordered destination, plus your lock triangles framing just outside their own crosshair when they face the pinned enemy. Both indicators die with the commander's unit.
- **Information hiding.** Snapshots are filtered per team: the enemy's boost value, standing orders and bot-intent state never reach your client. Boost-inference tells (overheat, sprint-lock, thruster effects) deliberately stay visible. Spectators watch in classic view with no command overlays.
- **Disconnects.** 1v1 keeps the instant forfeit; in 2v2 a disconnected commander's orders clear and the unit fights on as a plain bot.

## Units

Twenty pickable units (Mika, Unit 8, is hidden from every picker), near-identical base stats (100 HP — Kurumi 120; 250 boost; 11.76 sprint base; the walk speed is per unit since 2026-09-24, in the table). The tables are 9.6's as of b1105fv (2026-11-11), every cell checked against the sim's unit table (`shared/src/sim/constants.js`, online) and the client's (`client/src/main.js`, offline), which agree:

Reading the DPS table: the shotgun rows are the most theoretical — all 8 pellets only land point-blank, and at their 80 lock range the pattern is fully open; the 7-shell magazine burns in ~1.7 s before per-shell regen throttles the long run, where Rabu's box magazine keeps 76.9. Aris's burst is real for the 8-bolt spike (96 damage in ~2.4 s), then collapses to the worst sustained figure in the game. The sniper rows use cycle math (the 1 s cooldown + the 0.5 s floor charge) at full range-tier damage. Hina's 83.3 tops the automatics by a wide margin; eight automatics sit between 40 and 55 — Atsuko, Marina, Asuna, Minori, Yuuka, Saori, Subaru, Koyuki — where fights are decided by accuracy curves, uptime and positioning rather than raw DPS, and Maki and Kurumi sit under them. The marksman rifles sit under that band on purpose: Fubuki's and Aris's 336 ms slot and Iori's forced 0.75 s cooldown trade cadence for the heaviest per-shot chunks below the snipers.

Unit 13 is the lightest bullet in the game on Hina's cadence: the 48 ms tick slot (20.8 shots/s) in an SMG chassis and a 71-round drum — ~3.4 s of continuous fire (≈178 damage per drum) behind a 2 s reload. Suppression-first: her value is steady chip on the longest trigger in the SMG class, plus the boost her stream forces targets to burn escaping; the kill usually needs cross-fire or a second drum. Since 0.5.9 the SMG spread profiles follow the real guns; from the 2026-09-22 bloom port they did it through the base cone (Marina's PPSh kept a 0.06 base, Atsuko's EVO3 came down to 0.03), and since b1105fw, every base 0, through the cap alone: the WWII PPSh hoses wide, blooming to 0.11, the modern EVO3 shoots tight, blooming to 0.08.

Units 14 and 15 (user 2026-10-22) are Saori's and Atsuko's chassis with guns of their own. Subaru's HK33 is the M4 with 0.005 bloom per shot: the cap at the 13th round, where the M4's 30-round magazine ends one round short of it (from the 0.02 base until b1105fw, the 9th and the 21st), recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s from 200 ms after the last round (the M4's 0.035/s until 2026-10-24; until b1105fx a shooter standing still added no bloom, so a planted Subaru had the M4's cone exactly). Yuuka's MPX is the evo3 with 850 label RPM and 0.01 bloom per shot: a 70.6 ms cooldown lands on the 80 ms tick slot, Asuna's cadence — 64 and 80 ms are the only slots near — so she fires 12.5/s to Atsuko's 15.6, and her cone reaches the cap at the 9th round where the evo3's takes 28 (the 6th and the 18th from the 0.03 base until b1105fw), recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s from 200 ms after the last round (the SMGs' 0.05/s until 2026-10-24). Since b1105di (2026-11-08) the picker groups the units by class, so both sit with their classes.

Unit 17 (user 2026-10-23): Kurumi is the shield bearer, the only unit off the 100 HP base — **120 HP** (125 until 2026-10-24), her bars as long as her HP (the corner bar, the overhead pill and the command card are 1.2 of the 100 HP base length: `hpBarScale`, a unit's `hp` over the base; user 2026-10-24), and she is **never stunned** (`stunImmune`, a unit field read by the one hit-stun routine, `applyHitStun`: the hit's damage and momentum cut stand, the slow does not). The price is the UMP45's kit: a walk of 10 (Koyuki's tier), the shotguns' reach (80 since b1105eg doubled every lock range; 40 / 50 until then), 600 RPM on the 112 ms slot (8.9 rounds/s) with Atsuko's 3.5 bullet (31.3 burst DPS, 18.7 sustained — still the lowest of the automatics), a 25-round magazine on a 2 s reload, and a cone of 0 (0.03 until b1105fw) that opens by 0.015 a round to Marina's 0.11 (sure-hit ∞ → 29), recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s from 200 ms after the last round — frozen while she sprays (112 ms a round), back from the cap to the base in 0.65 s after (0.47 s to the 0.03 base) (the SMGs' 0.05/s until 2026-10-24; no delay, and 0.3/s, for an hour the same day). (The **reload hold** — `reloadHolds`, a unit field: no walk and no sprint while the magazine is changed, the dodge and the jump open, the same for a player and for the bot — came and went twice the same day; the field stays for any unit to carry.) Her rounds stun as the rifles' do (100 ms at 0.25). Sudden Death puts her at 1 HP like everyone, so she still dies to the first hit there. (Her HP and bullet were tuned through the day of 2026-10-23 — 150 / 3.5, 100, 150 / 2.5 — on player feedback, "a bloody tank" at 150, and settled at 125 / 3.5: a 30-round rifle magazine, 120 damage, still falls 5 short of her, and she kills a 100 HP target in one magazine with 4 rounds to spare.) Her pictures are the user's. (Unit 16, Rio's M1911, was added and removed the same day; the key is left unused.)

Unit 18 (user 2026-10-23): Rabu's M590 is Haruka's SDASS — the concentrated pattern, the 250 RPM, the shotguns' reach (80 since b1105eg; 40 / 50 until then) — on a **10-shell box magazine** that reloads as one in 2 s where the other shotguns load shell by shell: ten blasts in 2.2 s (320 damage point-blank, 76.9 sustained DPS against the shell-loaders' 26.7), then a 2 s gap with nothing in the gun. The bot empties the magazine in one pull (`botFireCap` 10, as Marina's drum; the shell-loaders fire 4 and rest). The only shotgun on the magazine rule; the key after Kurumi's. Her pictures are the user's.

Units 19 to 22 (2026-11-08, PT then 9.5) each grew from a unit already in the game. **Hiyori** (Unit 19, NTW-20) is Aru's sniper with a 3-round magazine, a walk of 10 (12 until b1105db) and tiers of her own on Aru's 15 / 50 zones: 60 / 40 / 20. **Iori** (Unit 20, KAR98K) is Fubuki's rifle with a bolt-action beat: a forced 0.75 s cooldown after every shot (the snipers' ring on the fire button), a 5-round magazine of 13-damage rounds on a 2 s reload, and Fubuki's accuracy, bloom, spindle and ribbon trail. **Maki** (Unit 21, RM338) is a machine gun on Koyuki's reach, bullet and bot rules: 100 rounds of 4.5 (5 until b1105dt) at 500 RPM — the 128 ms slot — and a 0 cone (0.02 until b1105fw) opening by 0.003 a round to 0.06, recovering at 0.045/s, so her capped cone keeps a 53 u sure-hit line. **Minori** (Unit 22, AKM) is Saori's chassis with the AKM's numbers: 5 damage (4.5 from b1105dt, 5 again since b1105eg), 600 RPM on the 112 ms slot, 0.015 bloom a round to a 0.11 cap recovering at the rifles' 0.17/s, and the autos' 33%-hit gate, which since the doubled ranges closes inside her lock (see the measured hit rates).

**Reading the stun column** (`duration @ move-scale`): every landed hit slows the victim's movement to *move-scale* for *duration* — e.g. `100 ms @ 0.25` means crawling at 25% speed for 100 ms. A new hit's stun takes over when none is running or when it is heavier (a lower scale); an equal or lighter hit during a running stun leaves it as it is. The slow is on the unit's whole movement — its own walk or sprint speed and the momentum a sprint banks from it (the hit also cuts what was banked) — so a stunned sprinting unit runs at the move-scale of its sprint, player or bot, offline or online (b1104e, user 2026-11-04). Until then a bot banked the sprint's momentum from its unslowed speed and the stun never reached that part of its speed: online always, offline since b1104d (before that the offline client slowed the summed speed, as a player's). A player and a bot also jump and stop sprinting by the same rules (b1104f, user 2026-11-04, "make player unit aligned with BOT"): a jump banks 0.7 of the speed the body had as the tick began, a sprint's carried part included — a player's had banked only the tick's input speed (a jump out of a sprint launched at 20, a bot's at 32) — and a sprint's end walks on at the walk speed, where a player had walked at 4.55 for 180 ms. Since b1104g a bot's jump also goes off at the speed it moves at that tick, as a player's: the sprint's base when it sprints, the walk when it walks or stands (user 2026-11-04, "make BOT aligned with player unit" — it had launched at the sprint's base whatever it was doing, so its jump from a walk went off at the sprint's base, 11.76, instead of its walk — 10 to 16 by unit, Saori's 12). **In practice the slow itself is a minor stat**: sprinting pays straight through it (written 2026-08-01, while a bot's sprint carried through the stun online; since b1104e a stunned sprint runs at the move-scale too, but a stun lasts only its column's 100 ms or less, so the sprint is back at full speed as soon as the hits stop — and everyone sprints away from fire anyway, stun or not), while a walking target was already highly hittable — so the currencies that actually decide fights are the damage actually landed and the boost the target burns escaping, not the movement penalty.

**Reading the spread columns (2026-09-22, spread bloom — ported from the demo line, owner questionnaire):** every cone angle is in radians and describes the FULL cone — a shot deviates from the aim line by at most half of it. Horizontal spread (HA) is gone from every unit. Instead each shot **blooms** the cone: firing adds *Bloom / shot* to the gun's spread, up to *SA cap*, and once the *Recovery* delay has passed since the last shot the extra spread drains back at the listed rate (the marksman rifles — Fubuki, Aris and Iori — recover between the shots of a burst too — *no delay* — which is what shapes them: Fubuki's second quick shot leaves at 0.043 rather than 0, Aris's at 0.063 rather than 0.02). Since angular error grows with distance, a cone has a **sure-hit distance** against a standing target — 3.2 ÷ spread, 3.2 being the hit capsule's width — inside which every shot lands; beyond it, hit chance falls off roughly as sure-hit ÷ distance. The table gives it at the base cone and at the cap: a fresh trigger pull is pin-point, a long spray is not, and tapping keeps a gun near its base numbers. Since b1105fw every base cone but Aris's is 0 and its sure-hit distance endless: a fresh trigger pull, or a recovered cone, puts its round on the aim line at any range, and what decides the hit is the target's drift during the round's flight (see the measured hit rates). **Standing still keeps no gun pin-point any more** (b1105fx, 2026-11-11; user: "Make bloom take effect in any firing condition"): every shot adds its *Bloom / shot*, standing, walking or sprinting, and the *Recovery* delay counts from the last shot in every case. Until then a shooter that had stood on the ground at under 1 unit/s for 200 ms fired without adding bloom, and whatever bloom it carried drained at the gun's recovery rate even while it kept firing — the price was being a stationary target; and since 2026-11-03 a sprint, even held in place or pressed into a wall, did not count as standing (sprint held with no direction had left the unit at speed 0 and its shots without bloom). The rule's stamp (`stillSince`, `tickStillness`, `isStill`) and its two numbers (`BLOOM_STILL_SPEED` 1, `BLOOM_STILL_DWELL_MS` 200) went with it, in the shared sim (online, and the client's prediction) and the offline client alike. The shotguns ignore the cones entirely: their pellets fly a fixed 8-point pattern that opens toward ~5.8 wide over the first 70 units of flight — at their 80 lock range it is fully open (it was a tight ~3.3-wide cluster at the old 40), and the volley dies at 150 (100 until b1105et); Hoshino's pattern is additionally stretched 1.4× horizontally (details below).

**Preferred engage distance:** every unit's fighting range is its **lock range ± 7** — the band where bots hold position, orbit, and fire: the shotguns and Kurumi 73–87, the other SMGs 93–107, the assault rifles 103–117, the machine guns 113–127, the marksman rifles 128–142, Aru and Hiyori 193–207, Kei 243–257 (since b1105et, 2026-11-10). One rule for all weapons: retune a lock range and the combat distance follows. **Since b1105eg (2026-11-09) it is one rule for both modes too**: every lock range was doubled and the 2v2 values were removed, so a 2v2 bot holds the same band as a 1v1 bot (until then a second, 2v2 number compressed the 2v2 bands into 50–70, so long-lock units would not hang back while a teammate died alone at the front). **Facing a shotgun** (b1105br): while a bot's current target is an enemy shotgun its band centres on at least 160 (`BOT_SHOTGUN_ENGAGE_RANGE`, 110 until b1105et; a longer lock range of its own stays, a shotgun bot keeps its own, Sudden Death is untouched) — every class but the snipers fights a shotgun at 153–167. The player-side lock reticle reads the unit's lock range.

**Measured hit rates** (re-measured 2026-11-11 on 9.7 b1105fw, every base cone 0 but Aris's 0.02, and Hina's row and panel again at b1105fz for her 0.16 cap and 0.06/s recovery and at b1105ga for her 150-round drum and her own 20% gate — nothing since b1105fw touches the other rows: b1105fx's standing-still change leaves the strafing shooter as it was, and a full run at b1105fz agrees with them within 2 points; every unit at its lock range and round speed as they stand: b1105et's lock ranges, b1105fd's and b1105fh's round speeds, and Hina gated since b1105fe; the run before, earlier the same day on 9.6 b1105fv, had the old base cones, and the runs before that, 2026-11-09 on b1105er and 2026-11-10 on b1105es, the older ranges and speeds as well — their numbers are kept under *Reading it*; the harness is `shared/tools/hitcurve.mjs`, the chart `shared/tools/hitcurve_png.py`, both in the tree, the whole run 19 s; two runs agree within 2 points, Aris's sprint row within 3 on her 8-round magazine). Once a cone grows with every round, one percentage no longer describes a gun: what matters is **how the hit chance decays across a magazine while the shooter is on the move**. So the protocol is a walking full-magazine spray, run on the shared sim (the code the server runs) on Plain Field — its boundary walls taken away for the run, since the machine guns' 120, the marksman rifles' 135 and the snipers' 200 and 250 reach the walls of the 240 × 240 field or past them — 200 runs per series:

- **Shooter:** strafes sideways at her own walk speed (16, 12 or 10 u/s; Hina 8), reversing every 400 ms so she is never still and every round blooms (since b1105fx a standing shooter's rounds bloom as well), and holds the trigger through **one whole magazine** (tap-fired for the shotguns and snipers; a charging sniper is rooted by the sim, as in play), **obeying the bot fire gate** (`botMayFire`). The marksman rifles' line — the 100% sure-hit line — has no end for Fubuki's and Iori's 0 base cone and stands at 160 for Aris's 0.02, beyond their 135 lock range, and closes far inside it after a round, so the three fire every round from a fully recovered cone, exactly as their bots do. Since the doubled ranges the autos' 33%-hit line (8.84 over the cone) of the two guns that open to 0.11 closes inside theirs too: Minori at 110 fires six rounds, from cones of 0 to 0.075, before her cone passes it and Marina at 100 thirty, from 0 to 0.087, and each then holds fire until the cone is fully recovered — 0.75 s and 2.0 s from a burst's last round to the next one's first (Kurumi's line, 80.4 at her 0.11 cap, sits right at her 80); Hina, on her own 20% line since b1105ga (the autos' 33% from b1105fe), fires thirty-two at 120, from 0 to 0.093, and holds about 1.8 s (twenty-five, from 0 to 0.072, with 1.5 s holds on the 33% line at b1105fz). Every other auto's line stays beyond its lock range even at the cap, and Koyuki and Maki are ungated. Every unit fires from **her own lock range** only.
- **Target:** a real unit — the 3.2-wide hit capsule, not the Range's signs — ping-ponging on a ±35° arc at lock range around the shooter, so it always moves across the line of fire, at three speeds: standing, the shooter's own walk speed, and that walk speed + 11.76 (the sprint boost). Reversal at the arc ends is instant, so the speed never dips. Shots aim at the target's current position, exactly as the game does (no lead).
- **Scoring:** every round is a hit or a miss (shotguns: pellets landed ÷ 8 per blast; Kei's beam counts as a round). Each curve is the hit chance of the *n*-th round of the magazine, averaged over the 200 runs; the numbers in the table are the mean over the whole magazine and, in brackets, the mean of its first third → last third.

*Shotgun rows:* Hoshino fires the 1.4× wide fan, Haruka the concentrated cloud (the two swapped patterns on 2026-10-10), and Rabu carries Haruka's pattern on her 10-shell magazine. At their 80 lock the pattern is fully open, so even a standing target takes about a third of the pellets — at the old 40 it was half open and Haruka's cloud landed 78%. b1105fw's 0 base took away the volley centre's small jitter (up to 0.64°); the three rows moved by a point at most. The picture is current (2026-11-11, b1105ga).

*Reading it:* the first→last gap is the bloom, and since b1105fw every cone but Aris's opens from 0, so a magazine's first rounds land every time on a standing target and on a walking one. **The assault rifles:** Saori and Asuna open a magazine at 100% on a standing target and close it at 72% and 60% — at 110 their capped 0.06 cone's sure-hit line (53) is about half the range — and Subaru, the fastest bloom, runs 96→58. **Minori's sawtooth** is her gate: at 110 she fires six rounds, from cones of 0 to 0.075, before her cone passes the 33% line, holds until it is fully recovered, and starts again — 81% on a standing target. **The SMGs** at 100: Atsuko 100→54 on a standing target, Yuuka 78→49, Marina 88→76 in bursts of thirty behind her gate. **The machine guns** at 120: Koyuki's cone reaches its cap at round 31 and sits there at ~26% — at 120 its capped cone's sure-hit line is 27 u — while Maki's 0.06 cap holds ~56%; **Hina**, on her own 20% line since b1105ga, fires her 150-round drum in bursts of thirty-two from cones of 0 to 0.093 with a hold of about 1.8 s between them, 73% on a standing target: the wider line spends more rounds from wider cones (80% at b1105fz, in bursts of twenty-five to 0.072 behind the autos' 33% line, whose 0.16 cap and 0.06/s recovery had shortened the holds from 2.7 s to 1.5 s; her cone never reaches the cap behind either gate). **The sprinter** is where the 0 base costs: every round is aimed where the target is, and during the flight a sprinter moves more than the capsule's half-width (1.6) at every automatic's lock range but Kurumi's, so a round from a 0 cone passes behind it — the first round of every other automatic lands 0–2% — and only a cone opened wide enough throws some rounds ahead of the body: the autos' sprint curves climb from there as the cone opens (Saori 3→35, Atsuko 8→42). **Kurumi** at 80 holds the best sprinting number of the automatics, 52%: in her round's 0.073 s a sprinter moves 1.58, just inside the half-width, so her first round lands every time and her bloom only takes rounds away (63→48). **The marksman rifles** never fire bloomed: Fubuki and Iori, from their 0 cone, land every round on a standing and on a walking target and 1% on a sprinter — at 135 a 1,500 u/s round flies 0.09 s, in which a walking target moves two thirds of the capsule's half-width and a sprinter 1.3 times it; Aris, her 0.02 base kept, lands 100 / 94 / 56, her laser bolt's 0.4 wider radius and whole 64-long body forgiving more of the drift. **The snipers** land every round: Aru and Hiyori at 200 (the 4,500 u/s round crosses it in 0.044 s, a sprinter's drift two thirds of the half-width) and Kei's instant beam at 250. The run before this one, earlier the same day on 9.6 b1105fv (the same ranges and speeds, the old base cones), read Saori 77 / 61 / 31, Asuna 73 / 58 / 33, Subaru 66 / 57 / 35, Minori 71 / 60 / 30 (in fives from cones of 0.02 to 0.08), Atsuko 63 / 52 / 37, Marina 54 / 49 / 38 (in tens from 0.06), Yuuka 53 / 47 / 37, Kurumi 52 / 52 / 47, Koyuki 33 / 31 / 27, Hina 60 / 57 / 43 (in twelves from 0.04, holding 1.4 s), Maki 57 / 54 / 40, Fubuki 100 / 79 / 37, Aris 100 / 94 / 57, Iori 100 / 78 / 37, Aru 89 / 86 / 74 and Hiyori 90 / 87 / 75, the shotguns within a point of now and Kei as now. The runs before that, 2026-11-09 on b1105er and 2026-11-10 on b1105es (the assault rifles at 112, the machine guns at 126, the marksman rifles at 160 with 1,100 u/s rounds, the snipers at 240 with 4,000 u/s rounds, Hina ungated), read Saori 75 / 60 / 31, Koyuki 31 / 29 / 25, Hina 22 / 21 / 19, Maki 55 / 51 / 35, Fubuki 100 / 46 / 1, Aris 100 / 63 / 5, Iori 100 / 44 / 1, Aru 77 / 76 / 63, Kei 100 / 100 / 100 and Hiyori 79 / 79 / 65. Against the 2026-10-24 run at the old ranges and speeds, every cone gun lost hit rate at the longer distances (Saori 99 / 81 / 35 then, 77 / 61 / 31 at b1105fv; Koyuki 49 / 44 / 30 then at 80, 33 / 31 / 27 at 120), and the shotguns the most (Haruka 78 / 46 / 18 then, 39 / 31 / 11 at b1105fv). Before b1105es the machine guns fought at 160 (Koyuki 24 / 22 / 18, Hina 16 / 16 / 14, Maki 44 / 41 / 29) and the marksman rifles at 112 (Fubuki 100 / 75 / 15, Aris 100 / 93 / 37, Iori 100 / 74 / 14; Aris's b1105er row read 95 / 39 on the moving targets, from a count that gave some of her rounds two bolts).

Projectiles fly straight (homing is zeroed universally). The targeting reticle is an **enemy-firing indicator**, not a range indicator: green by default, it flashes red while your current target is firing and stays red for the whole time a sniper is mid-charge with you as the target (see the sniper section). Being inside lock range is not signalled to players at all — the number only shapes bot behavior (bots hold their engage band around it). A faint in-lock tracer tint that once keyed to it was removed 2026-08-01.

### Units 2, 11 & 18 — the shotgun blast

- A trigger pull fires **one flying pellet cluster** carrying a fixed 8-point pattern (randomly rotated each shot, so no two blasts look alike while the spacing geometry never clumps). Each pellet keeps its **own hitbox** and dies individually on walls or the target; damage = pellets landed × 4 (all 8 point-blank = 32, all three shotguns; was × 5 / 40 before 2026-09-24).
- The pattern leaves the muzzle bunched and grows toward full width (~5.8 across) over the first **70 units** of flight. At the 80 lock range (40 until b1105eg doubled every lock range) it is fully open, so a blast at the lock lands as a full spread — at 40 it was ~57% open, ~3.3 across, a concentrated cluster; the pellets fly at 500 u/s (350 until b1105eg) and the volley dies at 100 (`pelletRange`, below).
- **Hoshino's wide fan (Unit 2):** her pattern is stretched **1.4× horizontally** after the per-shot rotation — the cloud is 1.4× wider and exactly as tall as Haruka's (fully open, as at her 80 lock: ~8.1 × 5.8; ~4.6 × 3.3 at the old 40). More graze coverage along the dodge axis — the dodge-catcher to Haruka's concentrated slug. (The fan was Haruka's from 2026-07-14 until the owner swapped the two patterns on 2026-10-10; nothing else differs between the two shotguns.)
- One blast = one simulated/networked object instead of 8 — the wire-cost half of the old online "shotgun lag" fix; the projectile broadphase (see Implementation notes) removed the other half, the dense-map CPU cost.
