The crate the model left floating over Storage is culled from the served copy (`glb_strip.mjs --cull`). Whatever stands taller than its walls — over 2.4 m (`fadeTall` in `GLB_MAPS`): the two engines, the reactor core, Storage's crate pile, a post by Shields — goes translucent only while it hides a unit from the camera, the Streets buildings' rule (`updateWallFade`) run on the pieces' own hulls: touching metre cells with geometry that high are one thing, split off the model at load, a stack counted by its top and faded whole (user 2026-10-19). The shooter view's scene is the daylight tone, as Warehouse's — the pale sky and fog, the white key light (user 2026-11-01; from 2026-10-19 to then it took Factory's night tone, `tone` in `GLB_MAPS`); command mode stays black (`cmdDark`). **Spawn places** (user 2026-10-19): nine set by hand from the user's marked render — the homes in navigation (p1) and the reactor room (p2), and seven more (the central junction, the room beside MedBay, the small room west of it, admin, MedBay's floor, communications, the shields corridor). After the first spawn every Trio respawn, 1v1 and 2v2 alike, is a random pick among the nine (`pick: 'random'` in the map's spawns), where Town picks by sight; a place with a living body on it is still passed over. The ship's hull confines the map, so it draws no red edge line (`edge: false`; the boundary walls stay). In command mode the scene around the ship is pure black, the stand included (`cmd_dark`: the plate's grid flickered against the hull; user 2026-10-19).

**Backrooms** (2026-10-19) — the fourth modelled map, the first indoors: *Original Backrooms* by Huuxloc on Sketchfab, licensed CC-BY-4.0 ([the model](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/original-backrooms-e5c6b30995ff442d9852a1dd697aaef1)), served as `client/public/maps/backrooms.glb` (4.5 MB, as modelled; its materials are unlit, the light baked in). The model is in metres — a hall 27 m across under a 2.7 m ceiling, a few partition walls — and small for a match, so it is served at **2 m per model unit** (`glb_rescale.mjs`; a guess, until the user tries the PT site's map scale test): a 55 × 57 m hall (219 × 229 units) under a 5.4 m ceiling. The generator reads it with `CEIL` 3.8 — the ceiling less a body's height: nothing stands at or over it, and the sim caps every body's feet there, so a jumping head stays under the tiles. Indoors the camera stands behind a wall most of the time, so every wall fades while it hides the player's own character (`fade_tall` 'body', `fade_own`); as every wall touches every other, a thing is a straight wall (`fade_walls`: the run of touching cells whose faces face the same way — the model's long faces cut into two-cell pieces first, so a 27 m wall is seeded along its whole length), and a wall crossed by the line from the camera to the character fades whole, the walls around it not; the ceiling tiles, lights and vents never fade (`never_fade`: the camera stays under them, and they would join every wall into one thing). The hall's own walls confine the map (`edge: false`). The hall is two cards (user 2026-10-19): **Backrooms**, with the usual HUD and rules, and **Backrooms (Anomaly)**, the same hall and data under the anomaly rules (`anomaly` in its row): no lock bracket, no edge arrows, no portraits and no other unit's bars — only the player's own, the floating one a pill alone, centred over the head — and the invincibility glow hidden by the walls, where elsewhere it shows through them; command mode untouched. Backrooms is listed first; the Anomaly is never a Random card's roll (`noRandom`). In command mode the roof hides (`roof`: the ceiling nodes) and the scene around the map is pure black, the stand included (`cmd_dark`). **Spawn places** (user 2026-10-19, marked on the plan): the homes at the north-west and south-east corners, two more — north of the closed room, the west aisle — and respawns at random among the four, as Spacecraft's. Known cost: the ceiling reads as one box over the hall, and the feet cap holds every body under 3.8 m — nothing flies higher, Aris included; the raised room at the south-west and the closed room at the centre are sealed by the model's own walls.

**Dust** (2026-10-19) — the fifth modelled map: *Dust2-short* by frostychaos144 on Sketchfab, licensed CC-BY-4.0 ([the model](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/dust2-short-211b9b2535e144fb9d1021228cc93d53)), served as `client/public/maps/dust.glb` (4.3 MB: the piece cut to its own box and 12 m around it — a few stray props lie far off in the model — and its ten 2 MB textures re-encoded at 1024 px as JPEG; 19 MB → under 5). The model is a thumbnail — the piece 0.7 × 1.1 units, its walls 0.11–0.14 tall — so it is served at **142.23 m per model unit** (the user's pick with the PT site's map scale test, 2026-10-19): the walls 16–20 m, the piece 114 × 178 m (455 × 711 units), its floor at y 0.59 (`ground` 83.92 m). The generator reads it with Town's parameters but `UNDER_STREET` 3.6 (a floor lies 1.4 m under the main one). The model is a game rip: every material is double-sided and its gates are shut — the long path to the north and the yard to the south-west lie behind closed gates, walls to a body, so the arena is the middle (the site on its plateau, the pit, the roofed hall, the lower yards); the rest stands as background. Reading it taught the generator rules it now applies to every model (2026-10-19): a one-sided face seen only from below (a roof's underside, a lintel) is a ceiling, never a floor, while a double-sided material's face is seen from both sides; the model's own sloped faces (1 degree or more, 3 m² or more) are read as ramps with their exact plane, so a street's long gentle grade is one slope and not a stair of 5 cm bands; the void's wall stands from the street whatever floor lies beside it; a slope's skirt stands only where a walked floor lies more than a step under its edge; and a ceiling nothing stands on merges by its own height class alone. A maze of walls like the Backrooms, it takes the same fade rules: every wall fades while it hides the player's own character, a straight wall at a time (`fade_tall` 'body', `fade_own`, `fade_walls`). The piece's own walls confine the map (`edge: false`). Listed before Backrooms in the picker (user 2026-10-19). **Spawn places**: the user's eight (2026-10-19) — the homes at the long path's gate and the lower yard's south end, six more around the middle; a Trio respawn picks among them by sight, as Town's. **Hand edits** (user 2026-10-24, each spot picked in the game with the PT site's collision inspector — F4 shows the player's place in the model's metres, a click in the F8 view names the box): six places the generator got wrong are fixed in the data itself, each under a `//` line in `arena.js` that says what and why, and listed in `shared/tools/maps/README.md` so a regeneration re-applies them — the guard along the deck's north edge lowered (a body walks off the 3.2 m drop), two "invisible pillars" removed and the slots they left closed (a shelf at the deck's height; a crate-high seam, the crate stack given a floor on its top), a thin slot and a pocket among the crates filled solid (a body jumped into both and the push-out parked it there), the 1.3 m crate and the 1.8 m ledge beside the deck given floors on their tops (topless things a body slid off).

**Warehouse** (2026-10-22) — the sixth modelled map: *Map 79 (Void)* by Elite Big Speakerman on Sketchfab, licensed CC-BY-4.0 ([the model](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/map-79-void-9faa9374c3794950994ab5dee79c42db)): the container cluster at the middle of its empty plain, cut 38 × 38 m, and a copy of it turned 180° about the yard's middle and set end to end, 38 × 62 m, after the user's sketch (the single cluster was the map for a day, then the doubled yard took its name). The model is the cluster's file with the copy's objects added as nodes that share its meshes and textures (`shared/tools/maps/glb_clone.mjs`; the textures to 1024 px by `glb_slim.cjs`, 11.6 MB), served as `client/public/maps/warehouse.glb`; the collision data is read off the joined model by the generator; the fade is by the model's objects (`fadeNodes`), each container, machine or crate fading whole when it hides the player's own unit. The homes at the yard's south-west and north-east corners and eight more places from the user's marked plan. Six of the containers — the three on the ground at each cluster's inner row, the plan's red lines (user 2026-10-22, "C: both ends") — are open at both ends (`glb_open_ends.mjs`): 7.5 m tunnels 1.5 m wide, the floor slab 0.2 m up, the roof a lid as before; each cluster's lengthwise container stands across the two diagonal ones' inner mouths, so a route through a diagonal turns once into the pocket beside it. The data was regenerated from the opened model (798 boxes, 676 floors).

**Compound** (2026-10-24) — the seventh modelled map, from the user's *cs_compound* glTF (44 MB, fetched from the repository's `raw-map-1` release; its source and licence are still to be noted): a fenced compound in a field, modelled in metres, served as `client/public/maps/compound.glb` (12.3 MB: the textures at 1024 px, the vertex normals dropped so three shades it flat, the indices 16-bit — `glb_slim.cjs`, `glb_strip.mjs`). Served at **1.34 m per model unit** (the user's pick with the map scale test, 2026-10-24: the containers, the cars and the fences read right against a body at it; `glb_rescale.mjs` puts a root scale node in the served copy, so the file's units are the game's metres) and placed at **4 units per metre**; the crop is 150 × 174 m (600 × 697 units) around the compound, the user's red outline widened west to the loading strip's wall and north to the fence corner: the yard inside its 2.4 m fence with its two buildings, the round tank and the chimney, the road that crosses the yard from the west gate to the north-east gate, the open shed north of the road, and the loading strip with its containers along the west edge between two barriers. The model's own fences and gates confine the play: the gate across the north-east road is shut and so are the strip's two barriers, so the grass, the big shed to the east and the two long sheds to the south-east stand as background — the generator's walk never reaches them and they read as solid (a 25 m wall where the ground is bare, the sheds and the things on the grass at their own heights); the arena's edge is the crop's box on the east and the south, and the west and north sides are **open** (`open` in the map's `MAP_BOUNDARY` row, read by the shared arena and the client alike): no boundary slab and no red line there — the strip's concrete wall and the fence corner at the north, inside the crop and read by the generator as every other thing, are the edge themselves (a slab a metre or two inside the wall was an invisible wall the visual did not add up to, user 2026-10-24); the generator's walk reaches no cell of the crop's edge on any side. The street is the yard's grass (`GROUND` -0.094 m, its height at the middle: the paved road lies 9 cm over it, and the grass rolls ±0.27 m, so the yard carries hundreds of small kerbs and slopes the body walks without noticing; read at 0 the yard was void). Three more parameters are the map's own: `UNDER_STREET` 0.45 (the grass dips that far under the street and is floor, not the model's hidden plate); `WINDING` 1 — the model's faces are wound consistently, so a face is a floor only when it faces up by its winding, double-sided material or not (Dust's and the Skeld's faces are seen from both sides, as before): without it a closed shell's underside — a tank hovering half a metre over the yard with 2.5 m of air inside — read as a floor a step up, its sloped edge as a kerb ramp, and a body walked into the tank and stood inside it (user 2026-10-24; the same reading had opened the insides of three cars and two crates, 75 m² in all, now solid); and `STREET_BAND` 0.3 — the band about the street a floor must lie in to seed the flood (0.1 on every other map): at 0.1 the rolling yard fell into hundreds of street-level islands, the loading strip was the largest of them, and the flood started outside the yard, which then read as solid. The bushes (the model's `Object_83`, black leaf cards up to 1.8 m, dotted over the yard) are left out of the read (the generator's `EXCLUDE`), so a body walks through them where they used to stand as low cover (user 2026-10-24). Floors are read up to **8 m** (`WALK_MAX`; 6 elsewhere): the big building's second floor lies at 6.9 m, up a switchback stair, and a body walks it. Generated with Town's parameters otherwise: 22,664 boxes and 16,878 floors, the heaviest data of the maps. Reading it taught the generator eight rules it now applies to every model (2026-10-24, the user's walk): a **parapet's hop side is a wide walked floor** — ground a body walks, the terrace — and not a thing's top the flood climbed (a car's bonnet read as a slope), a crate under the next in a stack, or a flight of steps a body's width wide: read as parapets, the car's roof and the stack's top lost their tops and a body that hopped onto them fell into the boxes, and the upper flight stopped short of the second floor; a **flight of steps is one plane**: a flat walked cell with a stair-steep cell of one direction within two cells on both sides is the tread between risers and joins the stretch, and a flight is fitted with half a riser of tolerance (`STAIR_TOL` 0.3 m; 0.15 for a slope), where a 32-degree flight used to fall apart into one-cell planes and flat cells and was walked as a sawtooth; **a slope under a slab** is a slope too: the passage floor under a walked floor above it (the first flight under the second floor it climbs to, a road under a bridge) is read for stretches with its own regions, and its planes are written in place of the passage decks (no skirts: what stands beside a flight under a slab is the model's own); and a **column climbs every level**: with the ground, a flight's tread and the second floor over both in one column, the topmost walkable level is the column's floor and the highest reached floor under it the passage (the ground under a flight's high end is given up: a body there stands on the street); a **thing stands from its own floor**: its box never reaches below its floor stack's bottom, however low the floor beside it lies — read from the ground floor beside the building, three 8 m walls stood on the ground floor under the second floor's edges, where the model has only air; and **a slab's edge is a passage too**: a lone plane within a slab's thickness of the passage roof beside it is the slab's edge beam, not the model's hidden plate, so the passage runs under it (one cell in from a real passage only: a plate under a yard stays solid behind its edge); and the **steep-face bands reach the walkable height**: the heights a column's vertical faces span are kept in 0.1 m bands up to the highest walkable floor plus a body's headroom and some (it was a fixed 8 m, and a face above the range was folded into the top band — the roof trusses at 12–14 m became a bar hanging a metre over the 6.9 m second floor, 44 boxes a body could not pass); and **cracks are filled**: a walked cell, or two, whose floor lies more than a step below the walked floors on both sides along an axis, those two within a step of each other, is a seam between two of the model's floor meshes the raster fell through — the landing's edge against the flight's last tread, a panel joint in the second floor — and no floor a body stands in, so it takes the lower floor beside it; read as drops, 58 such cells grew guard rails across the stair's foot and along the second floor's walkway, and a body whose centre crossed one fell to the ground floor (a seam's own cells need not be walked). Beyond the rules, one **cap raised**: a column holds up to eight floors, where it held four — a stack of four crates under the other building's catwalk used every slot, so the catwalk over them was no floor there, a body no longer fit beside the stack's cells, and the dead catwalk cells became two posts to the roof with a shaft around each, at the mouth of the covered bridge between the buildings: a body on the catwalk fell 7 m through a shaft (user 2026-10-24), and the model has no post there. And one more parameter that is the map's own data, **`FILL`**: rectangles of the model's metres set to a floor at a height, where the model leaves a hole by design that the game does not want — the big building's mezzanine stops half a metre short of the north wall's inner face, and a body on it fell through the slot to the ground floor (user 2026-10-24); the slot (x −17..−14 m, z 15.0..15.4 m) is filled to the mezzanine's 6.89 m, as a slab, so the doorway under it stays a passage. The same slot runs on west of x −17 behind a guard rail, as the model has it. And one more rule for every model, **treads** (2026-10-24): a slim strip of a thing — a quarter of a metre wide, which carries no top as a rule, since nothing stands on a fence — carries one when a body cannot fall off it (on both sides across its thin way, within a body's reach, stands a thing or a floor no lower than a step below its top; the void's wall counts as nothing) and a lidded strip or a thick thing's lid lies beside it within a step, read again and again until no such strip is left. The tarp sloping off the crate stack by the shed in the south yard was three such strips at 2.85, 2.62 and 2.40 m between the stack's top and the shed's eave; read once in the rectangles' order, the lower two were judged before the one beside the stack had its lid, and a body that walked north off the stack fell 3 m through the lidless strips into the solid slot under the eave and stood on the ground inside it (user 2026-10-24, at (−27.1, 52.7)); now it steps down them and the eave's slot holds it. 688 such tops over the map, each a strip between higher things; a strip with a drop beside it — the 174 m west wall's top, a fence's — carries none, as before (a pass that let every slim strip chain a lid put tops on both, 1,597 of them, and was dropped the same day). **Spawn places** (user 2026-10-24, the marked plan): the homes in the north shed and the south yard, and the user's eight more places — the north-west yard by the parked cars (−40, −45 m), the yard north of the big building's tank (−35, −25), the north-east road (22, −22), the yard road north of the big building (−34, 5), the loading strip at the road's west gate (−60, 5) and its south half (−59, 36), the yard east of the big building (1, 22), the south yard east of the shed (−8, 40); a Trio respawn picks among all ten by sight, as Town's and Dust's (until then six places set on the walked floor without the user's marks). Known costs: 12 MB downloaded once per session from the card's first tap; rounds and flight stop at the invisible wall just outside every fence and barrier; nothing beyond the fences can be entered; the client bundle carries 2.4 MB more data than before the map. The map's card is the user's own render of the compound from the south-east (2026-10-24; `client/public/maps/compound.png`, 1024 px at 256 colours): the profile shows it whole, the picker's wide thumb its middle — the two buildings and the bridge between them.

**The car, the flatbed and the trailer** (2026-11-03, b1102p; user, of the PT site's collision view with the pile circled beside Town's car: "how about just make those cboxes clean and simple like they are in Town reference? I'm talking this pile of stuff only", then of two layouts — each thing one box as Town's car is, or the car's body and its cabin apart — "I like option B"; of the trailer behind the wall: "use the same simple cbox logic") — the yellow car and the blue flatbed truck parked by the curved wall (model x −44.65..−37.85, z −39.3..−32.95) are seven boxes with lids, not the reading's 141 boxes, 123 floors and 30 slopes: the car's body to 1.49 m over the street in two boxes along its turn and its cabin to 2.09 m, the truck's cab to 2.89 m and its bed to its boards' top, 2.34 m, in three boxes along its turn; the cab and the bed reach into the wall behind them, so no crack is left between (`data_boxes.mjs`, tools README; the route grid file regenerated with it). Among the reading's slopes was one behind the car rising 0.8 m over a quarter of a metre — a ramp to the route grid's walk rule, which steps a body's centre line, up onto the car's roof where no body climbs: a bot sent past the pile toward the south spawn from the user's spot (model −35.98, −37.41) stood against the car's end for good. Now each step of the way is one a body takes: the car's body is a jump from the grass (about a metre), its cabin a step from the bonnet, the truck a jump from the car, and the wall's top a step from the cab and a jump from the bed; the grid's route past the pile goes over it and the wall, and a bot takes it. That way led bots onto the farm trailer behind the wall (model x −42.1..−37.45, z −33.01..−28.2), which the reading had made 65 boxes, 36 floors and 17 slopes with two slots in its bed down to the ground: a bot that jumped the wall from the truck landed on the bed and dropped into one, held there by the boards for good (15 of 116 runs with the enemy moved mid-crossing, in the change's first check). The trailer is five boxes now, in steps along its 45° turn, solid to its boards' top (2.39 m), one of them reaching the wall's foot where a post of the reading stood; a bot comes down the wall onto it and off it to the ground. Known costs: the truck's bed and the trailer are solid to their boards (a body stands at their height, not in a bed); the boxes stand out up to 0.8 m where the car (6.6°) and the truck (15°) are turned, the cab's up to a metre before the windscreen at the roof's height (its mirrors and bumper), the trailer's up to 0.64 m along its sides; a crossing waits out the bots' 1.5 s jump cooldown; and the wall can be climbed here, by a bot as by a player. Since b1102r (user, of the gap circled between the car and the truck: "Leave a walkable path there to match the visual gap there. Keep the cboxes simple so the BOT is unlikely to be stuck. No need exact precision.") the car's body and the bed's east half are two boxes each along their turn, and the gap between them is open from the west: a path 1.35 m wide and as deep with the grass for its floor, where the model's gap is 1.4–1.9 m; nearer the cab, where the gap narrows under a metre — a slot a body could wedge in — it stays filled.

**The two trailers by the fence** (2026-11-03, b1102q; user, of the collision view with the pair circled beside the first trailer's boxes: "modify the cboxes of these two as well. And fill the gap with cboxes as well so the unit won't fall into that pit") — the two farm trailers parked side by side by the curved wall and the black fence (model x −34.7..−27.55, z −33.05..−26.55), turned −75° and −52°, are one staircase of ten boxes with lids along x, solid to their boards' tops (2.47 and 2.31 m), in place of the reading's 129 boxes, 73 floors and 41 slopes. The gap between them is filled, and so is the pocket between them and the wall, which the reading had left as 2.8 by 4.3 m of open ground walled in by the trailers and the wall — a pit a body that came down into it could not leave (and a smaller one, 0.85 m across, in the nearer trailer's bed); the boxes on that side reach into the wall, and no ground in the area is walled in now. Known costs as the first trailer's: the boxes stand out up to about 0.65 m along the trailers' turned sides.

**Hospital** (2026-10-28) — the eighth modelled map, from the user's *animal_hospital_map_optimized__textured* glTF (Sketchfab, 20.5 MB: eight meshes merged by material, 179k triangles, three 1024 px textures): a single-storey animal hospital, mostly indoors — a 67 m corridor with wards either side, the lobby and the waiting room, a hydrotherapy room at the south end, a lawn to the east behind a fence with a gate, the road beyond. The served copy (`client/public/maps/hospital`): the six objects the user crossed out on the plan removed (a block west of the building, a bench, a planter by the lobby, a sign and a box east of the road), and after the first walk a chair by the lobby and a pistol prop on the waiting room's floor (user 2026-10-28), and the gate's centre post — a 0.26 m mullion at the leaves' meeting line, 3.9 m tall, that stood in the open gateway with two collision boxes of its own (user 2026-10-29, "no need the collision box and visual of this door-ish thing"), and the 3D lettering of the waiting room's wall sign — 900 triangles, 5 m of letters at 3.4 m, the cross beside them kept (two boxes clipped the ends, leaving AN, a dash and then the A; user 2026-10-29) (user 2026-10-29, "remove the 'animal hospital' text on the wall"; the map is named Hospital since) — `glb_delete_boxes.mjs <in> <out> "x0,x1,z0,z1,y0,y1;..."` drops every triangle whose corners all lie in a box, the way a thing in a merged mesh is taken out; every door open (user 2026-10-28, and 2026-10-29: "not removed but rotated as opened by 90 degrees"): the twelve door leaves — boxes of 1.67 x 2.04 m in their casings, each with a window, a kick plate and a handle — found by shape with `glb_doors.mjs <glb> <unitsPerMetre> [w h thickness bottom]` and swung 90° into their rooms about the jamb away from the handle with `glb_swing.mjs` (the triangles inside a leaf's boxes turned about a vertical hinge; `glb_handles.mjs` tells the hinge side), the lawn's two glass gate leaves swung onto the road the same way, so the road is walked (the south leaf's pull handle lay across the leaves' meeting line inside the north leaf's handle box, went with the north leaf and hung in the air past its end — user 2026-10-29, "this door knob belongs to the gate door at the other side"; turned back about the north hinge and out again with the south leaf, two more `glb_swing.mjs` entries on the open copy); the twenty leaves — the twelve doors', the gate's two and the six glass leaves the model has standing open on its own (the corridor's double doors, 2.3 × 3.9 m) — then moved into nodes of their own, one per leaf per source mesh (`glb_extract_node.mjs <in> <out> "DoorD1:box;DoorD2:box;…"` — the attributes shared, the index buffers new), named by the map's `fadeWhole: ['Door']`: each leaf is a thing of its own that fades whole, alone, when it hides a body — in the wall fade's pieces a leaf faded by the piece and showed torn (user 2026-10-29, "part of the main gate door is transparent"), and kept out of the fade altogether for a day (`neverFade`) a leaf hid a body (user 2026-10-29, "those doors NEED to go transparent when blocking sight … not only the bottom part"); the leaves' glass then given an opaque copy of its material (`glb_opaque_mesh.mjs <in> <out> "*_Object_4"`), since a pane outside the fade is drawn as the model's 16% glass while every other pane is drawn opaque (two more doorways had frames and no leaf, the entrance is open, the two frames by the vending machines at the corridor's south end are behind solid wall); the six glass leaves given the opaque copy too (`glb_opaque_mesh.mjs <in> <out> "*_Object_4"`, a * matching any run): left in the walls' mesh their lower pieces had faded between the camera and the body, and each leaf showed a see-through, jagged bottom (user 2026-10-29, "indoor doors are still transparent at the bottom"); then scaled to metres (`glb_rescale.mjs` by 0.37 — the user's pick with the map scale test, ×1.477 on the first cut at 0.25: doors 3.0 m, ceiling 5.7 m), textures to JPEG (`glb_slim.cjs`), normals dropped, the indices 16-bit and every primitive given its own vertices (`glb_strip.mjs`): 11.5 MB. The map loaded in eleven seconds against Compound's two (user 2026-10-29, "Hospital loads longer in game"): the leaf nodes shared their source chunks' whole attribute sets — 4.2 million vertex references for 380 thousand vertices — and the client copies a mesh's attributes whole for the fade, so eighty leaf meshes each carried a 65k-vertex copy (JS heap 364 MB); and each of the wall fade's 1,179 pieces indexed its source's 200k-vertex split set, over which three measured its bounding sphere at the first frame. Now the strip step writes a primitive that uses part of its set with just those vertices, and a fade piece's geometry holds its own vertices (main.js splitFadeParts `part`): the scene holds 1.0 million vertices in place of 4.8 (the pieces no longer index their sources' 236 million), the pieces are culled as pieces, and the map is ready in 3.7 seconds against 11.5 before, headless (Compound 1.5). Then the pieces merged (user 2026-10-29, "merge the wall pieces"): the model's merged meshes come in 65k-vertex chunks cut by vertex count, not by place, so one wall lay in three of them and took three meshes, three materials and three hull tests a frame; a thing's pieces of one material, one transform and one attribute layout are one mesh now (splitFadeParts partMulti, mergedFadeMesh — the door leaves' too), 662 pieces for 382 things in place of 1,179 (339 of the walls' material, 182 of the trims', 105 of the plants' and chairs', 36 glass), 3.0 seconds. What is left over Compound's 108 is the walls themselves: a room's four walls and its doorway's jambs are things of their own, so that one wall fades alone. The plan the user marked was `glb_section.cjs` (the model photographed from above with everything over a height cut away, so a roofed building shows its rooms) and `glb_slice.mjs` (the walls' section at a height, as lines). Collision: `DUMP=1 SCALE=4 GROUND=0.185 CELL=0.25 EXCLUDE= MARGIN=2 LOW_MAX=1.5 WALK_MAX=6 HEADROOM=2 UNDER_STREET=0.12 CEIL=5.5 FILL="-26.5,-22.4,-38.0,-15.0,-0.335;-33.9,-30.5,-38.1,-15.0,-0.335;-30.0,-26.5,-24.0,-15.0,-0.335;-27.6,-22.3,8.5,13.0,-0.335;-34.1,-27.8,8.5,13.2,-0.335" CLEAR="-7.215,-1.369,-16.39,-11.14,0.6,11.1;-7.215,-1.369,-0.666,4.588,0.6,11.1;-34.0,-21.5,8.4,14.6,-0.5,11.1;-27.8,-25.1,4.3,5.1,-0.5,3;-65.5,-63.3,-13.75,-13.3,0.1,1.1;-65.5,-63.3,-14.9,-13.3,1.0,1.3" CAP="-31.7,-25.2,-10.2,-9.0,0.55;-32.0,-25.0,-0.1,5.3,0.55" node glb_collision.mjs client/public/maps/hospital.glb hospital -82.35 24.5 -49.8 46.0 <out>` — the street is the building's floor (0.185 m; the middle lawn steps up 0.9 m, a kerb the flood climbs; the road lies 0.185 m under the floor and is walked through the gate; the parking beyond the road's wall is never reached and stands as background), CEIL 5.5 under the 5.7 m ceiling (no face of the roof is read; at the first cut's 0.25 m the door lintels stood 1.9 m over the floor and HEADROOM had to be 1.8 — at 0.37 they stand 2.8 m and the default 2 holds). FILL (user 2026-10-29, "this lawn should be standable"): the lawn north of the gate — the strips either side of the tall green hedge block and the strip along the building — and the lawn south of the fence, in five patches (the last ones after the user's second and third walks), are the site's base plate 0.34 m under the floor — under the street by more than UNDER_STREET, so unread as a hidden plate (reading deeper put a crawlspace under the whole building) — filled to that level. The strip along the building first ran on to z −10, over the floor of the closed room south of the waiting room, 0.3 m above the plate: a body walked in through the wall the fill had erased, stood sunk to its knees and saw daylight under every wall (user 2026-10-29, "an unnecessary leaking gap on the wall"); the strip stops at the building's edge, z −15, and the room is void again. The rectangle by the lobby's windows ran from z 4.4 across the lobby's south wall and its east wall: a fill touching a wall's cells erases the wall, so the window sill at x −27.6..−22.3 and the east wall at x −22.3 (z 4.4..7.7) had no box, and a body walked through the window (user 2026-10-29, "a collision wall is missing here"); it starts at z 8.5 now, the lawn's side of the wall. Door D8 by the west corridor (x −67.3..−65.2, z 0.3) is shut again: a frame fixed on a solid wall with nothing behind it, its leaf swung open stood 1.8 m out into a void room — turned back about its hinge with `glb_swing.mjs` on the door's own nodes (`"DoorD8::-176.18,0.85:0,-1:-1,0"` on hospital_open.glb before the rescale, the empty box list naming the nodes; user 2026-10-29, "close this door, it's visual only anyway"). CAP (new with this round, 2026-10-29: boxes of the model's metres in which a thing's top is read no higher than a height): the lobby's chairs — three rows of five facing the windows and the five along the sign wall; seats 0.72 m over the floor, backs 1.2 m, a jump — are read as 0.55 m blocks, one per row, stepped onto and walked along, the backs walked through, the aisles between the rows walked (user 2026-10-29, "make those chairs walk-on-able, without jumping needed"; a first cap took the five by the wall only and left the backs' tops hanging as beam boxes over the row — a capped cell hangs nothing under a body's headroom now, and a seat the flood had climbed as a kerb is floor at the cap). The rows then still stopped a body offline (user 2026-10-29, "well i'm unable to do that"): the offline client's own collision mirror (main.js unitOverlapsObstacle, resolveUnitObstacleCollisions) lacked the shared sim's floorTop clause (physics.js boxHoldsAt — a box whose top is a floor lets a body within a step of that top come) and pushed the body off the row's side before the ground rule could step it up; the mirror now asks the shared window, so offline steps as online does (b1025v). CLEAR (user 2026-10-29, "leaves don't need collision boxes"): the canopies of the two trees by the road from 0.6 m up (their trunks' bases stay posts), and the two trees on the south lawn and the hedge along it whole, from the ground, in one box over both (user 2026-10-29, the trunks blocked the way; a box must hold a tree whole — a branch crossing its edge is read and marks the cells inside), and the desk slab that runs through the west corridor's wall at (−64.4, −14.2) — 1.67 m wide, 1.29 m deep, its top at 1.17 m, 13 cm of it on the corridor's side — with the 1 m panel under that front: together a ledge box along the wall a body ran into (user 2026-10-29, "remove this collision box"; the slab is cleared whole, as its end faces left stubs, and the chair and cabinet behind the wall keep a box) — 5,034 triangles are not read. The two north fill rectangles stop a cell short of the 7.6 m concrete wall at x −30.26 between them: a filled cell is floor and drops what stood in it, and the wall had gone (user 2026-10-29, "a concrete wall with no collision box"). 1,746 boxes, 694 floors, 3,523 walkable nav points; the spawns are the user's marked plan (2026-10-29, the Backrooms' rule: a respawn where no living enemy sees it): the homes on the road's near stretch and in the ward at the corridor's far end, eight more places — the hydrotherapy room, three wards, the waiting room, the small room off the corridor, the lawn by the gate and the road's far stretch (before them the generator's own twelve of 2026-10-28). In 2v2 the enemy's mate stood in the ward's north wall, 3 m from the far home by the fixed offset every map used (user 2026-10-29); a mate's spawn is now the first clear side of its home — the map's own offset first, then the other sides at 3 m, then at 2 m, on the home's floor before anything walked up to — one rule for the server's match and the offline client (spawn.js mateSpawnFor); on every other map the mate stands where it did. Command mode cuts the model 4.6 m over the floor (the map's `cmdCeil`, user 2026-10-29, "remove the ceiling in command mode": the ceiling is one mesh with the walls, so no roof node could hide as the Backrooms' do; a clipping plane on the map's materials alone, the bodies and shots untouched — the cut runs under the lower ceiling's fixtures at 5.1 m and over the doors, 3.0 m). The card is the user's own photo of a ward, taken in the game's photo mode (2026-10-29); the picker's wide thumb shows its upper part (`thumb: 'top'`). Beds (0.85 m) and cabinets (1.0–1.2 m) carry floors on their tops: a body jumps onto them from their open sides (the 1.34 m jump clears them; a bed's 1.5 m foot rail does not).

**District** (2026-10-29) — the ninth modelled map, from the *jujutsu shenanigans map* glTF (Sketchfab, amogusstrikesback2, CC-BY-4.0, 30 MB: a Roblox map, 27,000 nodes — one per brick — 174k triangles, 68 small textures): a walled city block, 197 x 195 m — streets on a grid, the square at the middle, buildings of two to fifteen storeys, a plaza of dead trees — and the station plaza south of it, open to the city's south street, with the train platforms 8.7 m under it (the arena 197 x 274 m; user 2026-10-29, "the underground train station area should be available" — the first crop stopped at the city's wall, and the arena's perimeter stood across the plaza's mouth); the dark beside the plaza is walled by the map's VOID data. The plaza a body reaches is the forecourt in front of the station building (under its canopy, with the grand stair down to the platforms): the plates behind the building and east of the 7 m wall beside the forecourt are the model's backdrop, walled from the city, the forecourt and the platforms alike — a spawn of the first set stood on one for a day, and the bot spawned there never came out (moved 2026-10-29, the second day). The model's unit is 100 Roblox studs (its bowling balls are 2 studs across, its leaderboards 10 by 15 — the stud 0.28 m), so the served copy is scaled by 28 (glb_rescale.mjs); the street is the roads' top, y 0.55, the base plate 0.33 m under it (UNDER_STREET 0.4, so the plate's patches between the roads are not pits). What the model carried as pictures went before the rescale (glb_drop_nodes.mjs, user 2026-10-29, "remove those images", "remove this doll"): 50 of its 68 images were posters, billboards and signs — manga pages, memes, photos, stickers, shop signs and ads on little quads in front of the walls: 36 in the city, and 14 more in the station's halls and the bowling alley on the second day (user: "there are un-scrapped image textures in B2 and B3 levels") — and the quads went with the images; 15 pictures on solid things — the wallpaper tiled over the bowling alley's walls, its floor and the base plate, the bowling balls and the lane, the vending machines' fronts, the sweets banners' boards — are a plain grey instead (blank=), and three stay: a control panel's face on a roof, the station's name over its door, a menu board; the Reimu doll on the platform is gone. Two black boxes the model carried as sound regions — Speaker11 and Speaker12, 69 by 112 m and 17 by 134 m, 11 m tall, over half the city — are gone too. They never showed in play — their faces point inward, so from outside they are culled and from inside the fade dims a thing that holds the camera to a tenth — and the first note here said they stood as black walls, an assumption written before a photo was taken (the user asked for the before and after, 2026-10-29, and the two photos matched); what they did was collide: a body starting inside one was thrown 6 m. The 1,361 window panes — the one glass material, Part588Mtl — are gone too, from the model and from the collision: they filled openings with no wall behind them, so the windows stand open, seen through, shot through, walked through (user 2026-10-29, "that's what I want"). The game's copy is the 12,171 bricks merged into 1,366 things (glb_merge_parts.mjs: parts whose boxes touch are one thing, cut at 40 m across; the ground, the streets and the long walls things of their own), 5.5 MB from 30, and each thing fades whole when it hides the player's own unit (fadeNodes, as Warehouse's). The collision is read part by part (glb_parts_collision.mjs; user 2026-10-29, "use simple c boxes in this map, simple geometries"): each brick one box, its world bounds however it is turned; the box's top a floor where it is 0.6 m wide and long (a thing's lid under 20 m², a floor over) and where the box is no taller than a step (a kerb, a tread — the sim holds a body against any box without a lid, however low); a brick tilted to lie as a ramp, or a wedge, one plane surface over its sloped face's own footprint and no box (the second day: the plane lay over the brick's whole box at first, and a thick brick's face falls short of its box's ends by half the thickness, so the plane ran on past the face — 1.3 m over the top landing of the B1→B2 escalator, from the 3.8 m thick body under its steps; the step rule lifted a body onto nothing there and dropped it, the grid's walk from the landing arrived a storey too high, no edge joined the lanes to the landing, and B2 and B3, joined to the rest by that escalator alone, were pruned as a sealed void: user, "pathfinder seems to be unable to track the enemy through the deep slopes connecting B1 and B2, and B2 to B3"); a face steeper than 56 degrees (MAX_GRADE 1.5) is no ramp, the brick a box like any other; so the car park's ramps, the stairwells' flights, the station's stairs and the escalators down to its deepest halls are walked and the space under a ramp is passed — storeys stack, the sim standing a body on the highest surface within a step of its feet; a block of earth a metre thick and 200 m² or more, topping at the street or under it, is its top alone (the station and the cellars are built inside such blocks); an invisible part collides with nothing. A wall whose top meets a floor is stepped onto, not held against: the sim holds a body against a lidless box until the body's centre is a metre over its top, so a wall ending at a floor's level — the wall under a balcony, a tunnel's wall topping 20 cm under the road, the second tread of a stair whose boxes all rise from one base — stood as an invisible lip (user 2026-10-29, three spots); a lidless box whose top lies within a step under, or up to a metre under, a surface touching its footprint gets a lid and the lid's buffer, pass after pass, so a stair's treads lift one another. A stair the user wants as one slope is RAMP data (x −32.0..33.2 and 33.5..34.9 by the balcony at z 26..29.5: the treads go, one plane rises in their place). Every other flight the tool reads itself: a run of three or more boxes side by side along one axis, each no deeper than 1.6 m, their tops rising a tenth to a step from one to the next, is a flight: its top tread stays a step, and the other treads become one plane from the lowest tread's top up to the top tread's edge (the first cut ran the plane to the top tread's far side, and the step from it onto the landing grew past a body's step) (7 flights of 39 treads: the station's trench stair in three runs, the plaza's 29 m stair down to the platforms, the lower flight by the balcony, two small ones); a surface lying within 1.5 m over a flight's top — the block of earth a stair is cut down into, the base sheet — is cut away over the flight, so every stair's opening is open (user 2026-10-29, "conduct the simplified procedure to the underground facility; make stairs slope, make cboxes simple"). A hole is cut in the surfaces over the station's stair (HOLE, the map's own data: x −18.6..−4.8, z −44.2..−33.1, every surface topping over 0.15 m): the block of earth the stair is cut into topped it at 0.22 m, and a body walked over the trench. The copy it reads has no tree crowns (the Leaf nodes) and no branches (the brown parts not standing on the ground, 283 of them): a trunk is one box, the leaves have none (user 2026-10-29, "tree trunks can have simple collision box, leaves need no C-boxes"). Left out: parts under 0.5 m every way (MIN_SIZE 0.5; 0.3 at first — a bolt, a knob, a sign's bracket), flat sheets under 10 cm (road markings), nothing under the street (BELOW 30 — it was 7, then 13, until the user went down a second time: a hall 14.6 m under the north-east quarter, long halls at 17.7 m, the bowling alley at 22 to 26 m; a body walking off the 13 m cut's last floor was lifted to the street, the sim's ground where no surface lies), parts outside the crop. The cell generator (glb_collision.mjs) was tried first: it reads one floor per cell, so a storey over a storey cannot be — it shut every 7-stud doorway (1.96 m, under its 2 m headroom), chose a fourth storey over a ground-floor ramp, and with its floors capped at 6 m blocked every stair there (user 2026-10-29, three reports). 9,326 boxes, 8,701 surfaces (276 ramps), 78,779 walkable nav points on the grid's fifteen layers, 11,000 of them under the street (the trench and B1 6,400, B2 1,700, B3 3,000 — none of B2 and B3 before the second day's cut) and 3,600 over 20 m (the towers' upper storeys and roofs — none before the cap rose to sixteen); the spawns a first set on the ring road's corners and mid-sides, an inner street and the station forecourt; team 1 starts at the south-east corner and team 2 at the north-west (swapped 2026-11-02, user: "swap the initial spawn spot for Team1 and Team2"). No red edge: the city's wall is the edge (user 2026-10-29). The card's picture is the plan from straight above (glb_section.cjs). **The stair's handrails (b1026y, 2026-10-30; user: "these two are extending too long, blocking the way on the ground floor")** — the two boards along the wide stair down to the underground (x −19.4..−0.9, each a tilted board falling 9.8 m, a metre over the steps) were a box each 9.8 m tall over their whole length, so the street east of the stair's parapet was walled off where the boards run under the deck; a TRIM rectangle in the map's recipe cuts the two boxes back to the parapet's end (x −4.4). Looked at from six sides first; the street walked across where it was blocked, the stair walked down after. **The counter at the window (b1026z, 2026-10-30; user: "this CB is blocking unit's way walking to the window")** — a 1 m counter set at 45 degrees in a third-floor room was one box the size of its bounding square, 3.8 by 3.6 m, and held a body two metres from the window; the recipe's `CUT=Part9241` reads it in half-metre lengths along the diagonal, eight small boxes a body walks up to. **The station's hall (b1105i, 2026-11-05; the user's District instructions, worked in PT)** — (1) Two stair slopes completed (user: "The slope for the stairs here is not complete", x −74.47 z −70.75; "This side as well", x −73.52 z −24.62): each flight was two planes with a flat tread between them, the second plane running out short, so a body stood on a step in the middle of the flight; the three planes are one plane from the top tread to the bottom (0.5765 rise per metre), the box at the top of the second flight lowered to the slope (`district_data.json`, hand-edited). (2) Two hidden doors (user: "Keep the visual here, but trim the cboxes so it works as a hidden door here", "And here too"): the wall boxes #3011 (the hall's east wall, x 7.23..7.59, z 42.28..44.54) and #5265 (its south wall, x −52.54..−49.86, z 129.23..129.55) start at −6.90 m instead of −8.35, a 2.4 m opening over the floor, and the six posts under them are gone; the wall is drawn as it was, so the door is hidden. Over each opening, on the far side (the east face of the first, the south face of the second), a copy of the pillar plaque #3442 (0.84 × 0.49 m, 0.46 m proud, its bottom 0.2 m over the opening; `glb_piece_clone.mjs` on the served model) is the hint (user: "Add one of this object as a door lintel on the other side so the player is hinted"). (3) The two ladders out of the pit under the street (U1, U2 — the ladders paragraph; user: "Add these two ladders"). (4) The hall was no part of the bots' grid at all — its floor slabs are a 0.65 m kerb over the hall floor that the step rule does not climb, so they were a sealed void to the prune: THE WAYS IN and THE HEADROOM (the paragraph below) — 13,300 nodes more on District, the hall's slabs at −9.30, the pit floor at −18.16 and the cellars at −16..−11 among them, 131 phantom nodes under thin sheets gone (user: "BOT does not know the area over this area, let them know"). **b1105j, the same day** — (5) The five climbable walls (THE WALLS, the ladders paragraph): W1 the west platform's wall over the track bed (the face x 28.33, the bed −11.42, the platform −9.30, 202 m) and the terraces' retaining walls W2–W5 (the faces x −63.20 and −77.63, from −18.16 onto −15.40 and from −15.40 onto −12.39; the 7 m gap between each tier's two walls left alone). (6) The hidden doors' edges (user: "add a pure visual darker lines as door edges implying the entrance on both sides of the walls"): on both faces of each door wall a dark frame — two jambs and a head, 6 cm wide, 1.5 cm proud, from the visible floor to the opening's top — in the served model (`glb_add_boxes.mjs`), no collision. (7) The second floor's balcony strip at x 33.92..34.39, z 31.1..50.7 (user: "there's a gap at the second floor of this spot, causing unit to fall off", standing under it at x 34.13 z 39.78): the model's 3 cm sheet between the slab's west edge and its balustrade is a flat sheet under 10 cm, which the collision reader leaves out, so a body stepping off the slab fell 6 m to the street; a box and a floor at the sheet's top (6.28 m) are hand-added, and the balustrade (0.84 m, its own box) holds the body there.

**Neon** (2026-10-31; named Blockade from 2026-11-03, user: "change map name “Neon” to “Blockade” in codes and in game displays.", until 2026-11-11, user: "on a second thought, change all names from Blockade to Neon. PT and 9.6." — its key `neon`, its files `neon.*`) — the tenth modelled map: *Downtown Echo - miniroyale.io* by zct_33 on Sketchfab (the author of Town's model), licensed CC-BY-4.0 ([the model](https://sketchfab.com/3d-models/downtown-echo-miniroyaleio-79a2c6382cbe4e39905e0653d19dec57)), served as uploaded less sixteen things (`client/public/maps/neon.glb`, 10.9 MB: 224 meshes, 136k triangles, 40 small textures — 11.1 MB and 264 meshes on 276 objects with the scaffold's copy and its edits — a clone shares its original's mesh — below — the two wooden signposts, the three 3 m pads on the main street's north sidewalk with their three holograms, the two 1.6 m pads, the two drain covers and the 3.4 m disc in the north-west back lot, user 2026-11-01, "remove these objects visually and physically"; and two panels of the railing on the east edge of the 8.6 m deck, model x 1.25 z 25.7..33.6, the panel north of them and the deck's north rail kept, user 2026-11-02, "remove the visual and cboxes of the red-circled fences. It's a partial section, not the whole fence" — dropped with `glb_drop_nodes.mjs`, out of sight and out of the collision alike: the collision read again off the trimmed model, the deck open to the street below along those two panels) at 1 m per model unit (the user's pick, 2026-10-31 — a shop door 2.7 m; the map scale test, F7 on the PT site, tries others): a neon city block on a 32 m tile grid, 192 x 96 m — a main street along x and a cross street along z in a T, the back lots behind the shops with containers and fire-escape stairs, five lobby buildings with cross-shaped halls and a doorway on each side, a two-level mesh cage in the main street (and its mirrored copy beside it since 2026-11-02), a skybridge 20 m over it, towers up to 255 m over the blocks. The street is the road's top, 4.2927 in the model. A ceiling 26 m over the street (`MAP_BOUNDARY` `ceil: 104`; the user's pick, the skybridge's roof at 25.6 m the highest place to stand) caps every body's feet, and nothing over it carries a box (user 2026-10-31, "for the angles/locations that units cannot reach, you can skip building the c-boxes there"). In the Random card's pool; the walls fade as District's (both the user's picks). The card is the user's own picture down the main street over the scaffolds (2026-11-02, b1102i); the picker's wide thumb shows its middle, the default ("Crop the middle part for the thumbnail"). Its boost scale is the default, 1 (it had District's and Compound's 5 from 2026-11-01 until 2026-11-03, user: "remove the 5 times boost feature in Blockade").

Its collision is **the solid reading** (`shared/tools/maps/glb_solid_collision.mjs`, new with this map). The first readings — the parts generator with a box per part, then per piece — boxed whatever air lay inside a part's bounds (user 2026-10-31: "the red marked areas are not accurate enough; C-boxes are taking redundant spaces" — an air duct's box holding the air inside its bend, a slanted sign's box standing over the sidewalk — "and note that there's a tunnel inside" — a building's box over its hall). The solid reading boxes the model's solid instead: the triangles drawn into 0.25 m cells, a body 0.75 m square and 2 m tall flooded through the open air from the ceiling down, every cell no body reaches solid — a sealed building's inside, a slot narrower than a body, the corner behind the duct — and the solid covered by boxes, each grown as large as the solid allows, the thickest parts first, a box the others cover dropped. The floors: the slopes (the fire escapes as one plane each) and the ground's floors come from the parts generator, kept where a flooded body's feet rest on them; every other floor is a box's own top where a body stands, at the model's height there. A ground slab under a floor no higher than a step (the sidewalks, the lots' paving) carries no box. The columns with no ground under them (the crop's two corners west of the west building, where the model has nothing) are walled to the ceiling, as District's VOID. The south-east podium's lobby is a cross of two 4 m arms 2.26 m under their ceiling (the floor 0.54 m over the street) with a door corridor at each end of the east-west arm; at 1 m a unit its `LOW` rectangle lets a 1.5 m body through (user 2026-10-31, "open it up, I'll adjust the unit scale later in play test"), and a unit's head passes the ceiling there. Its layout is the simplest (user 2026-11-01, "unit will stuck in some kind of layer when jump in this tunnel ... I want very simple cbox layout for the tunnel ... no weird layers inside" — a jump landed a unit on a raised block across the south arm, 1.25 m, and on the ceiling slab's own floor, 2.30 m, inside the shell): `CLEAR` leaves the arms and the corridors unread from 0.56 m to 3.2 m, over a jump's reach, so a unit walks through the block, the kerbs and a beam's V at the east door ("tunnel entrance blocked", 2026-10-31), and `FILL` makes the four corners and the shell above the arms one solid to the roof (the corners were empty shell the flood had got into, the arms' side walls now 0.1 m behind the model's faces). Two more walls the same evening (user 2026-11-01, "add c-box here as well, i want this wall gap sealed" / "add an invisible c-box to block this little gap"): the model's wall across the south arm just past the east-west arm — a counter with its sill 1.4 m up and the wall over it to the ceiling, 0.75 m thick, which the CLEAR had left a unit walking through — is `FILL`ed from its floor to the shell, so the arm's end behind it, the vestibule inside the south door, is entered from that door alone; and the 1.4 m slot between the podium's plinth and the south building east of the south door's forecourt carries an invisible wall, one cell (0.25 m) thick and 3.2 m high. The pit between the two big containers by the cross street — 0.75 to 2.25 m wide, 6.25 m long, walled by their 5.19 m sides and closed at both ends, so a unit that dropped in never got out — is `FILL`ed to their roofs (user 2026-11-02, "seal this gap with cbox so the unit won't fall into the gap ... Unit should be able to walk between this gap on the top of two containers"): an invisible floor level with both roofs, a unit walking straight across at 5.19 m; the gap still shows. The forecourt and the vestibule are so a pocket off the street — a unit dropped into the forecourt off a roof leaves it by the south door only (said to the user). The south building's base plate, which the generator read as one 0.48 m floor over its whole rectangle and so over that alley and the street north of the building's face (a unit stood a third of a metre over the street there, "feet 0.48 m"), is cut back to the face: `FLOORCUT`, a generator floor's part inside a box dropped. The stairs are slopes ("use slope for stairs", the first playtest): `RAMP` lays one plane over each fire escape and each of the scaffold's mesh ramps, measured off the model, and the boxes under it end at it — a body walked up each, the largest rise in a tick 0.33 m. The audits' fixes (2026-10-31 and 11-01, pictures and the engine's own walks over every region): a slope the model does not draw over three quarters of its rectangle is left out (a chamfer along a container turned 45 degrees, read as a plane over its whole axis-aligned rectangle, stood a floor in the air over the sidewalk); no box holds a slope's cell together with another top's (the lobby halls' tiled floor lay a tenth of a metre under the tiles); a box under a slope's foot, a floor it does not carry, or a bar narrower than a floor is stepped over, as is a wall a floor lies on (the boxes under the tower's 16 m ledge pushed a body off it); a top narrower than a floor joins its neighbours' into one (a roof strip turned 40 degrees dropped a body to the street); a floor inside another of its height goes; and the boxes are trimmed to the parts each alone covers (`TRIM=1`: grown largest-first through one block, they overlapped — their cells seventeen times the solid, "huge clustered cboxes", user 2026-11-01 — and the collision is the same with them trimmed). The two round lobby buildings' cross halls stay solid: the model walls them off the street. 6,677 boxes and 3,124 floors; the route grid 19,579 walkable nodes. Spawns: five places the user stood and named (2026-11-01) — team 1 at model (−33.3, 52.2) and team 2 at (35.5, 11.5), three more on the street — each at the floor the user stood on: `spawn_set.mjs` takes `x,z,y` now, the walked floor nearest y, for a place under a deck the grid also walks (a sixth, at the junction under the 12.9 m deck, came out the same day: "remove spot 3").

**The scaffold's copy** (2026-11-02, b1102f; user: "I want to copy this cluster and place another one of it on the main road", "roughly place it there, the slope facing the right side of the map", then of two layouts the left-right mirror and, of a 2.9 m gap between the two, none): the main street's scaffold — 44 objects, 5,980 triangles: the ramp cage from the street up to the 5.8 m deck, the deck's two cages, the upper ramp to the upper cage's top at 8.6 m, the walkway north at 8.6 m and the ramp up to the top platform at 12.6 m — copied mirrored onto the road's south half: x' = 7.12 − x, z' = z + 8.01 (model x 3.56..26.99, z 29.85..48.39). Its ramp's foot is at its east end, facing east by the junction; its deck runs beside the original's along the road's middle and the two touch at one corner, so the road is closed at the street — a unit goes round by the sidewalks or over a scaffold; its top platform hangs over the road's north half with nothing under it (the original's leans on a shop's awning; kept so, the user's call). A mirror is no node matrix the client or the collision tools read right — the faces turn inside out — so the copy's meshes are new geometry baked from the original's (`glb_clone.mjs --mirror`: the positions and normals carried through, each triangle's winding reversed). Its collision is read off the model with the copy by the same recipe, the copy's five ramps mirrored into `RAMP`, and only the copy's rectangle of that reading goes into the data (`data_region.mjs`): read whole, the touching copy had re-cut the original's corner boxes and floors — the boxes are grown largest-first over the whole map and the flood fills the joint — and opened a 14 cm slot along the foot of its walls; the rest of the map, the original scaffold too, keeps its data exactly. Bodies walk the copy's ramps and walkway as the original's, and none passes the joint at the street.

**The scaffold's edits** (2026-11-02, b1102g; user, of marked pictures: "delete this one and add a connected one as catwalk", "extend the length of the new one and delete that one from the old ones", "change the opening to the other side", then "The extension length should only be one fence long. The red line part will be sealed." and "the yellow side wall on the ground level should be removed after the extension"): the two walkways joined at 8.6 m by one more 4 m walkway piece, a clone of the original's (model x 1.57..5.57, z 29.37..33.37; no railings, as the walkway's middle has none), the copy's railing on that side of its walkway gone; the copy's deck one cage longer to the west (x −4.43..3.57: its deck and south wall cloned from the copy's west cage, its west wall moved to the new end) beside the original's east cage, with no wall between the two at the street — the original's south wall there gone and the extension given none — so the original's under-deck, the extension and the copy's under-deck are one room; the original's west cage closed on the south by that wall, moved 7.5 m west (8 m left its end post half a metre short of the corner, and a body slipped between it and the extension's post), and opened on the north, its north wall gone: the room's ways in are that north side and, as before, the copy's open south side. The old joint, where the copy touches the original, is now the room's corner: the slot between the two posts there, kept as it was (the user's call), is filled by a `FILL` box, as the reading had filled it before. Collision read by the same recipe, and only the edited places taken from that reading — the catwalk's rectangle from 7.5 m up, the extension's and the west cage's below 6 m (`data_region.mjs`'s height band): read whole, it had re-cut the original's walls beside them (the 14 cm slot at their feet, as before); the rest of the map keeps its data. Bodies cross the catwalk both ways, go in by the north side and out by the copy's south, and pass neither corner of the room; the bots' grid routes all three.

**Then** (b1102h; user, of marked pictures: "Add floors at these two 2nd floor places. Seems like 1 fence area on one side and 2 fences area on the other side to me.", "bullets should be able to go through these gaps" of the room's two corners, and of the boxes over the green container: "These few cboxes should be as high as the container instead of being higher than container."): two decks at the second floor, 5.8 m, clones of the copy's deck — one cage west of the extension (model x −12.43..−4.43, z 40.38..48.38: a piece of it moved 16 m west) and two cages north of the copy's deck (x 3.57..19.57, z 32.37..40.37: two pieces moved 8 m north) — walked onto from the decks beside them, the street under them open. The slits at the room's two corners (the inner one at x −4.44 and the old joint at x 3.56, both at z 40.38) were filled by the reading, as it fills any gap narrower than a body, and stopped rounds too: the boxes between the posts there, 0.9 to 5.4 m up, are flagged `noProjectile` (`data_flag.mjs`), so a round flies down 89% and 93% of the lines the eye sees through them (52% and 0% before) and down 2 of the 556 lines one of the model's faces stops (a post's, or a cage's mesh); a body is still held, and a bot at a slit shoots through it as a player would. The boxes over the green container (model x 21.9..23.95, z 17.5..23.7), which rose with its sign to 7 m, are cut at its roof, 5.2 m, so a body walks the roof through the sign. The rest of the map keeps its data.

**The ramp tops** (b1102j; user 2026-11-03, of pictures with the beam at the top of each lower ramp circled either side of its hazard-striped middle: "currently the unit would be blocked by the red-circled spots at the top of the steep slopes. Make the unit walk-passable."): the reading had laid a 3 cm plate at h 5.75–5.78 over the deck-edge beam at the top of both 45° ramps from the street (model x −12.69..−12.44 on the original, x 19.56..19.81 on the copy), with a floor on it, across each ramp's width but its middle, 0.5–0.75 m over the ramp, so a body walking up beside the middle was held 0.6 m short of the top (x −12.98 and x 20.10, feet 4.73 m, where the user stood). The plates and their floors are gone (`data_region.mjs` with `none`), and every lane across both ramps now walks up onto the deck and down again, as the middle did; the upper ramps and the top platforms' were already open. 7,510 boxes and 3,645 floors.

**What the modelled map brought to the sim** (b1021b–z) — rules written for Town's glTF geometry, in force on every map:

**Broadphase everywhere** (b1021b–c, i–j) — the plain brain's perch search, every segment and point test against the map's boxes and floors (sight, projectiles, steering), the ground lookups (`groundHeightAt`, `surfaceHeightAtXZ`) and the body's own box tests (`unitOverlapsObstacle`, `resolveUnitObstacleCollisions`, the nav grid's floors at a point) read only the boxes whose cells the query touches. The broadphase hands back a superset and the exact test decides, so every answer is the same as the full scan's; a dense modelled map (7,000 boxes) costs what a hand-made one did.

**Bounded wall field** (b1021g) — the steering's boxes around a body push at most as hard as the nearest one would alone, so a wall cut into twenty boxes weighs what one wall weighs. The offline client's perimeter is its boundary boxes alone; the fixed cannon walls at 138 units are gone.

**The ground under a point** (b1021h) — the highest surface a body can step onto under the point, the street (0) only where no surface covers it: a floor below the street (a sunken yard, a garage ramp) is the ground there. The sim and the offline client's mirror.

**The void's edge** (b1026j, user 2026-10-29, "the unit got transported above to ground floor from B3 at this spot") — `voidUnder` in `physics.js`: the street (0) is the ground where no surface covers a point only while it is within a step of the body's feet, or below them; a body deeper than that with nothing under it stands over a void — the end of a cellar's floor where the model has nothing: District's bowling lanes end behind the pins at z −37.3, and the pit beyond, under the pinsetter deck, has no floor — and the old rule lifted it 26 m to the road. The tick (`tickMatch`, after the walls' push-out) holds a grounded body at the edge, its move into the void undone and its velocity dropped as at a wall; a body in the air over a void lands on the floor it left (`integrateVertical`, `floorY`), and from there walks back. The offline client mirrors both (`voidUnder`, `getGroundLevelY`, the hold after `resolveUnitObstacleCollisions`). Nothing changes where the street is within a step: every map before District stands its bodies there. Tests: `shared/test/void.test.js`.

**Step height 0.65 m** (b1021o) — `SURFACE_STEP_HEIGHT` moved to `constants.js` and raised from 1.6 to 2.6 units: a body walks onto any floor no more than 0.65 m higher, the modelled maps' tallest kerb, so a pavement's edge is purely visual. The nav grid's `MAX_STEP` and the bots' `BOT_CLIMB_MIN_RISE` are that constant plus the old 0.1 of slack; the offline client's mirror of `groundHeightAt` imports it. Boxes are untouched: a low wall, a crate or a car's side still stops a body at its old height (`topBuffer`), so only floors without a box beneath them — pavements, low decks, ramps — change. Known cost: on the older maps a surface between 1.6 and 2.6 units above its neighbour with no box under it is now stepped onto; none was found that mattered.

**The ladders** (b1105a, user 2026-11-04/05, "yeah i need a ladder") — `shared/src/sim/ladder.js`, one rule for the server's tick, the online prediction and the offline client: a ladder is a record of the arena (`arena.js` `ARENA_LADDERS`, hand-set rows as the spawns are — the climb line a body's width off the rungs, its facing, its grab width, the feet at the foot and at the lip, and the EXIT on the platform it serves); the collision data is not touched, the rule carries the body. **Space is the ladder key** (user: the jump button, its look unchanged): standing at the foot (within a metre in front of the line, half a metre beside its width, or pressed on the wall — b1105g; until then half a metre out and not against the wall, where a run at the ladder stops, so the press worked only a step back) or on the platform within a metre of the exit, the press mounts — no jump; a press while running at the foot from up to 2.5 m out is kept 600 ms and mounts the body as it arrives, no jump going off (b1105g, user: "very hard to trigger ... when I'm rush at the ladder"); on the ladder the press lets go and the body drops where it is (no boost, no arc); the press that mounted or let go is spent until the button is let go; a body passing or jumping through a ladder is never caught. A human steers by the stick along the ladder's facing — toward the wall climbs, away descends, anything else holds (sideways never lets go); at the top the body is carried over the lip onto the exit and released standing, at the foot it stands. The climb is the sprint's steady speed, 29.4 units/s (7.35 m/s; the user's choice), every leg, a pure function of the ticks on every host (offline on a 16 ms clock of its own, as the boost gauge); no gravity, no momentum, no stun (a hit does its damage, the stun is skipped, a running one is dropped at the mount), no boost drained or refilled, no shot, dodge or sprint; dying on the ladder lets go. The camera looks from the climber's back along the ladder — 19 units behind and 10 above the body since b1105g (the user's pick of four samples; 13 and 6.8 before), `CLIMB_CAM` in `main.js` — and the free pov comes back as it was; the climb motion is Vital_Retreat — each unit's own, in every file since the user's new files of 2026-11-05 (b1105d/e/f: twelve files replaced, the same skeletons, the full clip sets; Hoshino's is `01_Vital_Retreat`); a file without one would borrow Kurumi's through `client/public/models/climb_clip.json`, kept as the fallback; the clip turns the body about (it is a retreat), so the model is turned the other way on the ladder and the camera sees the climber's back (b1105c; `CLIMB_CLIP_TURNED` in `main.js`). Compound's one ladder (the hall's east wall, up through the hatch between the catwalks at 6.9 m, the exit on the north catwalk — the one that goes on) and District's ten (eight from the sidewalk up the towers' north and south faces to the parapet's top at 45.37 m, two from the bridge roof at 40.75 m; the exits on the roofs at 44.67 m behind the 0.70 m parapet, the short ones' on the inner ramps at 42.32 m) are live; the two under District's street are not. **Bots** (b1105b): a ladder is a link on the route grid (`navgrid.js` *THE LADDERS*, attached to every grid the hosts hand out, the grid files unchanged) between the node at its grab point and the node at its exit, both ways, costing the climb's length in cells — taken when it is the quicker way, left alone when the stairs are; the climb down costs the same, and a drop link off an edge (2.5) wins where one exists (user: "有邊可跳就跳下，沒有才爬下"). Only the searches that ASK climb: the plain brain's route and firing-spot searches (its travel — Pursue / Maze) and the command layer's orders; the hide-spot search (Defense, the Hide stance) and the Sudden Death brain never do. The route follower (`nbrain.js`, the Maze leg) and the order driver (`command.js` / the offline `applyMoveOrder`) walk the bot to the grab point (or the exit), set its goal (`botLadderGoal` — one tick's, renewed each tick they head there, so a dropped route leaves no goal behind; an order owns it over the brain) and `ladderBotStep` mounts the body in the zone; the ride is the rule's, the brain sleeps for it, and the bot wakes standing on the landing, where its waypoint is reached. A move order's **hold-cycle** (keep the finger down) offers, when the picked route climbs a ladder, the same spot by the way without one (when there is one — shown as the next pick; the route's ladder legs are drawn solid amber over the dashed preview); the choice rides the order (`noLadder`, online too), so its replans plan the same way. `shared/test/ladder.test.js`, `ladder_bots.test.js`. **The walls** (b1105j, user 2026-11-05: "It's a wall of the train's passenger home platform ... let unit be able to climb back, or they'll stuck forever"; "Make these climbable as well"; the five points confirmed) — a record with `span: true` is a whole wall face climbed at ANY point along it: District's platform wall over the track bed (W1, 202 m: a body that falls onto the tracks runs at the wall and presses jump anywhere along it) and the four retaining walls of the terraces under the street (W2–W5, 6–7 m each, two tiers). The row is the same row with the width the wall's whole run; the mount keeps where along the wall the body took it (`ladderS`, clamped half a metre inside the ends), the column and the exit are moved along by it, so the body climbs straight up where it stood and steps off straight behind. UP ONLY: a wall has no top zone — a press on the platform's edge or a terrace is a jump as ever, so no one is caught by a 200 m wall top; on the wall the stick away from it climbs back down. Nothing is drawn on a wall (user: the face stays). The grid links a wall every 8 m along it, up only; the way down is the drop off the edge. A ladder's `ladderS` is 0 and every form is the ladder's own, bit for bit.

**Nav grid ground rule** (b1021p) — `candidateFloors` in `navgrid.js` lists the street (0) only where no surface lies within `SURFACE_STEP_HEIGHT` of it, as `groundHeightAt` does; before, 0 was always a candidate, and at a ramp's foot dipping under the street the grid's level (0) and the sim's ground (the ramp) disagreed by more than the edge test's 0.05, so the cell had no edges and the bots could not enter the region beyond (Town's west terrace). A floor over a passage keeps the street beneath it as a level (a slab above the step is no floor for a body on the street).

**Plane surfaces** (b1021p) — a surface of type `plane` (`y0`, `gx`, `gz`: the height at the rectangle's (minX, minZ) corner and the rise per unit along x and z) is a sloped floor in any direction; `materializeSurface` gives it its `heightAt`, and `sightHitsSurface` treats it as a ramp (linear along the ray). The glTF generator writes every slope and kerb as a plane, so a diagonal ramp is one surface instead of a staircase of decks. The axis `ramp` stays for the hand-made maps.

**Box lid follows the step** (b1021q) — `boxHoldsAt(o, y)` in `physics.js` is the one vertical window for a box (the movement's push-out, `unitOverlapsObstacle`, `walkSegmentBlocked` and the bots' steering all use it). A box whose top is a floor — a surface lies on it, marked once per arena as `floorTop` in `arena.js` — no longer holds a body whose feet are within `SURFACE_STEP_HEIGHT` of that top: the ground rule will step the body onto the floor, so the box lets it come. Before, `topBuffer 0.8` held the body until its feet were within 1.65 units of the top, so a half-metre step onto a deck's body was a wall. Walls, fences and belts (no floor on top) hold as before.

**One jump threshold** (b1021r) — `JUMP_RISE_MIN` (`constants.js`, `SURFACE_STEP_HEIGHT + 0.1`) is the rise above which a body needs a jump; the nav grid's walkable step and jump links, the path smoother and both bot brains read it. They used to carry the old step as a literal 1.7 in five places, so after the step grew to 2.6 the bots jumped at kerbs the body walks.

**Landing plant** (2026-10-22, user: "make the containers standable") — a body that lands on a thing's lid (the generator marks a crate's, a container's, a roof's top `lid`) at another level than the floor it left — up onto it, or down onto it from a taller thing — is planted there: its momentum is dropped and the stick it still holds is ignored until it is let go or `LAND_PLANT_MS` (600 ms) pass; `LAND_PLANT_LEVEL` (0.1 m) is how much the level must change. A sprint-jump from Warehouse's 3.2 m crate onto a 2.5 m container's 2.4 m roof crossed it in a third of a second and fell off the far side while the player still held the stick. A landing on the street or a plateau, a hop along the same roof, a step down to the street plant nothing. The rule lives in the shared sim (`integrateVertical` raises the flag and drops the momentum, `applyInput` holds the body) and is mirrored in the client's `updateTransforms` / `updatePlayer` for the local player; `physics.js groundSurfaceAt` names the surface a body stands on. Tests: `shared/test/plant.test.js`.

**Walk rule** (b1021s) — `walkFloorTo` in `physics.js` walks a segment with the step rule every unit, as movement does every tick, and returns the floor the body arrives on. The nav grid's walk edges ask it (both ways must arrive on each other's floor), the path builders flag a node reached by a jump link (`jump`), and the bots (`nbrain.js`, `sdbrain.js`) vault at a flagged waypoint only. Before, edges and vaults went by the floor difference between two nodes four units apart (`JUMP_RISE_MIN`), so a kerb's 45-degree ramp or a flight of steps — which the body walks — was a jump link, and the bot hopped and hovered over every such edge.

**The ways in and the headroom** (b1105i, 2026-11-05; user, District: "BOT does not know the area over this area, let them know") — two rules of the grid's builder (`navgrid.js`), every nav file rebuilt. THE WAYS IN: the sealed-void prune dropped every covered walk component that touched no node with nothing overhead — the space inside a solid platform, rightly, but with it every underground floor a body reaches only by a hop or a ladder: District's station hall (its floor slabs 0.65 m over the hall floor, a kerb the step rule does not climb), the pit the two ladders climb out of (25 by 24 m, not one node), the cellars under the user's spot. The links are built before the prune now, and a covered component that a TWO-WAY jump link or a ladder joins to an open one is open too, on until nothing changes; a drop alone opens nothing (a pit a body falls into and cannot leave is no place for a route). The ladders are part of the data signature (`navDataSignature(obstacles, surfaces, ladders)`): a row added is a file to regenerate, and the loader refuses the old one. THE HEADROOM: a level whose next floor lies less than a body's height over it — GROUND_BASE_Y + 2 units, 1.1 m, the height physics.js boxHoldsAt gives a body — is no level. A big slab carries no box (a block of 200 m² or more is its top alone), so the space inside one "fits" a body, and the ways-in reached 6,600 such phantom levels on District and 1,100 on Town before the rule (the first rebuild, measured: the hall floor inside its own slabs, 0.65 m under their tops). Read on the shipped grids: District 78,772 → 91,979 nodes (13,338 added, 131 gone; jump links 303 → 2,758), Town 15,864 → 16,107 (+579, −336: a deck 1.1 m over a floor was walked INSIDE before and on top now), Neon +529/−8, the five others a few each way. `shared/test/navgrid_prune.test.js` is the yard that holds the four cases: the shelf a body hops onto stays, the shelf it can only fall onto goes, the ladder keeps it, the sheet 0.9 m up drops the level under it.

**Levels, slopes and drops** (b1026f, user 2026-10-29, "pathfinder seems to have issue planning the route over slopes and levels; I also want the bot to know how to jump off to get to the enemy faster") — three changes to the grid's builder (`navgrid.js`), all maps' nav files rebuilt (NAV_DATA_VERSION 2).
